# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the project uses [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/). Until 1.0, minor versions may break the API.

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed
- **Pasting HTML (#309):** On macOS and Windows, paste providers get the clipboard's `text/html` (Monaco's clipboard `DataTransfer`), so "Paste As..." offers the built-in HTML provider's "Insert HTML" after copying from a browser or another editor, as in Monaco. GPUI's clipboard (0.3.8, and zed main) reads no HTML, so it's read from the system clipboard (`public.html` from `NSPasteboard`, the `HTML Format` clipboard format on Windows, from `StartHTML` to `EndHTML` as browsers give it), only when its text is the text GPUI read. Linux and the browser still have no `text/html`. Text dragged in from other apps still isn't delivered, as GPUI's window accepts dropped files only.
- **Copy with syntax highlighting (#231):** Ported Monaco's `copyWithSyntaxHighlighting` (new option `copy_with_syntax_highlighting`, default on). Copying a selection under 64K UTF-16 units puts HTML beside the text, built as Monaco's `ViewModel.getRichTextToCopy` and `tokenizeLineToHTML` build it: a `<div>` in the theme's foreground and background and the editor's font family, weight, size and line height, with a `<div>` of colored `<span>`s per line (`<br>` for an empty one), tabs as spaces to the next tab stop, `<`, `>` and `&` escaped, and every other space of a run as `&#160;` on Windows. An empty selection copies its line's HTML when `empty_selection_clipboard` is on; several selections and plain text models copy no HTML. GPUI's clipboard (0.3.8, and zed main) has no HTML, so the editor adds it to the system clipboard itself right after GPUI writes the text: `NSPasteboard`'s `public.html` on macOS and the `HTML Format` (CF_HTML) clipboard format on Windows, only when the clipboard's text is the copied text. Linux and the browser keep plain text copies. Monaco puts the default font list's double quotes inside the `style` attribute; here they're single quotes, so the HTML parses.
- **LSP will save (#561):** `LspClient::will_save` sends `textDocument/willSave` and `LspClient::will_save_wait_until` sends `textDocument/willSaveWaitUntil`, when the server asks for them, and the latter applies the server's edits to the model as one undo step before its task resolves. As with VS Code's save participants, a server that fails or doesn't answer within `WILL_SAVE_WAIT_UNTIL_TIMEOUT` (1.5s) doesn't hold up the save, and edits for a version the document has moved on from aren't applied. The client advertises `synchronization.willSave` and `willSaveWaitUntil`.
- **LSP bridge: type hierarchy (#566):** `LspClient::prepare_type_hierarchy(&model, position, cx)`, `supertypes` and `subtypes` send `textDocument/prepareTypeHierarchy`, `typeHierarchy/supertypes` and `typeHierarchy/subtypes` and return `TypeHierarchyItem`s with the editor's `SymbolKind`, `PointUtf16` ranges and normalized uris. The server's items go back to it unchanged. `lsp-types` 0.97 has no `typeHierarchyProvider` capability and reads an item's `tags` as one tag, so these requests are sent as JSON, and the new `LspClient::raw_capabilities()` keeps the server's capabilities as it sent them. The client now advertises `textDocument.typeHierarchy`. gpui-monaco has no type hierarchy view (Monaco has none either), so hosts build that UI on this API.
- **LSP bridge: call hierarchy (#565):** `LspClient::prepare_call_hierarchy(&model, position, cx)`, `incoming_calls` and `outgoing_calls` send `textDocument/prepareCallHierarchy`, `callHierarchy/incomingCalls` and `callHierarchy/outgoingCalls` and return `CallHierarchyItem`s, `IncomingCall`s and `OutgoingCall`s with the editor's `SymbolKind`, `PointUtf16` ranges and normalized uris. The server's items go back to it unchanged, `data` included. The client now advertises `textDocument.callHierarchy`. gpui-monaco has no call hierarchy view (Monaco has none either), so hosts build that UI on this API.
- **LSP message request actions (#558):** `window/showMessageRequest` is now `LspClientEvent::ShowMessageRequest { kind, message, actions, respond }`, and the server gets the action the host answers with through `MessageResponder::respond`, extra properties included, or `null` if the prompt is dismissed or every clone of the responder is dropped unanswered, as `vscode-languageclient` answers with the item the user picked. Before, the request was answered `null` at once and emitted as `ShowMessage`, which now only has `window/showMessage`. The client advertises `window.showMessage.messageActionItem.additionalPropertiesSupport`.
- **LSP settings changes (#560):** `LspClient::set_settings` replaces the settings served through `workspace/configuration` and sends `workspace/didChangeConfiguration` with `settings: null`, as `vscode-languageclient` does for pull-model servers, so a host can change a server's settings without restarting it. `LspClient::settings` returns them. The client advertises `workspace.didChangeConfiguration`.
- **LSP bridge: workspace symbols (#564):** `LspClient::workspace_symbols(query, cx)` sends `workspace/symbol` and returns `WorkspaceSymbol`s with the editor's `SymbolKind`, `PointUtf16` ranges and normalized uris, from flat `SymbolInformation` or `WorkspaceSymbol` results. `resolve_workspace_symbol` sends `workspaceSymbol/resolve` when the server supports it, as VS Code does before opening a picked symbol whose range the server left out. The client now advertises `workspace.symbol` with `resolveSupport`. gpui-monaco has no workspace symbol picker (Monaco has none either), so hosts build that UI on this API.
- **Minimap glyphs above scale 2 (#307):** As in Monaco (`MinimapCharRendererFactory.createFromSampleData`), minimap scales above 2 in device pixels (scale 2 or 3 on a Retina display) draw their characters from the editor font, sampled bold at 16px into 10×16 cells as Monaco's `createSampleData` does, instead of enlarging the scale-2 glyphs. GPUI can't rasterize a font into a buffer, so the new default `minimap-font` feature finds `font_family` among the system's fonts (`fontdb`) and rasterizes it with `swash`, the rasterizer GPUI uses on Linux. That runs once per family on the background executor (about 45ms the first time, mostly listing the system fonts; 8ms for another family), shared by every editor; until it lands, and for fonts registered only with GPUI or in the browser, the minimap enlarges the scale-2 glyphs and draws again when the samples are in. A family without a bold face is made bold, as browsers do. Scales 1 and 2 still use the prebaked glyphs.
- **Context menu alternative actions (#541):** As in Monaco's menus (`IAction.alt`, `getActionForAltMode`), a context menu item can have an alternative action. `EditorActionDescriptor::context_menu_alt` names it for a host action (Monaco's menu item `alt`): another host action or a built-in one, by id. While Alt is held, or Shift on Windows and Linux, the item shows the alternative's label and keybinding (`updateAlternativeAction`, on each `ModifierKeyEmitter` change), and a click runs it (`getActionToRun`). Alt+Enter runs it from the keyboard (`isTriggerKeyEvent`), and Shift+Enter on Windows and Linux; so do Alt+Space on macOS and Linux and Shift+Space on Linux, where Space is a trigger key of Monaco's menu (it isn't on Windows, so Space no longer runs a menu item there), through the new `ContextMenuSelectAlternative` action; on an item without an alternative they do nothing. Releasing the modifier shows the item again. A modifier held when the menu opens counts, as Monaco's `ContextMenuService` now resets the key status when the menu hides rather than when it shows. An alternative whose precondition doesn't hold is left out, since `getActionForAltMode` only picks an enabled one. The menu's width counts the alternatives, so it doesn't resize while Alt is held. Monaco also re-registers mnemonics, which the editor's menu doesn't have.
- **Bracket highlights update 50ms after the last change (#548):** As in Monaco's `BracketMatchingController` (`_updateBracketsSoon`, a 50ms `RunOnceScheduler`), the bracket highlights are found again 50ms after the last cursor move, edit, focus change, model or language configuration change or new parse, instead of at once on each. Typing faster than that moves the highlights with the text and doesn't look for the pairs on every keystroke. A cursor's pair is reused while the text doesn't change (`_lastBracketsData`), a `match_brackets` change clears the highlights at once, and a theme change recolors them where they are.
- **Multi diff editor visible items (#539):** Ported Monaco's `visibleDiffItems` (`MultiDiffEditorWidget`, microsoft/vscode#341872). The new `MultiDiffEditor::visible_items()` gives the files in view in document order, the one at the top of the viewport first: the files whose part of the list intersects the viewport (Monaco's `layout.items[i].visibility === 'visible'`), collapsed and binary files included. The new `MultiDiffEditorEvent::VisibleItemsChanged` fires once each time that list changes, as Monaco derives it with `arrayEqualsC`: when the list scrolls, is resized or its files change height, and when `set_documents` changes the files in view or their indices, but not for a scroll that keeps the same files in view. The list comes from the layout the editor draws and allocates nothing while it's unchanged. Monaco's new public `viewModel` is the editor's documents, by index, as now documented. A resize now draws the list again at the new size; before, it stayed laid out for the previous size until the next redraw, so files that came into view didn't show. Monaco's "exposes the host view model and the diff items visible in the viewport" test is ported.
- **Marker limits (#536):** As in Monaco (`MarkerDecorations.MAX_MARKER_DECORATIONS_LIMIT`), the first 5000 markers of a model's uri are decorated instead of 500 (`MAX_DECORATED_MARKERS` is now 5000), and a model tells when it doesn't show them all. Ported from `markerDecorationsService.test.ts` and `markerService.test.ts`.
  - **Limit state:** the new `TextModel::marker_limit()` and `marker_limits(cx, uri)` (Monaco `getMarkerLimits`) return a `MarkerLimit` (`IMarkerLimit`). Its `is_markers_truncated` is `Some(5000)` when the uri has more markers than that (`_updateDecorations` reads one past the limit), and its `is_markers_source_truncated` says an owner sent only part of its markers. The new `on_did_change_marker_limit(cx, …)` (`onDidChangeMarkerLimit`) reports a model after the update that changed either flag, once per model, and markers that didn't change keep their decorations.
  - **Truncated sources:** `set_markers_for_uri_with_options` and `TextModel::set_markers_with_options` take a `MarkerChangeOptions { is_truncated }` (Monaco `changeOne`'s `IMarkerChangeOptions`), and `is_source_truncated(cx, uri)` (`isSourceTruncated`) holds while any owner's markers of the uri are truncated. Setting an owner's markers again without it, or removing them (`remove_all_markers`, Monaco `changeAll(owner, [])`), clears the owner's flag. `set_markers` and `set_markers_for_uri` set markers as whole; the LSP bridge keeps using them, as LSP diagnostics have no such flag.
  - **Cost of many markers:** decorations are looked up by id in an index once a model has more than 32 (built when first needed, kept while none are added, removed or reordered), and replacing many removes them through a hash set, so decorating thousands of markers is no longer quadratic. The `editorSelectionHasDiagnostics` context key, computed on every frame, checks the markers' decorations in place instead of copying and sorting every marker. With 5000 markers in a model: replacing them all takes 15ms (3ms for 500 before), and the editor's context keys 35µs a frame, down from 230–290µs with 500 (release build, Apple M5 Pro).
- **Auto-closing pairs' `beforeText` (#534):** Ported Monaco's `beforeText` condition on auto-closing pairs (`IAutoClosingPairConditional.beforeText`, microsoft/vscode#337709). A pair with one auto-closes only if the line's text before the cursor, including the just-typed opening text, matches it (`StandardAutoClosingPairConditional.shouldAutoCloseBefore`); if it doesn't, `_findAutoClosingPairOpen` goes on to a shorter pair the typed character completes, when typing and when a composition ends. So with `begin`/`end` limited to `(?:^|\s)begin$` and `n`/`]`, typing `n` after `prefixbegi` gives `prefixbegin]`. `AutoClosingPair` gains `before_text: Option<Pattern>`, read from `language-configuration.json` as a string or `{ "pattern", "flags" }` like the other patterns and listed by `LanguageConfiguration::patterns`; a pair whose `beforeText` doesn't compile is left out, as VS Code's `_extractValidAutoClosingPairs` does. The new field breaks `AutoClosingPair` struct literals; it now derives `Default`, so they can end with `..Default::default()`. `Pattern` implements `PartialEq` and `Eq`, comparing source and flags.
- **Pasting files copied in a file manager (#537):** As in Monaco's `ClipboardEventUtils.getTextData` since microsoft/vscode#342695, a paste of a clipboard with copied files but no text or editor metadata (a copy in Windows' File Explorer) has the files' paths, one per line, as its text: `CodeEditorEvent::WillPaste` reports it, and the editor's own paste (with `pasteAs` off, or when no paste provider applies) inserts it. Before, the paths ran together with no separator, from GPUI's `ClipboardItem::text`. Paste providers no longer get those run-together paths as `text/plain`; they get the files and `text/uri-list`, so the built-in uri-list provider inserts the paths separated by spaces, as Monaco's `PathProvider` does. A clipboard with text as well (macOS' Finder adds the files' names) still pastes its text.
- **Monospace fonts with ligatures (#535):** As in Monaco since microsoft/vscode#341778 (`FontMeasurementsImpl._actualReadFontInfo`), `is_monospace` is measured with ligatures on as well: `fi`, `<=`, `=>` and `===` are measured too, and each probe must be as wide as its characters' cells (`n`'s width) within 0.005px, the tolerance now for every probe (it was 0.001). A font whose ligatures keep the width, like most coding fonts, is monospace with ligatures on, so `fullwidth_character_width: TwoCells` applies, and `font_info().is_monospace` reports it. Before, ligatures on meant not monospace.
- **Linked editing during rapid edits (#538):** As in Monaco since microsoft/vscode#343009, an edit inside the linked editing reference range records its model version as the last request's (`_currentRequestModelVersion`), so a range update that comes before the mirror edit, with the cursor still in the reference range, keeps the ranges instead of asking the providers again. Ported Monaco's quick insert and delete tests with a range update before synchronization, and its test that edits outside the reference range refresh the ranges. The port's mirror edit runs before any scheduled update, so it never lost synchronization as Monaco did; now it also doesn't ask the providers again in that case.
- **Passwords in uris (#540):** `normalize_uri` (and so model, marker and LSP uri comparisons) splits a uri's user information at its first colon and encodes colons in the password, as Monaco's `URI.toString()` now does (microsoft/vscode#334259): `http://foo:pa:ss@localhost/far` is `http://foo:pa%3Ass@localhost/far`, where it was `http://foo%3Apa:ss@localhost/far`.
- **Bracket highlighting after each parse (#378):** Bracket matching now keeps strings and comments in blocks of 16 rows instead of 64. A small or medium file reparses after every keystroke, the parse drops the block the edit is in, and the next bracket highlight queries that block again, so a quarter of the rows is queried. Scans over many blocks (Page Down, a far enclosing pair) cost the same, and results are unchanged. Over 600 keystrokes, main-thread samples in bracket highlighting went from 313 to 181 on a 277-line Rust file (185 to 66 in the strings-and-comments query) and from 217 to 164 on 100k lines; Page Down on 100k lines is unchanged (playground perf mode, Apple M5 Pro).
- **Bracket matching reads rows in sequence (#378):** The bracket searches (highlighting the pair at the cursor, jump to bracket, auto-closing and selection ranges) now read the rows they visit one after another from the rope's chunks, continuing from where the previous row ended, upward or downward. Before, each row took its own rope seek. A long row is still read only within the search window. Results are unchanged. A `matching_bracket` call at 2,000 spread-out positions takes 29µs instead of 49µs on a 100k-line file, 30µs instead of 61µs on 300k lines, and 21µs instead of 26µs on 277 lines (release build, best of 7). While typing with the playground demos on, bracket highlighting takes 30–60% fewer main-thread samples (277 lines 67 → 48, 100k lines 45 → 30, 300k lines 32 → 13). Keystroke-to-paint medians are unchanged within noise.
- **Paging through huge files (#378):** As in Monaco (`BracketPairsTextModelPart.canBuildAST`), a document longer than 5,000,000 UTF-16 units gets no bracket pairs: no bracket pair colors and no bracket pair guides, and no whole-document bracket pass. Before, such files got bracket colors, and GPUI shapes a line in a separate run at each color change, so every bracket split its line (about 4× the runs per line on a 1M-line Rust file) and Page Down shaped about twice as long. Page Down to paint on a 1M-line (35MB) Rust file without the demo providers: median 3.37ms to 2.69ms, p95 5.65ms to 4.26ms; on 300k lines (10MB) 3.84ms to 2.67ms (playground perf mode, Apple M5 Pro). Files at or under the limit draw the same as before (pixel-identical screenshots of a 100k-line file); in longer ones, only the bracket colors differ.
- **Typing on long lines (#378):** typing in a 2MB single-line minified file takes a median 2.15ms from keystroke to paint, down from 3.6ms, and its p95 is 2.8ms, down from 12.5ms (playground perf mode, Apple M5 Pro). Drawing is unchanged.
  - **Shaping chunks:** a long line's shaping chunks are now cut where a rolling hash of the text picks, not a fixed distance after the previous cut, so a keystroke reshapes only the chunk it's in (and the one where the line stops rendering) instead of often every chunk after it. Chunks are a few hundred bytes.
  - **Old syntax trees:** the highlight spans of a replaced parse are dropped on the background executor; they could hold the last reference to the old tree, and freeing a big file's tree took several milliseconds of the frame after each reparse.
  - **Reading the line:** each frame reads a shown row only as far as its segments go (a long line stops rendering at 10 000 columns), and so does rendered whitespace. `TextModel::word_at_position`, which the suggest widget calls on every keystroke, reads only Monaco `getWordAtText`'s window on a line longer than 1000 units: 500 units each side of the 1-based column, counted from the start of the language run, as Monaco does. A longer word is cut to that window, as in Monaco. The suggest widget's indentation check and the leading line content it passes to providers read only up to the cursor.
- **Bracket highlighting while typing (#378):** A finished parse no longer throws away the strings and comments bracket matching keeps for each 64-row block. Blocks the parse left alone carry over, moved by the edits before them: no edit touched them, and the parse didn't change the tokens on their rows. Small and medium files reparse after every keystroke, so each parse used to make the bracket highlight query every block it scanned again. That recompute was most of the editor's own main-thread work per keystroke. Blocks shifted by an inserted or removed line break are kept too until the parse catches up, and a bracket's offset on an ASCII row is read from the block's row starts instead of a rope seek. Results are unchanged. Over 600 keystrokes, main-thread samples in bracket highlighting went from 217 to 72 on a 277-line Rust file and from 153 to 89 on 100k lines. Keystroke to paint on 100k lines went from 2.09ms to 2.01ms (median); on 277 lines, the recompute runs after the keystroke's frame, so that frame is unchanged (playground perf mode, Apple M5 Pro).
- **Scrolling with inlay hints (#378):** New inlay hints for the rows scrolled to replace only those rows' hints, as Monaco's `_updateHintsDecorators` does. Before, every hint shown so far was moved, re-sorted and re-indexed. Each hint now keeps a slot with its decorations, and while no edit has moved them, the rows' hints are found by binary search and spliced out. The playground's demo provider finds calls by looking up the identifier before each `(`, not by searching every row for every function's name. Paging through a 100k-line Rust file with the demo providers: main-thread busy samples 1034 → 502 (the demo provider 536 → 2, applying the hints 98 → 13). Keystroke to paint on that file is 3.19 → 2.77ms. Page Down to paint, which doesn't include the hints arriving 25ms later, is unchanged. The hints shown are pixel-identical.
- **Per-frame layout (#378):** Drawing a frame no longer goes over every view zone, re-queries highlights it already has or seeks each shown row several times. Keystroke to paint on a 100k-line Rust file with the demo code lenses (about 5,400 view zones): median 2.85ms to 2.06ms (p95 3.48ms to 2.73ms); Page Down on it 5.38ms to 4.62ms; Page Down on 277 lines 4.89ms to 3.69ms; 1M lines typing 1.50ms to 1.29ms (playground perf mode, Apple M5 Pro). Layout and paint are unchanged (before and after screenshots are pixel-identical outside the scrollbar's fade).
  - **View zones:** the shown zones, in layout order, and their layout are kept while the zones, their rows and their sizes stay the same, so scrolling and redraws reuse them, and only the zones on screen are visited to render them. After an edit, each zone's row moves by the lines the edits before it added or removed, and only zones in edited text are looked up again, instead of every zone's row being found in the text (with nothing folded or wrapped, a row is its view line).
  - **Highlights:** the syntax spans read for the visible lines are kept for that parse, so frames showing the same lines of the same parse (a keystroke drawn before its reparse finishes, a blink, scrolling at the end of the file) don't query the tree again.
  - **Rows:** each shown row's text and byte range are read once per frame and shared by the highlight, bracket and line layout passes. Rendered whitespace in `selection` mode skips rows without a selection.
- **Typing in large files (#378):** `ViewLines` keeps its per-row data (projections and view-line counts) in shared chunks of about 1,000 rows, so an edit no longer copies and later frees the whole document's rows when the minimap's tiles still hold the previous mapping (on a 1M-line file that was about a third of each keystroke's main-thread time). Adding or removing lines updates only the chunks around the edit instead of recounting every row, and a new mapping shares one chunk for all its unwrapped rows. Keystroke to paint on a 1M-line file: median 2.66ms to 2.17ms, p95 4.08ms to 2.47ms; on 300k lines p95 6.38ms to 5.61ms; `ViewLines::new` on 100k lines 304µs to 6µs.
- **Bracket matching on large files (#378):** Typing and moving the cursor no longer re-query strings and comments for every bracket highlight: the model keeps each 64-row block's strings and comments until the next parse, so cursor moves and keystrokes between parses reuse them, with the same results as querying them again. Each scanned row is read with one seek instead of building a rope slice, and finding the rows too long to tokenize takes one pass over the text instead of three seeks per row. Keystroke to paint on a 100k-line Rust file went from 4.21ms to 3.16ms (median), 300k lines from 2.27ms to 2.09ms, and 277 lines from 2.07ms to 1.65ms. Page Down on 100k lines went from 4.02ms to 3.68ms.
- **Opening a file (#378):** Opening a file in a running app is fast again: 100k lines 62 → 5ms, 300k lines 31 → 11ms, 1M lines 86 → 36ms, a 2MB minified line 14 → 4ms and 277 lines 1.8 → 1.2ms (playground perf mode, Apple M5 Pro). Typing and scrolling are unchanged or faster.
  - **Code lens zones:** going to a new model removes the lenses' zones in one pass, with one `ViewZonesChanged`, as Monaco's single `changeViewZones` does, instead of one at a time (quadratic with thousands of lenses).
  - **Indentation guessing:** `guess_indentation` streams its 10 000 lines from the rope instead of copying and UTF-16 encoding each one. It guesses exactly what it did before.
  - **Rope:** ASCII chunks are measured a line at a time. `point_to_offset`, `point_to_point_utf16` and line lengths skip the char-by-char walk. Line ending detection and the unusual line terminator check use byte searches.
  - **Minimap:** the first tile reads from the rope only what fits in the minimap's width, not whole lines.
  - **Playground providers:** the demo providers scan the document with borrowed lines. Code lenses are found off the main thread, and the inlay hints' document index is cached per model and version.
- **Typing on long lines (#378):** typing in a 2MB single-line minified file takes a median 3.8ms from keystroke to paint, down from 14.3ms. Before, each keystroke read the whole line as a string or as UTF-16 several times. The suggest preview and ghost text did, for the replaced range and the line's indentation. The minimap did, for its selection and decoration offsets, and indent guides did, for the leading whitespace. Now `Rope::line_units_utf16` reads only the columns needed, and the minimap reads only up to its width. Bracket matching skips the position lookup when there are no strings or comments to skip, and skips bracket comparisons whose first character can't match. Results are unchanged.
- **Language and theme definitions (#489):** ported JavaScript's regex classes for language configurations, Monaco's anchored `firstLine` and its black default token color (audit 5 §F.5–§F.7).
  - **JavaScript regex classes:** `wordPattern`, `indentationRules`, `onEnterRules`, `folding.markers` and other `Pattern`s go through the Monarch translator, so `\w` and `\d` are ASCII, `\s` is JavaScript's whitespace set and `.` stops at `\r`, U+2028 and U+2029 (unless the `s` flag is set): `wordPattern: "\\w+"` finds `na` in `naïve`, as in Monaco. A hyphen next to a class escape (`[\w-.]`) stays literal in Monarch rules too.
  - **`firstLine`:** a language's `with_first_line` regex is anchored at the line start (a `^` is added when missing) and read as a JavaScript regex, so `<\?xml` no longer guesses XML for `foo <?xml`.
  - **Default token color:** `Theme::define` without `inherit` or `editor.foreground` colors unmatched tokens `#000000`, as Monaco's `resolveParsedTokenThemeRules` does, instead of the editor foreground.
- **Smart select and docs (#493):** As in Monaco, Expand Selection adds its fallback ranges (the grammar's syntax nodes, or brackets without one) only when no selection range provider is registered, so a registered provider that answers nothing leaves just its ranges and words. Growing through syntax nodes where Monaco uses brackets is now documented in the book's differences (audit 5 §D.7). The differences also say edits and decorations take 0-based `Range<PointUtf16>`, with byte-offset variants on `TextModel` (§G.10), and the LSP crate docs no longer list only five features (§G.11).
- **Mouse and drag and drop (#491):** ported Monaco's middle-click targets, its drop-into check for read-only editors, the drag-and-drop pointer, the copy modifier reset on blur, the primary selection test with `contextmenu` off, and the fixed `dnd-target` colors (audit 5 §H.3–§H.8).
  - **Middle clicks:** like the left button, a middle click takes focus and starts the column selection only on the text, or on the line numbers with `select_on_line_numbers` (Monaco `MouseHandler._onMouseDown`); on the minimap, scrollbars, glyph margin and line decorations it leaves the focus and cursors alone. With `scroll_on_middle_click`, a middle click on the text takes focus before scrolling, so a key press ends the scrolling (`MiddleScrollController`).
  - **Read-only drops:** dragged files show no drop caret over a read-only editor (`isDropIntoEnabled` is `!readOnly && dropIntoEditor.enabled`).
  - **Drag pointer:** dragging the selection shows the arrow (or the copy pointer with Alt, Ctrl on Windows and Linux) from the first drag step, over the dragged selection too, not the I-beam until the mouse leaves it (`_onEditorMouseDrag`).
  - **Copy modifier after blur:** the editor losing focus forgets the copy modifier, whose key-up may go to another window, so a later drag without it moves the text instead of copying it (`onEditorBlur`).
  - **Right-click without the context menu:** with `context_menu` off, a right-click inside a secondary selection leaves one cursor at the click; only the primary selection keeps the cursors (Monaco tests `getSelection` there, and every selection only when the menu shows).
  - **Drop line color:** the drop line is black in light and high contrast light themes, `#AEAFAD` in dark ones and white in high contrast dark ones (`dnd.css`), not `editorCursor.foreground`. `Theme::dnd_target_color` returns it.
- **Accessibility (#492):** Ported Monaco's `domReadOnly` and the suggest list's roles (audit 5 §H.9, §H.10). A read-only editor is no longer reported to screen readers as read-only unless the new `EditorOptions::dom_read_only` is on as well, as Monaco's textarea gets `readonly` only with `domReadOnly && readOnly`. The suggest list is a "Suggest" listbox of options (list items on Windows) named by Monaco's `getAriaLabel`, the focused one `aria-selected`; once its item resolves, it is the editor's active descendant and the editor's `aria-autocomplete` is "list", so screen readers read it politely as the selection instead of an assertive alert on every arrow key. "Loading..." and "No suggestions." are still read as `status`es.
- **Model uris in the API (#490):** Ported Monaco's `model.uri` where the API names a model: its normalized uri, `inmemory://model/N` for a model created without one (audit 5 §G.6–§G.8).
  - **Go to locations commands:** `editor.action.goToLocations`, `peekLocations` and `showReferences` find an editor showing a model without a uri by its `inmemory://model/N`, and compare the command's uri, the locations' and the model's normalized (Monaco compares `URI.toString()`s), so `file:///a%20b.rs` reaches the editor of `file:///a b.rs`. Navigation results are sorted and deduplicated by normalized uri, a location without one taking the queried model's, and F12 through them keeps going while the cursor is in one of them. The peek view's file list and title describe a file by its folder as Monaco's label service does (`getUriLabel(dirname(uri))`): a `file` uri's fs path (with a UNC path's server), any other uri's path, so a model without a uri is `1` in `/`.
  - **`ModelChange`:** `old_uri` and `new_uri` are the models' `resource()`: switching between two models without a uri reports `inmemory://model/1 → inmemory://model/2` rather than `None → None`, and `None` stands only for no model (`set_model(None)`).
  - **`ProviderContext`:** providers name the model by `resource` (Monaco's `model.uri`), which the built-in inline suggestions now compare to reuse results, so two models without a uri no longer share them.
- **Font options (#486):** Ported Monaco's `allowVariableFonts` and `allowVariableFontsInAccessibilityMode` (`effectiveAllowVariableFonts`): when off, decorations that change the font are left out of the text, sticky lines, gutter, minimap, overview ruler, hovers and the editor's decoration getters, and their font changes aren't forwarded; the diff editor's sides turn both off. Two-cell full-width characters now need Monaco's `isMonospace` (ligatures off, and `n`, a space, the digits, `→`, `·` and `|/-_ilm%` in regular, italic and bold equally wide), which `font_info()` reports too, with Monaco's other `FontInfo` fields (`maxDigitWidth`, `typicalFullwidthCharacterWidth`, `middotWidth`, `wsmiddotWidth`, `canUseHalfwidthRightwardsArrow`, `pixelRatio`, `fontFeatureSettings`); the font is measured once per font instead of every frame. `computed_options().font_size` is the zoomed size without clamping it to 6–100px again, and `FontLigatures::Settings` validates `""` and `"false"` to `Off` and `"true"` to `On` (audit 5 §B.8, §F.8–§F.10).
- **Go to definition (#487):** Ported Monaco's Cmd/Ctrl+hover link asked for per word: the providers are asked as soon as the mouse reaches a word (no 50ms wait) and not again while it stays on that word, found or not, so the link and its hover no longer blink as the mouse crosses the middle of the word's last character. The go-to-definition target highlight (and the multi diff editor's reveal highlight) has a dotted border in high contrast themes, like the word highlights (audit 5 §D.4–§D.5).
- **Multi diff file actions and list-wide options (#488):** File header actions render their icon's `$(name)` codicons (`"$(fold)"` no longer shows literally), and take Monaco's menu item `when` and `toggled` (`MultiDiffFileAction::with_when`, `with_toggled`): each file's header shows only the actions whose `when` holds for it, and a toggled action shows highlighted (`actionBar.toggledBackground`) with its toggled icon and label, as a checkbox to screen readers. Both test the file's keys (`file_context_keys`): the list's, the document's `context_keys` and Monaco's `multiDiffEditorItemAllUnchangedRegionsShown`, set while every unchanged region of the file shows (`DiffEditor::all_unchanged_regions_shown`). `set_options_override` sets options every file takes over its own, as Monaco's widget-level `diffEditorOptions` do, so a list-wide `ignore_trim_whitespace` now reaches a file with its own options; the list's layout still applies after it.
- **Find and multi-cursor (#485):** Ported Monaco's words of any non-separator characters for Cmd/Ctrl+D and the find seed, Cmd/Ctrl+D sessions ending on text focus changes, move and add-previous starting from the primary cursor's word, Select All Occurrences without the find widget's match limit, the find input's toggles flashing and the find widget's edge cases (audit 5 §A.6–§A.9, §C.25, §C.26).
  - **Symbol-only words:** Cmd/Ctrl+D, its variants and the find seed take the word at the cursor as Monaco's `getConfiguredWordAtPosition` does, any run of characters that aren't whitespace or word separators, so an emoji, `→→` or `--` (with `-` not a separator) is selected and searched for instead of ignored.
  - **Sessions on focus changes:** a Cmd/Ctrl+D session ends when the editor text loses the focus, and when it gains it unless `selected_text_match_mode` is `FindOptions`, dropping a word session's whole-word and match-case overrides.
  - **Move and add previous:** only Add Selection to Next Find Match expands every empty cursor to its word; Move Selection to Next/Previous Find Match and Add Selection to Previous Find Match begin a session whose first result is the primary cursor's word (Monaco's `currentMatch`), which move puts in place of the last selection and add appends. Several selections now share their text only when none after the first is empty, as Monaco's `modelRangesContainSameText`.
  - **Select All Occurrences:** no longer stops at the find widget's 19,999 matches; only `multi_cursor_limit` caps the cursors.
  - **Find options flash:** with find open, a multicursor session that uses the find options flashes the find input's toggles (Monaco `FindInput.highlightFindOptions`, a 100ms fade from yellow, or translucent white in dark themes). The toggle commands highlight the options only while find is closed, as Monaco's.
  - **Find widget edge cases:** the narrow and collapsed thresholds use the matches count's grown width (Monaco `MAX_MATCHES_COUNT_WIDTH`), and the find seed's length limit counts UTF-16 units and the model's line breaks (`getValueLengthInRange`) instead of bytes.
- **Token colors outside the main view (#484):** Ported Monaco's semantic token colors on sticky scroll lines and in the minimap, the minimap's injected text, and `editor.selectionForeground` in high-contrast themes only (audit 5 §B.5–§B.7).
  - **Semantic tokens on sticky lines and in the minimap:** sticky scroll lines draw the semantic token styles over the syntax highlighting as their rows do (Monaco renders the model's line tokens, `getViewLineRenderingData`), and the minimap colors its lines with them (`getMinimapLinesRenderingData`), redrawing its tiles when new tokens land or semantic highlighting is turned off.
  - **Injected text in the minimap:** minimap lines are the view lines' content, so inlay hints, `before`/`after` text and ghost text show and shift the rest of the line, wrapping with it, in their `tokens` colors or the default token color (`getLineTokensWithInjections`); a row whose injected text changes is drawn again. `LineInjectedText` gains a `tokens` field.
  - **`editor.selectionForeground`:** selected text is recolored only in high-contrast themes (Monaco's `inline-selected-text` decoration, added when `isHighContrast`), and not with a transparent color; other themes keep the token colors under the selection.
- **Embedded languages and bracket configuration (#481):** ported Monaco's typing rules at the cursor's language, its bracket pairs over `brackets` and `colorizedBracketPairs` together, and its handling of empty bracket and onEnter lists (audit 5 §F.1–§F.4).
  - **Typing in an embedded language:** Enter, typing and electric characters use the language at the cursor, as JavaScript in an HTML `<script>`: Enter after `  /** doc` there continues the doc comment with ` * `. Its part of the line is what the onEnter and indentation rules see, and the line before counts only when that language continues from it. Typing leaves a line that mixes languages alone, Enter keeps such a line's indentation, and the indentation inherited from the lines above stops at a line starting in another language. Auto-closing and surrounding pairs stay the model's language's, as in Monaco.
  - **Bracket pairs:** bracket pair guides, `bracket_pairs_in_range`, `match_bracket` and `find_enclosing_brackets` pair every bracket of `brackets` and `colorizedBracketPairs`, so HTML's `{}` (whose `colorizedBracketPairs` is empty) get guides. Colorization colors only the colorized pairs, which default to `brackets` without `<>`: a closed `<…>` is neither colored nor nests the brackets inside it, and unmatched brackets are still marked as unexpected.
  - **Empty lists:** `brackets: []` or `onEnterRules: []` count as set: `onEnterRules: []` alone gives Enter the default bracket rules, and `brackets: []` turns them off.
  - **Indentation rules in strings:** brackets of `colorizedBracketPairs` are removed from strings and comments before the indentation rules match, like `brackets`, so `x = "(";` doesn't indent the next line when `(` is only a colorized pair.
- **Decorations and the gutter (#483):** Ported Monaco's decoration glyphs hidden without the glyph margin, the glyph margin and line decorations on a `showIfCollapsed` decoration's own range (only the decorations overlay ends it on the line before), indent and bracket guides placed by the space width, tabular figures in line numbers (sticky ones too), and `DecorationOptions::hide_in_comment_tokens` and `hide_in_string_tokens` (`hideInCommentTokens`, `hideInStringTokens`) (audit 5 §B.1–§B.4, §G.9).
- **Suggest word ranges and accepting (#470):** Ported Monaco's use of the language's `wordPattern` for suggest's words: the default insert and replace ranges, quick suggestions (`shouldAutoTrigger`), word-based items' ranges, `atEndOfWord`, Tab completion and inline quick suggestions. In CSS, accepting `font-weight` after `font-w` no longer gives `font-font-weight`, and typing `-` keeps suggesting; by default `$` separates words. Accepting after moving the caret right inside the word without typing no longer deletes the text after the word (Monaco's `LineSuffix` delta), and commit characters no longer accept the focused item while the list is frozen (audit 5 §C.1, §C.2, §C.3).
- **Code lens and code actions (#477):** Ported Monaco's code lens zones following their line during edits, its refresh and resolve timing, the lightbulb hidden under a drag and the lone AI fix not asking again (audit 5 §C.14–§C.17).
  - **Zones follow their line:** each lens's range is tracked through edits as Monaco's `codeLensDecorationOptions` decorations are (growing when typing at its edges, collapsing when an edit replaces it), measuring again only the lines an edit touches, and on every edit each line's zone moves to its first lens's line right away, as Monaco's `onDidChangeModelContent` listener does. A line break typed above a lens or the line above deleted no longer leaves the zone over the wrong line until the next request, which then blanked it and faded it in again. Lenses whose ranges an edit removed, or now on the same line as others, go at once, and "Show CodeLens Commands for Current Line" finds the lenses on their current line.
  - **Refresh and resolve timing:** gaining text focus asks for the lenses again after the debounce, and losing it cancels a request still waiting (Monaco `onDidFocusEditorText`, `onDidBlurEditorText`). The visible lenses are resolved after a moving-average pause of at least 250ms once the scroll position or layout changes or new lenses arrive, instead of every frame, so a fling resolves only where it stops; each run cancels the resolves still running, and edits cancel them too (Monaco `_resolveCodeLensesScheduler`).
  - **Lightbulb under a drag:** a drag with the primary button entering the lightbulb hides it (Monaco `LightBulbWidget`'s `mouseenter`).
  - **Lone AI fix:** clicking a lightbulb whose only action is an AI fix runs it without asking for code actions again afterwards (Monaco's `retrigger: false`).
- **LSP bridge: code lenses, document links, document colors and linked editing (#482):** Ported `monaco-languageclient`'s code lens, document link, color and linked editing range features to `gpui-monaco-lsp` (audit 5 §G.5).
  - **Code lenses:** a server with `codeLensProvider` gets a `CodeLensProvider` (`textDocument/codeLens`), and `codeLens/resolve` fills in lenses without a command when it has `resolveProvider`. `workspace/codeLens/refresh` calls `refresh_code_lenses` instead of being refused, so rust-analyzer's "Run | Debug" and "N references" lenses show and update.
  - **Document links:** `documentLinkProvider` registers a `LinkProvider` (`textDocument/documentLink`), with targets normalized as uris, tooltips, and `documentLink/resolve` for links without a target.
  - **Colors:** `colorProvider` registers a `DocumentColorProvider` (`textDocument/documentColor` and `textDocument/colorPresentation`), so the color decorators and picker use the server's colors and presentations.
  - **Linked editing:** `linkedEditingRangeProvider` registers a `LinkedEditingRangeProvider` (`textDocument/linkedEditingRange`), with the server's word pattern; `vscode-html-language-server` renames tag pairs.
  - **Capabilities:** the client advertises `codeLens`, `documentLink` (with `tooltipSupport`), `colorProvider` and `linkedEditingRange`, and `workspace.codeLens.refreshSupport`.
  - **`data` on `CodeLens` and `DocumentLink`:** both have a `data` field (like `CodeAction` and `CompletionItem`) that their provider's resolve gets back; the bridge keeps the server's lens or link there. Struct literals need `data: None` or `..Default::default()`.
- **Diff editor (#480):** Ported Monaco's line endings in staged values and copied deleted lines, reveals only in a diff with changes, the compare decorations of a compared moved block, the gutter toolbars' overflow menu and the accessible diff viewer's whitespace and control-character rendering (audit 5 §E.1–§E.5).
  - **CRLF in staged values and copies:** `DiffHunkContext::original_with_modified_changes` joins each side's text with its model's line ending (Monaco `TextModelText`), and "Copy deleted lines", "Copy deleted line (N)" and copying a selection in the inline deleted lines use the original model's line ending (`getValueInRange`), an empty line copying as that line ending. Staging a hunk of a CRLF file no longer rewrites every line ending.
  - **Reveals in identical diffs and content flushes:** a cursor in an identical diff no longer reveals lines in the kept whole-file region (Monaco's `ensureModifiedLineIsVisible` returns while the diff has no mappings), so after the first change everything but its context hides; cursor changes from a content flush (`set_value`, a new model) reveal nothing either.
  - **Compared moved blocks:** while a moved block is compared, each change gets its line backgrounds and every inner change a plain text background, as in Monaco's `movedTextToCompare` branch: no whole-line text background for a pure insertion or deletion, no empty-range bars and no in-range filter.
  - **Gutter toolbar overflow:** past 3 buttons (1 for a small change) the hunk and selection toolbars show a "More Actions..." button opening a menu with the rest below it (Monaco `WorkbenchToolBar`'s `overflowBehavior`), instead of dropping them; the button's tooltip is hidden while its menu is open, and a toolbar taller than its change (a small first-line change with the overflow button) stays inside the gutter.
  - **Accessible diff viewer rows:** each row is rendered with its side's `render_whitespace`, `render_control_characters`, ligatures, tab size and `stop_rendering_line_after` (with the "Show more" label), as Monaco's `renderViewLine2` does; deleted rows use the original editor's options.
- **Folding (#479):** Ported Monaco's folding commands waiting for the first ranges (after `CodeEditor::new`, `set_model` or `set_value`), Remove Manual Folding Ranges computing the ranges again and middle clicks on a collapsed line's `⋯` or end unfolding it recursively (audit 5 §D.1–§D.3).
- **Quick access (#478):** Ported Monaco's Go to Offset counting CRLF line breaks as two characters, Go to Symbol activating the enclosing symbol only on its first population, trimmed symbol names, the "does not provide symbol information" label with a wait for a provider, and the quick input's busy progress bar (audit 5 §C.23–§C.24).
- **Content hover: the character under the mouse, keeping a hover the mouse moves toward, and focus (#474):** Ported Monaco's hover anchor, `isMouseGettingCloser`, the card's mouse leave, its focus across results and on hiding, and the verbosity command and buttons (audit 5 §C.9–§C.13).
  - **The character under the mouse:** a mouse hover is about the character the mouse is on (Monaco's mouse target `range`), not the nearer caret position: providers are asked at its start, and a decoration's message shows only when the decoration holds that character (a `show_if_collapsed` one, as markers are, a character more each side). Resting on the left half of the `;` in `let x = foo;` no longer shows a decoration on `foo`, the right half of the `.` in `foo.bar` asks about the `.` rather than `bar`, and the right half of the `;` shows a marker on `foo`.
  - **Moving toward the hover:** after the hiding delay, a sticky hover stays while the mouse isn't more than 4px farther from the card than it has been since it was asked for, so the mouse can cross blank space or other text to reach it; the hover where the mouse is is computed meanwhile and an empty one keeps the card (`insistOnKeepingHoverVisible`).
  - **Leaving the card:** the mouse leaving the card (or the glyph hover) for outside the editor, where it overflows, hides it.
  - **Focus:** a focused hover keeps the focus when a provider's part streams in (the new card takes over the old one's focus), and hiding a hover that had the focus, by scrolling for example, returns it to the editor.
  - **Verbosity:** Increase or Decrease Hover Verbosity without a focused part updates every part that supports it, and a part's + and - stay at the bottom of what the card shows of it while scrolling; the buttons now act on their own part when markers come before it.
- **Inline toolbar, color pickers, paste/drop and the message (#476):** ported Monaco's always-shown toolbar count, the pickers closing on edits from elsewhere, failed paste resolves, re-picking the active paste option and the message's close bounds (audit 5 §C.18–§C.22).
  - **Toolbar count while typing:** with `show_toolbar: Always`, typing no longer blanks the "1/3" count and greys Previous/Next: while the toolbar shows, a request that wasn't explicit is made again explicitly (Monaco's "request explicit" autorun), and the toolbar keeps the last count and enabled buttons for 100ms after the editor has none.
  - **Color pickers and other edits:** a text change the picker didn't make (another editor on the model, the host, `execute_edits`, undo) hides the hover holding a color picker and the standalone picker and refocuses the editor, so picking a color afterwards no longer writes over the wrong characters.
  - **Failed paste and drop resolves:** when `resolve_document_paste_edit` fails, "Error resolving edit" is logged and nothing is inserted; the options button shows instead (unless `show_paste_selector` is off), offering the other edits. Picking one then doesn't undo anything first.
  - **Re-picking the active option:** picking the checked paste or drop option only closes the list; the options button stays.
  - **Message close bounds:** the inline message (e.g. "No definition found") closes when the mouse moves past the first column of the third row below where it first moved, as Monaco's `Range(line - 3, 1, mouseLine + 3, 1)` does, instead of keeping that whole row.
- **Editor commands and undo (#473):** Ported Monaco's non-keyboard `type` and `paste`, the linked-editing mirror joining a paste's undo step and `cursorMove` by view columns (audit 5 §A.3–§A.5).
  - **`type` and `paste` from another source:** `trigger_from` with any source but `"keyboard"` types without the typing interceptors (Monaco `TypeWithoutInterceptorsOperation`): `(` no longer auto-closes, a line break isn't auto-indented, and no `WillType`/`DidType` fires, so suggest's trigger characters, signature help, inline completions and format-on-type stay quiet. `paste` from another source fires no `DidPaste`, so format-on-paste and auto-indent-on-paste don't run, as in Monaco's `_type` and `_paste`.
  - **Linked editing after a paste or cut:** the mirror edit joins the undo step of the edit that triggered it even when that edit pushed an undo stop after itself (Monaco `popUndoStop`), so one Undo reverts both tags.
  - **`cursorMove` left and right:** move on the cursors' view states, as the arrow keys do, so they step over a fold in one move, and `halfLine` is half the wrapped segment's length rather than the model line's.
- **Suggest and parameter hints (#475):** ported Monaco's `sortText` fallback, the undo step of resolved additional edits and the provider order of signature help (audit 5 §C.4–§C.8).
  - **Sort text:** an item without a `sortText` (or an empty one) sorts by its label against items that have one, as Monaco fills it with the label: `{label: "bbb"}` now comes before `{label: "aaa", sortText: "zzz"}`.
  - **Resolved additional edits:** additional edits that arrive with the resolve after accepting an item join the insertion's undo step, so one Undo removes both; typing before they arrive still keeps them a separate step.
  - **Shifted additional edits:** an additional edit on the request's line that starts after the request position moves by the text typed since the request, so `;` after `)` stays after it.
  - **Language and provider changes:** an open suggest session closes when the model's language changes, and asks the providers again when a completion provider is registered or removed (closing when none remain).
  - **Signature help order:** the providers are asked one at a time, best first, and the first result wins, even one without signatures, which hides the hints; later providers aren't asked.
- **LSP bridge uris and edits (#471):** The bridge now names documents as `monaco-languageclient` does and applies the server's edits through the bulk edit service (audit 5 §G.1–§G.4).
  - **Normalized uris:** documents are keyed and sent by the model's `resource()` (`model.uri.toString()`), so a path with a space or a non-ASCII character attaches (`file:///my project/a.rs` is sent as `file:///my%20project/a.rs`) instead of failing with "invalid uri". A model without a uri attaches as `inmemory://model/N`, and providers get its `resource` in the new `ProviderContext::resource`. Uris from the server are normalized before they're compared, so diagnostics and edits a server publishes for its own spelling (`file:///c%3A/a.ts` for `file:///C:/a.ts`) reach the model, and its definitions in the queried document have no uri. Attached models are registered (`register_model`).
  - **`workspace/applyEdit`:** goes through the new App-level `gpui_monaco::apply_workspace_edit(cx, edit)`, the bulk edit service without an editor. File operations go to the host's file edit handler, models that aren't attached are edited, change annotations that need confirmation go through the preview handler, and an edit for a stale document version isn't applied, as in `vscode-languageclient`. The response says whether the edit was applied, with the error as the `failureReason`, instead of always `applied: true`. `LspClient::apply_workspace_edit` now returns a task.
  - **Diagnostics for documents that aren't attached:** go to the marker service through the new `set_markers_for_uri(cx, owner, uri, markers)`, so `model_markers` lists them and a model opened later shows them at once.
  - **Code action diagnostics:** requests send the server's diagnostics back as it published them, with numeric codes as numbers, related information and `data`, even after an edit moved the marker. Markers the server didn't publish keep their related information.
  - **This document's edits:** `CodeEditor::apply_workspace_edit` treats an edit naming the editor's model in another spelling (`%20` for a space) as this document's, so the cursors follow it and a single snippet edit starts a snippet session.
- **Widget focus vs text focus (#472):** ported Monaco's split between an editor's text focus and its widgets' focus (audit 5 §H.1, §H.2, §D.6).
  - **Host keys in widget inputs:** a key from `add_editor_action` or `add_keybinding_rules` pressed in the find, replace, rename or quick input box runs on the editor, as Monaco's keybindings run on the editor with widget focus, instead of in the box (uppercasing the search string rather than the selection). The box keeps the focus. The boxes have `textInputFocus` but no longer `editorTextFocus` or `editorFocus` (their editor has `editorFocus`), so a rule with `when: "CodeEditor && editorTextFocus"` doesn't apply there; the built-in text keys now need `textInputFocus` and still edit the box. A built-in action run with `run_action`, `trigger` or a rule while a widget has the focus runs on the editor without moving the focus.
  - **Focused sticky line:** while a sticky line has the keyboard, `editorTextFocus` and `textInputFocus` are off (`editorFocus` stays), so Cmd/Ctrl+/, Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+K, Alt+Down and the other modified text keys no longer edit the text or move the cursor.
  - **Bracket highlights:** the matched pair stays highlighted while a widget inside the editor (the find input, a focused hover) has the focus, and clears when the focus leaves the editor, as Monaco's `hasWidgetFocus` check does.
- Fixed an IME composition with one cursor being one undo step per update, so Undo after composing `日本` went back through `にほん`, `にほ` and `に`, and its commit sealing the typing before it. As in Monaco (`compositionType`), the updates and the commit are typing edits at every cursor, one cursor included, joining the typing before and after them in one undo step, and a composition starting over the selection replaces it in that step. An IME or autocorrect replacement on the cursor's line that doesn't contain the cursor (`teh |` corrected to `the`) no longer collapses the cursors and moves the caret into the text: as Monaco's `_emitTypeEvent` does, the text between the range and the cursor is padded in and the replacement made at every cursor (audit 5 §A.1, §A.2, #469).
- Fixed `MultiDiffEditor::set_scroll_top` called before the list's first layout being undone by the first change revealed on load, which also left the stuck file's original and modified sides showing different lines while it caught up. A scroll set after loading now wins over that reveal, as it does in Monaco, where the reveal happens at once; the first change still gets the cursor (#465).
- **Monaco quirks and VS Code additions, decided (#431):** Ported Monaco's interval line numbers with folded lines, its standard multi diff header without a shadow and its tokenizer disposal, and documented the rest (audit 4 §B.15, §E.4, §E.29, §F.7, §H.5, §G.17).
  - **Interval line numbers:** with `LineNumbers::Interval`, the always-numbered "last" line is the one whose number is the view's line count, as in Monaco: with lines 2–10 of 25 folded, line 16 is numbered and line 25 isn't.
  - **Sticky header shadow:** the default (`NoCardsNonCompact`) multi diff variant no longer draws a shadow under a header with its file scrolled under it. Monaco's rule for it never matches; only the cards variant's header floats with a shadow.
  - **Disposing a tokenizer:** disposing a `set_monarch_tokens_provider`, `set_tokens_provider` or factory tokenizer no longer brings back a runtime tokenizer it replaced, as in Monaco; the language goes back to its own highlighting (its registered highlighter or tree-sitter grammar), as listed in Differences from Monaco.
  - **Kept and documented:** the accessible diff viewer's correctly ordered `@@` header (Monaco's sides are swapped) and Insert toggling overtype mode (a VS Code workbench binding; one `KeybindingRule::remove` drops it) are in Differences from Monaco.
  - **Migrating from Monaco:** a `KeyMod`/`KeyCode` table (`secondary-` for `KeyMod.CtrlCmd`, chords), end-inclusive `Range` helpers, `create_model` for `monaco.editor.create`, and rows for `createDiffEditor`, `createMultiFileDiffEditor`, `getLanguages` and `registerNewSymbolNameProvider`; `keymap.md` and `KeybindingRule::keystrokes` explain `secondary-`.
- **Inline edits and the paste chip (#430):** ported Monaco's inline edit and paste options button details (audit 4 §C.44–§C.47, §G.16).
  - **Docked gutter icon:** the inline edit pill beside its edit shows `keyboard-tab-above` (the cursor on or above the edit's first line) or `keyboard-tab-below` when Tab doesn't accept, instead of arrows.
  - **Toolbar warning:** the warning row above the inline toolbar shows the message only, without an icon (the `icon` is the ghost text's).
  - **Paste button anchor:** the paste and drop options button sits below the end of the edit (the first range, tracked as it grows), not at the cursor, which a snippet leaves on its first tabstop.
  - **Keybinding labels:** the word replacement's alternative action label and the jump-to label show the keys bound to `inlineSuggest.commitAlternativeAction` and `inlineSuggest.commit` as keycaps, rather than a hard-coded "⇧⇥" and "Tab", so they follow rebinding and the platform.
  - **Command icons:** `InlineCompletionList::commands` are `InlineCompletionCommand`s (Monaco's `{ command, icon? }`); the gutter menu draws their `icon`, `symbol-event` without one. A `Command` converts with `.into()`.
- **Quick input and color picker polish (#429):** Ported Monaco's Go to Symbol containers and item activation, the `index-zero` toggle, the standalone quick input's highlight colors, the color picker's tracked range and the standalone picker closing over a swatch (audit 4 §C.26–§C.31).
  - **Go to Symbol containers:** a symbol's own `container_name` is its description, ahead of its parent's name (Monaco `OutlineModel._flattenDocumentSymbols`), so flat results such as the LSP bridge's `SymbolInformation` show and match theirs. The words after the first still match the container, and the matched part of the description is now highlighted like the label's (Monaco `highlights.description`); deprecated symbols highlight neither. `outline::flatten_symbols` follows the same rule, so the diff editor's breadcrumbs see it too.
  - **Go to Symbol activation:** Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+O opens with Monaco's `ItemActivation.NONE`, so with the cursor in no symbol nothing is active or previewed and the view doesn't move; the innermost symbol around the cursor is still active, and typing (or `@` from the help list) activates the first item again. Down or Up with nothing active focuses the first row (Monaco's list `focusNext`/`focusPrevious`).
  - **Zero-based offset toggle:** the `::` toggle is Monaco's `Toggle` with the `index-zero` codicon (16px, 1px padding and border) instead of a "0" glyph.
  - **Highlight colors:** matched characters in the quick input use `standaloneQuickInput.css`'s colors by base theme (`#0097fb` in vs-dark, `#0066bf` in vs and `#9dddff` on its focused row, `#f38518` in hc-black, `#0f4a85` in hc-light), new `EditorColors::quick_input_highlight_foreground` and `quick_input_focus_highlight_foreground`, instead of `list.highlightForeground` and `quickInputList.focusHighlightForeground`.
  - **Color picker range:** after writing a presentation, the picker tracks the replaced range through every edit with `GrowsOnlyWhenTypingAfter` (Monaco `updateEditorModel`'s tracked range), so additional text edits above the color and presentations over several lines no longer leave the next write on a stale range.
  - **Standalone picker:** it hides when the mouse moves onto a color swatch in the text (Monaco's `onMouseMove` over `colorpicker-color-decoration`).
  - **Tests:** Go to Symbol matching and highlighting a flat symbol's and a parent's container, activating nothing outside symbols (Enter, Down, typing and the help's `@`), the toggle's size, `flatten_symbols` preferring a symbol's own container, the standalone highlight colors, the picker tracking its color through an additional edit and a two-line presentation, and the standalone picker hiding over a swatch but not over text.
- **Gutter and minimap (#427):** Ported Monaco's whole-line minimap fold highlight, line decorations by view range and 16px glyph codicons (audit 4 §B.12–§B.14).
  - **Minimap fold highlight:** the fold decoration is whole-line on the header (`isWholeLine`), so its minimap range runs from the header's first column to its end: a half-color band on every wrapped piece of the header and the full `editor.foldBackground` over its text. Before, the range was one point at the line end, so only the last piece got the band and nothing marked the text.
  - **Line decorations by view range:** `gutter_bar`, `lines_decoration_text`, `margin_background` and glyphs are drawn on the view lines of the decoration's view range (Monaco `LinesDecorationsOverlay`, `MarginViewLineDecorationsOverlay`, `GlyphMarginWidgets`), its start converted with `PositionAffinity.Right` and its end with `Left`, so a decoration on a wrapped line's first columns is on its first piece only (a whole-line one on all of them). `first_line_decoration` is on the range's start line only, not every row's first piece, and the `lines_decorations_tooltip` box is `decorationsWidth` wide, over the folding column too.
  - **Glyph codicons:** a codicon `glyph` is shaped at 16px (Monaco's `cgmr codicon`, `font: 16px/1 codicon`) and centered in its lane and line, not at the editor's font size.
- **Providers registered or removed later (#416):** ported Monaco's provider registry `onDidChange` listeners for inlay hints, code lenses and code actions (audit 4 §G.8, §C.36; the links item, §G.7, was fixed by #415). Registering or unregistering an inlay hints provider asks open editors for hints again right away (Monaco `InlayHintsController._update`), so a provider's hints show without an edit or a scroll and an unregistered provider's hints go, all of them when none is left. Code lens providers do the same after the lens debounce (Monaco `CodeLensContribution._onModelChange`). The lightbulb is asked for again right away when a code action provider is registered or removed, the model's language changes or the `lightbulb` option changes (Monaco `CodeActionModel._update`), instead of only after the caret moves or markers change.
- **Multi diff editor polish (#425):** Ported Monaco's editors for collapsed files, initial file height, keyboard-reachable chevrons, `focus()` and rename flag in `MultiDiffEditor` (audit 4 §E.25–§E.28).
  - **Collapsed files keep their editor:** every file in view has a `DiffEditor`, collapsed or not (hidden while collapsed), as Monaco binds every visible item, so collapsing the active file keeps `active_control()` and no longer fires `ActiveControlChanged` on each collapse and expand.
  - **Initial height:** a file not measured yet takes Monaco's 500px in all, header and bottom padding included, instead of 500px for the editor alone (540px with the standard header); revealing file 10 of 20 unmeasured files scrolls to 5000px, as in Monaco.
  - **Keyboard:** the chevron is a focusable button in every variant, as Monaco's `Button`: Tab reaches it (after the compact variants' header, before the file's editor) and Enter or Space collapses or expands the file and activates it. Tab and Shift+Tab move on from a header, chevron or binary placeholder.
  - **`focus_active`:** Monaco's `focus()` focuses the active file's modified editor, or a binary file's placeholder, and returns `false` when there's no active file or it isn't in view.
  - **Rename flag:** `R` and the struck-through original path show only when `original_label` differs from `label` (Monaco compares the two paths), not whenever `original_label` is set.
- **Tokenization (#428):** ported Monaco's `maxTokenizationLineLength` in the Monarch tokenizer and its absence for `setTokensProvider`, the bracket pair tokenizer's standard token types, configuration lookup by language id for embedded languages, and one `registerTokensProviderFactory` factory per language (audit 4 §F.3–§F.6). A model's `max_tokenization_line_length` now reaches its Monarch highlighter, embedded languages included, and changing it re-tokenizes: a skipped line keeps its state, so an unterminated comment on it no longer colors the next line, and limits above 20,000 highlight longer lines. Languages highlighted by `set_tokens_provider` or a factory's tokens provider tokenize lines of any length, for display, `highlight_name_at`, `token_type_at` and `line_tokens`. Bracket pair colorization, bracket pair guides and inline completions' bracket closing skip every token whose standard type isn't `Other` (`regexp…`, `invalid.string`, `meta.comment`), and colorization treats lines past the limit as plain. Comment commands and word patterns in tree-sitter injections (JavaScript in an HTML `<script>`) use the language's current configuration right after `set_language_configuration`, not the one the region was parsed with. A new `register_tokens_provider_factory` disposes the language's previous factory, which isn't called (or whose tokenizer is removed). New `MonarchHighlighter::with_max_tokenization_line_length`, `EmbeddedLanguages::max_tokenization_line_length` and `Highlighter::limits_line_length`.
- **Suggest and parameter hints styling (#423):** ported Monaco's suggest and parameter hints styling details (audit 4 §C.15–§C.20).
  - **High contrast borders:** `hc-black` and `hc-light` give the suggest list, the details panel and parameter hints Monaco's 2px borders, also in the list's and details' layout (`SuggestDetailsWidget.getLayoutInfo`'s `borderWidth`).
  - **Row text:** the right-hand `.details-label` is no longer dimmed; a deprecated item dims only its label, not its `label_detail`; `detail`, `label_detail` and `label_description` lose their line breaks (`stripNewLines`) instead of clipping or turning them into spaces.
  - **Read-more chevron:** the focused row shows it under the mouse while the details panel is open too, so clicking it closes the panel.
  - **Icon metrics:** with `suggest.show_icons` off, rows keep the codicon's 4px margin (`.no-icons` hides only the glyph); a color item's swatch box is as wide as the row is tall.
  - **Parameter hints scrollbar:** the body is a `DomScrollableElement` again, with the 10px scrollbar shown while the mouse is over it.
  - **Details scroll:** the details panel goes back to the top when the focused item resolves or shows "Loading..." (Monaco's `renderItem` and `renderLoading` set `scrollTop = 0`).
- **View lines and cursors (#426):** Ported Monaco's blink reset, grapheme-wide block cursor, off-screen active bracket guide, advanced wrap indent, whitespace with injected text and NUL rendering (audit 4 §B.5–§B.10).
  - **Blink reset:** changing the options restarts the cursor blink (Monaco `ViewCursors.onConfigurationChanged` → `_updateBlinking`), so turning on `read_only` or `cursor_blinking: Solid` shows a solid cursor at once instead of blinking (or staying hidden) until the next cursor move.
  - **Grapheme-wide block cursor:** the block, outline and underline cursors cover the whole grapheme after the cursor, and the block draws all of it in the cursor text color (Monaco `_getGraphemeAwarePosition`), so a decomposed `é` keeps its accent and ZWJ emoji and flags get a full-width block. Inside a grapheme the cursor draws at its start.
  - **Off-screen active bracket guide:** with the primary cursor out of view, the active bracket pair is the innermost pair at the cursor, highlighted only if it's in view (Monaco `getLinesBracketGuides`), instead of the innermost visible pair containing it, which marked an outer block active.
  - **Advanced wrap indent:** with `wrapping_strategy: Advanced`, continuation lines are indented by the existing indent plus `tab_size` per extra indent, not rounded to a tab stop, and only the existing indent decides sticking to column 1 (Monaco's DOM line-break computer): 2 spaces with `Indent` and tab size 4 indent by 6, not 4.
  - **Whitespace with injected text:** rendered whitespace scans the view line, injected text included (Monaco `_applyRenderWhitespace`): spaces followed by an `after` decoration's text aren't trailing, and spaces inside injected text get marks with `All`.
  - **NUL:** a NUL shows as U+FFFD with `render_control_characters: false` (Monaco's `&#00;`), not as whatever the font draws for it.
- **Links through Monaco's opener service, and markdown gaps (#415):** followed links open the way Monaco's `OpenerService` and its default openers do, `execute_link_provider` returns the editor's links, and markdown renders unlabeled code blocks, `<kbd>` and task lists (audit 4 §C.9, §C.10, §G.14, §G.15).
  - **Links into the editor's model:** a link no `register_link_opener` opener takes is opened like Monaco's `EditorOpener` in the focused editor (Monaco `getFocusedCodeEditor`; for Cmd/Ctrl+click and `openLink`, the editor itself). A link into its own model selects the `#L<line>[,<col>][-L<line>[,<col>]]` fragment (Monaco `extractSelection`) and centers it (the standalone `doOpenEditor`). A link to another model goes to the `register_editor_opener` openers, with the fragment's position or range. This covers markdown links in hovers, suggest details, parameter hints and messages, which used to drop anything but web, mail and `command:` links, and detected links. Unlike Monaco, which asks the editor openers first, a link into the editor's own model doesn't reach them, as for go to definition.
  - **Detected links open by default:** Cmd/Ctrl+click, the `openLink` middle click and `editor.action.openLink` now open `http`, `https` and `mailto` links in the browser and run `command:` links (Monaco `openLinkOccurrence` passes `allowCommands: true`) when no link opener takes them, as markdown links already did. A marker's code link opens web links too but swallows `command:` links, as Monaco's `open(code.target)` does. **Breaking:** `CodeEditorEvent::OpenLink` now only reports links nothing opened: links to other models no editor opener took, and other schemes. Hosts that opened web links from the event should drop that, or take them first with `register_link_opener`.
  - **Recomputing links:** links are recomputed right away when the model's language or the link providers change (Monaco `onDidChangeModelLanguage` and the provider registry's `onDidChange`), not only after edits.
  - **`api_commands::execute_link_provider`:** now Monaco's `getLinks`: the built-in URL links (Monaco's `*` link provider) come first and each provider's links, worst-ranked first, replace those they overlap or touch, so the result is sorted and the best provider wins. Before, it concatenated the providers' links in rank order, overlaps included, without the URL links. It shares the editor's code.
  - **Markdown:** a code block without a language is highlighted in the editor model's language in hovers, suggest details, parameter hints and the inline suggestion toolbar (Monaco `EditorMarkdownCodeBlockRenderer`), and stays plain text in messages, which Monaco renders without an editor. `<kbd>` (with `support_html`) is a keycap in `keybindingLabel.background`/`foreground`, with its border and bottom border, rounded by 3px (Monaco's `renderedMarkdown.css`; the inset `widget.shadow` isn't drawn), instead of inline code. GFM task lists (`- [ ]`, `- [x]`) show a disabled checkbox instead of the bullet and the literal `[ ]`. **Breaking:** `MarkdownStyle` has a new `code_language` field.
  - **Tests:** Monaco's `extractSelection` cases, GPUI tests clicking hover links into the model (a position and a range) and to another model (the editor openers, the link openers first), detected links opening in the browser, running commands and moving the editor without an opener, links following language and provider changes, `execute_link_provider` merging with the URL links, keycaps, task lists, the default code block language, and the default openers. Tests that expected `OpenLink` for web links now look at the browser. The playground's demo hover shows an unlabeled code block, keycaps and a task list.
- **Diff editor polish (#424):** Ported Monaco's gutter item spans, moved-block line positions, revert arrow and fold glyph lanes, modified-editor overrides, sash ratio, legacy inner changes and the old accessible diff viewer ids (audit 4 §E.14–§E.20).
  - **Gutter spans:** a gutter toolbar spans the view zones next to its change (Monaco `EditorGutter.render` with `getTopForLineNumber(_, true)`/`getBottomForLineNumber(_, true)`): inline, the deleted lines above; side by side, the padding below. Before, it spanned the modified lines only, so it sat on the last line of an inline change and counted as small at line 1.
  - **Moved-block lines:** a moved block's line starts halfway from the top of its "Code moved" caption to the bottom of its last line (Monaco `computeLineStart`), about 9px higher than before.
  - **Glyph lanes:** margin revert arrows go in the right lane at z-index 10001 and fold-unchanged glyphs at z-index 10001 (Monaco `RevertButton.getPosition`, `unchangedLinesDecorationShow`), so a host glyph on a changed line no longer hides them.
  - **Modified-editor overrides:** the modified editor gets `reveal_horizontal_right_padding` 45 (15 plus the overview ruler) and no `vertical_has_arrows` (Monaco `_adjustOptionsForRightHandSide`).
  - **Sash ratio:** a dragged sash survives a new `split_view_default_ratio`; only a reset follows the default again (Monaco `_sashRatio ?? splitViewDefaultRatio`).
  - **Legacy inner changes:** legacy changes without character changes (20+ lines) give `char_changes: None` in `line_changes` and get no margin revert arrow, and an empty original takes Monaco's provider fast path (one change, one inner change covering the modified text) before either algorithm.
  - **API:** `trigger` takes `editor.action.diffReview.next`/`.prev` (Monaco's aliases); `reveal_first_diff` does nothing without a model, and `set_model` and the `max_file_size` cut-off drop a reveal still waiting.
- **Input method edits and undo (#422):** Ported Monaco's input method replacements at every selection, undo from outside the editor restoring the focused editor's selections, and Remove Brackets undo steps (audit 4 §A.4, §A.5, §D.10).
  - **Replacing the selection:** text the input method puts in place of exactly the primary selection (the Character Viewer, an emoji picker) is typed at every selection with the typing interceptors (Monaco `_emitTypeEvent` with no deltas, then `type`), so it replaces every selection and keeps the cursors, and `(` surrounds the selection, instead of replacing the primary selection only and dropping the other cursors.
  - **Replacing around one cursor:** a replacement around a single cursor without a composition (a press-and-hold accent, autocorrect) is a `compositionType` as with several cursors: a `TypingOther` edit that joins the typing before it, so one undo removes `café` instead of turning `é` back into `e`.
  - **Undo from outside:** `TextModel::undo`/`redo` called from outside the editor restore the undo step's selections in the focused editor, with the `Undo`/`Redo` reason, as Monaco's `Cursor.onModelContentChanged` does; unfocused editors recover their cursors from markers and report `RecoverFromMarkers`.
  - **Remove Brackets:** each cursor's pair is removed as its own undo step (Monaco pushes an undo stop around each `executeEdits`), so one undo restores only the last cursor's pair; a pair shared by several cursors is still removed once.
- **Peek view zoom (#453):** the peek view's frame, header and height sash use the editor's zoomed line height (Monaco `EditorOption.lineHeight`). Before, they used the unzoomed height, so with font zoom the header and list were sized for smaller lines. Follow-up to #420.
- **Layout changes (#420):** ported Monaco's stable viewport, zoomed line heights and font measurement (audit 4 §F.1, §F.2, §B.11).
  - **First visible line:** changing the options (wrapping, font size, line height, zoom, ...) or the editor's width, and `set_hidden_areas` or folding, keep the first visible line where it is on screen, as Monaco's `ViewModel._onConfigurationChanged` and `setHiddenAreas` do with `_captureStableViewport`; the scroll position no longer keeps its pixel value, which showed an earlier or later line. Not at the top of the file, and not when the first visible line is hidden, as in Monaco.
  - **Fractional zoomed line heights:** the zoomed line height isn't rounded again (Monaco `BareFontInfo._create`): with 18px lines, zoom level 1 lays lines out 19.8px apart, not 20px, and `computed_options().line_height` and `font_info().line_height` are 19.8. Zoomed out, a line height may go under 8px, as in Monaco. Widgets, zones and the diff editor use the zoomed line height too.
  - **Typical half-width character:** `font_info().typical_halfwidth_character_width`, wrapping columns, rulers and `scroll_beyond_last_column` measure an `n`, not an `m`, as Monaco's `typicalHalfwidthCharacterWidth`; with proportional fonts, rulers and wrapping now match Monaco's columns.
