- **Decoration tracking and linked editing (#402):** model decorations follow edits with Monaco's `nodeAcceptEdit` (audit 4 §A.1–§A.3).
  - **Replaced spans:** a decoration edge inside a replaced span used to jump to the start or end of the new text. It now stays as far into the new text as it was into the old, up to the shorter of the two lengths, and only edges past that move to the end. For example, Transform to Uppercase keeps a decoration on its word. Monaco counts these lengths in UTF-16 code units; the port does too, and an edge that would land inside a character moves to that character's end. This applies to user decorations, linked-editing ranges, snippet placeholders, find matches and the auto-closed characters. "Selection insert - across boundary" in `tests/linked_editing.rs` now passes and is no longer ignored.
  - **`collapse_on_replace_edit`:** new `DecorationOptions` field (Monaco `collapseOnReplaceEdit`). An edit replacing the whole range collapses it to the edit's start.
  - **Breaking:** `DecorationSet::apply_patch` and `apply_patch_forcing` take the text before and after the edit, to measure replaced spans.
  - **Linked editing word pattern:** without a provider or language word pattern, linked editing uses Monaco's default word pattern, so a space ends it instead of being mirrored.
  - **Linked editing reinitialize:** turning `linked_editing` off removes the ranges at once. Turning it on, registering or removing a provider, and changing the model or language ask for the ranges at once, as Monaco's `reinitialize` does.
- **Scrolling and mouse focus (#411):** five fixes to scrolling and clicks (audit 4 §D.5–§D.8, §H.4).
  - **Clicks reveal minimally:** a mouse click no longer scrolls sideways to put 30px of room left of the cursor or `reveal_horizontal_right_padding` right of it, so clicking a visible column near either edge keeps the view still (Monaco's `minimalReveal`). A plain click that leaves the cursor where it was doesn't reveal it at all (`_moveTo`).
  - **One-line cursor scrolls jump:** with `smooth_scrolling`, a cursor move that scrolls a line or less (Down on the last visible line) now scrolls right away instead of gliding for 135ms, so held arrow keys keep up with the caret. Longer moves still animate, and so does the horizontal part (Monaco `onRevealRangeRequest`).
  - **No inertia for mouse wheels:** with `inertial_scroll`, physical mouse wheels (whole-line deltas) no longer keep coasting after a notch; only trackpad scrolling does, as in Monaco.
  - **`revealLine` string arguments:** a string `lineNumber` is read like JavaScript's `parseInt`, so `"12px"` or `" 12.9"` reveal line 12 rather than the first line.
  - **Focus on clicks:** clicking the scrollbars or the minimap, or right-clicking the gutter, scrollbars or minimap, no longer takes the focus from another editor; only the text (and line numbers with `select_on_line_numbers`) does, as in Monaco's `_onMouseDown`. With `context_menu` off, a right-click on the line numbers or glyph margin now moves the cursor there (unless it's in a selection) and focuses, as it already did over the text. With `context_menu` off, right-clicking a content widget or view zone still leaves the focus with the widget, where Monaco would focus the editor.
- **Suggest widget (#414):** the list goes above multi-line ghost text, the `suggestionCanResolve`, `suggestionMakesTextEdit` and `suggestionHasInsertAndReplaceRange` context keys match Monaco's, the list keys do nothing while "Loading..." or "No suggestions." shows, and the status bar's labels and keys come from the focused item and the keybindings (audit 4 §C.11–§C.14).
  - **Above ghost text:** once the focused suggestion previews as two or more lines of ghost text (its own, or an inline completion extending it), the list goes above the cursor line when there's more than 150px there, so it doesn't cover them (Monaco `forceRenderingAbove`, from `InlineCompletionsController`). It stays forced until nothing is previewed. Before, the list covered the ghost lines.
  - **Context keys:** `suggestionCanResolve` is true when the provider resolves items, the item has docs, or its detail isn't its label, as the status bar's "Show More" already was. Before, it was true only for items with `data`. `suggestionMakesTextEdit` is false only with `accept_suggestion_on_enter: Smart`, in a session that opened by itself, for an item without other edits that isn't a snippet, once its insert text is typed. Before, it compared the insert text with the text before the cursor in every mode. `suggestionHasInsertAndReplaceRange` compares where the insert and replace ranges end, with the session's ranges for items without them (so mid-word, word-based items have both).
  - **Keys while a message shows:** Down, Up, PageDown and PageUp do nothing while "Loading..." or "No suggestions." shows, as Monaco's select commands run then. Before, they moved the cursor, which closed the widget and cancelled the request.
  - **Status bar:** "Insert" unless the focused item's insert and replace ranges end apart; only then the `suggest.insert_mode` label and the other mode. Before, `insert_mode: Replace` showed "Replace" for every item. The keys are each command's keybinding: a rule or binding added for it, else Monaco's primary key. Insert's is Enter where Enter accepts (`acceptSuggestionOnEnter` and `suggestionMakesTextEdit`), else Tab. Before, the labels were hard-coded, with "⏎" where Monaco's UI label is "Enter".
  - **Playground:** typing `self.sn` shows the `snapshot` suggestion extended over three lines of ghost text.
- **Commands and keybindings (#412):** provider commands run editor actions, `-command` keybinding rules remove a default binding, and Escape closes the find widget and the other heavier Escape rules before the paste/drop options button, as in Monaco (audit 4 §G.5, §G.6, §H.3).
  - **Provider commands:** a completion item's or code action's command that isn't a registered command now runs as `CodeEditor::run_command` runs a keybinding rule's: an editor action (`editor.action.triggerParameterHints` after a function, `editor.action.rename`, …), a Monaco command or a GPUI action, with its first argument, once the current update is done (Monaco `ICommandService.executeCommand`). A command nothing handles goes to the host as `CodeEditorEvent::ExecuteCommand`, as code lenses' do. Before, a completion item's was only logged as not found, and a code action's showed "command … not found" as a message.
  - **`-command` rules:** `KeybindingRule::remove(keystrokes, command)` (or a rule whose command starts with `-`) takes the command's default binding off the keystrokes, or off all its keystrokes if they're empty, and leaves the keys' other bindings (Monaco `-actions.find`). It's bound as GPUI `Unbind` markers, so disposing it brings the binding back in its place, and keybinding labels drop it too. Before, the rule bound a command that doesn't exist, so the keys ran nothing, and a rule without keystrokes failed to parse. Unlike Monaco, a rule with a `when` removes the action wherever the `when` holds rather than only a default with the same `when`, and an action two commands share (`editor.hidePasteWidget` and `editor.hideDropWidget`) goes for both.
  - **Escape:** the paste/drop options button is hidden at Monaco's weight (`EditorContrib`), after closing the find widget, leaving a snippet, closing parameter hints, the suggest widget or the problem zone; before, its own binding outranked all of them.
- **Widget accessibility (#413):** the editor's widgets now have Monaco's roles and names for screen readers, and the editor node has Monaco's role description and `aria-autocomplete` (audit 4 §H.1, §H.2). GPUI builds an accessibility node only for an element with an id and a role, so before, the find, replace, rename and quick inputs were all read as "Editor content", and the menus, lists and find buttons were silent.
  - **Inputs:** the find and replace inputs are "Find" and "Replace", and the rename input is "Rename input. Type new name and press Enter to commit." The quick input is named by its provider as in Monaco: Go to Line's instructions, Go to Symbol's message when there's nothing to list, "Go to Match..."'s placeholder, else "Type to narrow down results.". Widget inputs are plain inputs in Monaco, so they have no role description or `aria-autocomplete`. Internally, `new_widget_input` takes the label.
  - **Find widget:** a "Find / Replace" dialog. Match Case, Whole Word, Regex, Find in Selection and Preserve Case are checkboxes with their checked state, and the other controls are buttons. Each is named by its title with the keybinding, as in Monaco ("Next Match (Enter)"), and Toggle Replace is expanded while the replace input shows.
  - **Menus and lists:** the context menu and its submenus are menus of menu items, with their keys, and a checked item is a checked menu checkbox. The code action list is an "Action Widget" listbox whose options say why an action is disabled ("Extract, Disabled Reason: …"). The references tree is a "References" tree whose rows read Monaco's `ariaMessage`s ("2 symbols in lib.rs, full path /src/lib.rs", "foo(); in main.rs on line 12 at column 5"). In all three the focused row is the active descendant, so arrowing reads each row. The quick input's options are a "Quick Input" listbox, named by Monaco's `ariaLabel` ("Go to line 12, column 1. Press Enter to navigate.", or "name (function)" for a symbol), and the sticky scroll lines are a list of their text. The quick input's input keeps focus, and GPUI only honors an active descendant under the focused element, so the active option is `aria-selected` instead of the input's active descendant (Monaco's `aria-activedescendant` on the combobox).
  - **Editor node:** the role description is "editor", and `aria-autocomplete` is "both", "none" when read-only, or "list" while `set_aria_options` names an active descendant (Monaco's `TextAreaEditContext`).
  - **Tests:** GPUI's test platform builds no accessibility tree, so the widgets go through one `AriaNode` helper that sets the role, label and states, and that the tests can record (hidden `record_aria_for_tests`, `recorded_aria_for_tests` and `CodeEditor::aria_for_tests`).
- **Diff editor high contrast and inline deleted lines (#421):** Ported Monaco's overview colors, deleted-lines borders, tint and render options, and the original side's border (audit 4 §E.9–§E.13).
  - **Overview colors:** the diff overview ruler falls back to Monaco's `defaultInsertColor` and `defaultRemoveColor` (at twice their alpha) when the theme sets neither the overview nor the text colors. Before, its marks were transparent in hc-black and hc-light.
  - **Deleted lines' borders:** the inline view's deleted lines get a 1px `diffEditor.removedTextBorder` around the zone and around each changed run (Monaco's `line-delete` and `char-delete`), dashed in high contrast themes. Before, they had backgrounds only.
  - **Full-width tint:** deleted lines without inner changes (the legacy diff's pure deletions) layer `diffEditor.removedTextBackground` across each view line, as Monaco's `view-line char-delete` does.
  - **Render options:** deleted lines follow the modified editor's `render_whitespace` (`·` and `→` in `editorWhitespace.foreground`), `render_control_characters` and `font_ligatures`, as Monaco's `RenderOptions.fromEditor`. Before, they only expanded tabs.
  - **Original side and gutter:** side by side, the original editor gets `diffEditor.border` on its right and a mirrored `scrollbar.shadow`, and the gutter menu draws Monaco's top scroll shadow while the modified editor is scrolled down.
- **Code actions and code lenses (#419):** Ported Monaco's code action menu placement, lightbulb position and code lens updates (audit 4 §C.32–§C.35).
  - **The menu after scrolling:** the Cmd/Ctrl+. menu is fixed in the window where its line was first laid out (Monaco `toCoords`), so scrolling the line out of view leaves it showing instead of unplaced while it kept the focus, and Enter applied an action nobody could see. A layout change (a resize, the glyph margin, the minimap, …) closes it (Monaco `onDidLayoutChange`).
  - **The selection's start:** the lightbulb and the Cmd/Ctrl+. menu go at the primary selection's start, not its head, so a selection made downward gets them on its first line (Monaco's `CodeActionsState` position). "No code actions available" and why an action is disabled still show at the cursor.
  - **No glyph margin:** where Monaco would put the lightbulb in the glyph margin (a code line between two code lines), nothing draws when the glyph margin is off, instead of a bulb over the line's first characters.
  - **Lens updates:** new lenses are merged into the shown ones by line (Monaco `_renderCodeLensSymbols`): a line that still has lenses keeps its zone and its titles until the new ones resolve, without blanking and fading in again after every edit. Lines that lost theirs drop their zone, new ones get an empty zone that fades in once resolved, and resolves of a line's old lenses no longer land on its new ones.
- **Marker navigation in files and the marker zone header (#418):** ported Monaco's `NextMarkerInFilesAction` and the zone's `PeekViewWidget` head (audit 4 §C.42, §C.43).
  - **F8 / Shift+F8:** go through the markers of every uri, not only the model's, as Monaco's `editor.action.marker.nextInFiles` does: by uri, then most severe first and by position, and "1 of 5 problems" counts them all. A marker in another uri closes the zone and goes to the host's editor openers with its range (else `OpenLocation`); once an opener has shown that uri in the editor, the zone opens there. Before, they acted as Alt+F8 and Shift+Alt+F8. Switching between the two kinds starts a new list, which moves from the cursor first, as in Monaco. New `CodeEditor::go_to_marker_in_files`.
  - **Zone header:** a 1px line in the frame color between the header and the body, the title 20px in, the severity icon 4px before the file name, "1 of 2 problems" at 0.9em with a 0.5em margin, and the next, previous and close actions with Monaco's action bar padding, 2px from the right. Before, it had no separator, 8px padding, 6px gaps and a 13px count.
- **Provider requests (#417):** Ported Monaco's formatting options, semantic token provider groups, completion range validation and LSP signature help context (audit 4 §G.9–§G.12).
  - **Formatting indent size:** formatters get the model's resolved `indent_size` as `FormattingOptions::tab_size`, as Monaco's `getFormattingOptions` passes `indentSize`. Before, they got the tab size.
  - **Semantic token groups:** document tokens come only from the best-scoring providers (Monaco `_getDocumentSemanticTokensProviderHighestGroup`), and the viewport asks every best-scoring range provider, using the first result with tokens (`getDocumentRangeSemanticTokens`). Before, a `*` provider colored a language whose own provider had no tokens, and the viewport asked only the newest range provider.
  - **Invalid replace ranges:** completion items whose `replace_range` spans lines, isn't on the cursor's line, or doesn't start where the insert range does are skipped, as Monaco's `CompletionItem.isInvalid`. Before, only `range` was checked.
  - **LSP signature help context:** `gpui-monaco-lsp` implements `provide_signature_help_with_context`, sending the editor's `isRetrigger` and `activeSignatureHelp` (`monaco-languageclient`'s `asSignatureHelpContext`). Before, it sent `activeSignatureHelp: null` and derived `isRetrigger` from the character, so a server went back to its first overload.
- **Model language (#409):** a model's language follows Monaco's language service: `plaintext` without one, resolved when the model is created, and a language's id for its name or alias (audit 4 §G.2–§G.4).
  - **`plaintext`:** **Breaking:** once resolved, a model without a language has `language_id()` `Some("plaintext")`, not `None`, as Monaco's `getLanguageId()`, so providers registered for `plaintext` are asked about it, `ProviderContext.language_id` is `plaintext` and `editorLangId == plaintext` holds. `TextModelEvent::LanguageChanged` reports `plaintext` instead of `None` too. Provider selectors score the model's `resource()` (`inmemory://model/N` without a uri, as Monaco's model uri) instead of `uri()`, so `{ scheme: "inmemory" }` filters match it; `ProviderContext.uri` stays the host's uri for LSP bridges.
  - **Resolved at creation:** models resolve their language when created once `init` has run (Monaco's `TextModel` constructor), and in `create_model` and `register_model` before it: the uri guess is made, `language_id()` and `language()` are set and `on_language` callbacks run, so `on_did_create_model` listeners and headless calls such as `api_commands::execute_link_provider` see the language. The new `TextModel::resolve_language` does this; `ensure_syntax` still also starts parsing, which waits until an editor shows the model (Monaco tokenizes only attached models). `create_model` without a uri or language guesses from the first line, as Monaco's `createTextModel` does.
  - **Names and aliases:** a language's name, alias or extension (`"Rust"`, `"RUST"`, `"rs"`) given to `with_language` or `set_language` now gives the model the language's id, `rust`, as a MIME type already did, so `rust` providers, `editorLangId` and `set_language_configuration(cx, "rust", …)` apply. Monaco gives `plaintext` for these; the port keeps accepting them. An unregistered id is still kept, so the model takes the language once it's registered.
  - **Definition hover:** the code block in a Cmd/Ctrl+hover definition preview takes its language from the target's uri, as Monaco's `guessLanguageIdByFilepathOrFirstLine(uri)` does, rather than from the model's language id, so a model without a language still gets an unlabelled fence.
  - **Tests:** `tests/model_language.rs` covers `plaintext` providers, the `inmemory` scheme and `editorLangId`, `create_model` resolving from the uri and first line, `on_language` and `on_did_create_model` at creation, lazy parsing, and names, aliases, extensions and MIME types.
- **Find widget: Enter, multicursor option flashes, closing, Close title and chrome (#408):** the find widget follows Monaco's `findController`, `multicursor` and `findWidget.css` (audit 4 §C.21–§C.25).
  - **Enter in the find input** now runs Find Next (Monaco `NextMatchFindAction`) rather than only moving to the next match: it adds an undo stop, closes the widget on landing on a match with `find.close_on_result` (as Shift+Enter and F3 already did), and with no match restarts find and re-reads "No results found for '…'" to screen readers. Find Next and Previous from F3, Cmd/Ctrl+G and Shift+Enter now re-read it too.
  - **Multicursor sessions flash the find options:** with find closed, Cmd/Ctrl+D (past the word a session starts from), Cmd/Ctrl+K Cmd/Ctrl+D and their previous-match versions, and Select All Occurrences show the match case, whole word and regex toggles at the top right for 2s when the session uses the find options (any session started from an empty cursor, or `selected_text_match_mode: FindOptions`), as Monaco's `_highlightFindOptions` does. With find open Monaco flashes the find input's toggles instead, which isn't ported.
  - **Closing keeps the widget:** closing find no longer destroys the widget. It slides out over 200ms (at once with `App::reduce_motion`), turning back from where it is when reopened mid-slide, and keeps its state, so a width set by dragging the sash survives closing and reopening. Its overlay is removed once it has slid out.
  - **Close tooltip:** the Close button's title is "Close (Escape)". It took its key from the unbound `CloseFindWidget`; it now shows a `closeFindWidget` keybinding rule if one is added, else Escape's binding.
  - **Chrome:** the replace toggle sits 5px from the left edge and 4px from the top and bottom (`.button.left` margins); Close is at 5px from the top and 4px from the right; the widget's shadow is `--vscode-shadow-lg`, as the find options widget's already was, in place of `widget.shadow`; the find in selection toggle has a 5px radius and `toolbar.hoverBackground` on hover, checked or not.
- **Decorations overlay and highlights (#410):** decoration pieces at wrap points, touching decorations, unfocused selection highlights, high contrast borders and the word highlight limit follow Monaco (audit 4 §B.1–§B.4, §D.9).
  - **Wrap points:** decoration ranges map to wrapped lines as Monaco's `convertModelRangeToViewRange` does (start affinity right, end affinity left, right for an empty range). A find match, unicode highlight or diagnostic starting at a wrap point no longer leaves a half-character box or squiggle at the end of the view line above, one ending there none at the start of the line below, and an empty match there is boxed once, on the later line.
  - **Merging:** touching or overlapping decorations of one z-index that paint the same (background, border, outline, squiggle, `show_if_collapsed`, …) merge into one box, as Monaco's `_renderNormalDecorations` merges same-class ones, with the collapsed-range widening applied to the merged range. The cursor between `()` now gets one bracket match box instead of two with a double border between, and adjacent unicode highlights and regex `.` matches box each run.
  - **Unfocused selection highlights:** while the editor isn't focused, selection highlights take `editor.selectionHighlightBackground` at half opacity and no border (Monaco's `.selectionHighlight` outside `.focused`); they still stay up.
  - **High contrast borders:** the current find match has a 2px border (`.currentFindMatch`); the other find matches and the word and selection highlights are dashed in high contrast themes, standing in for Monaco's `dotted` (GPUI has no dotted borders), and the find scope is dashed as in Monaco. These follow the theme when the decorations are made, as their colors already did. The high contrast cursor's sides show while the primary selection isn't empty, not any selection (Monaco's `has-selection`).
  - **Word highlight limit:** textual word highlights stop at 999 occurrences (Monaco's `LIMIT_FIND_COUNT`), so F7 cycles through those, instead of the find widget's 19,999.
  - **Breaking:** `DecorationOptions` has a new public field, `border_width` (`None` is 1px); struct literals need `..Default::default()`.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for pieces at a wrap point, merging, the bracket match box, the unfocused fade, the high contrast find borders and the 999 limit.
- **Peek references (#406):** F4 moves the cursor and the peek view, Enter only moves the view after a Peek command, `findReferences` always peeks, and peeking again at the view closes it, as in Monaco's `ReferencesController` (audit 4 §C.37–§C.41, §D.4).
  - **F4 / Shift+F4:** go from the result nearest the view to the next or previous one, wrapping, and move the cursor and the view there (Monaco `goToNextOrPreviousReference`, `_gotoReference`). The focus goes back to the editor or the preview if one of them had it, else to the list. A result in another model closes the view and goes to the host's editor openers, else `OpenLocation`. Before, F4 only moved the list's selection and the preview. `peek_select_relative` does the same, focusing the list.
  - **Peek mode:** the view remembers whether a Peek command opened it (Monaco `peekMode`, `openInPeek`): Peek Definition and the other Peek commands and `peekLocations`. Only then do Enter and a double click on a result in this model move the view there. After Go to References, the other "Go to" commands, `goToLocations` and `findReferences`, they close it and select the result (Monaco `openReference`). Before, they always moved the view.
  - **Last line:** a peek view under the last line puts it near the top, so the view shows (Monaco `ZoneWidget._showImpl`'s `revealLineNearTop`, as the problem zone already did). Before, the view was added below the viewport.
  - **`editor.action.findReferences`:** opens the peek view at once, titled "Loading..." until the references come, then "No results" in the body without any (Monaco calls `toggleWidget` directly). It's never in peek mode and runs no alternative command. Before, it went through navigation: a "No references found" message, or the alternative for one result.
  - **Toggling:** a Peek command, Go to command or `findReferences` showing a peek view while the selection contains the open view's position closes it instead (Monaco `toggleWidget`). The view now opens under the selection's start, not the cursor.
  - **Opening a result:** a click on the title selects the whole result, as Monaco's `openReference` passes its range; Enter outside peek mode too. A double click in the preview goes to the clicked place, not the selected result (Monaco passes the mouse target's range; the port's is the clicked position, so it's a caret). With Ctrl, Cmd or Alt it opens to the side even with `stable_peek`, as in Monaco. Before, both left a caret at the result's start.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for F4 from the editor and the list (cursor, zone, focus, wrapping, another model), peek mode for Enter and double clicks, the last-line reveal, `findReferences` loading, empty and with results, toggling, the title click and the preview double click. Monaco has no tests for the references controller.
- **Multi diff editor view state, per-file editor options and list layout (#407):** `MultiDiffEditor` ports more of Monaco's `MultiDiffEditorWidget` (audit 4 §E.21–§E.24).
  - **View state before or with the files:** `restore_view_state` keeps the files' states and the active file for files not listed yet (Monaco `_lastDocStates` and `_lastActiveDiffItemKey`). After `set_documents_loading`, or in the same update as `set_documents`, the restored collapsed states, selections, scroll position and active file now win over going to the first change. The active file gets the focus unless `set_preserve_focus_on_load(true)` (Monaco `_restorePendingActiveDiffItem`). Files that show up later still take their state, until the new `clear_pending_restoration_state` (Monaco `clearPendingRestorationState`). Unlike Monaco, a restored active file that's off screen gets an editor so it can take the focus, and a collapsed one isn't focused, since collapsed files have no editor (§E.25).
  - **Per-file editor options:** `MultiDiffDocument::editor_options` (`MultiDiffDocumentEditorOptions`: `read_only` and `accessibility_support`) carries the editor options of Monaco's `IDocumentDiffItem.options`. Each time a file gets an editor it applies them, and options it doesn't set go back to the defaults, so a read-only file stays read-only when scrolled back into view and doesn't pass it on to the file that reuses its editor. Change them with `set_document_editor_options`. Other editor options aren't covered yet. **Breaking:** `MultiDiffDocument` has a new field, so struct literals need `..Default::default()`.
  - **List-wide layout:** `set_render_side_by_side`, `toggle_render_side_by_side`, `set_view_mode` (`DiffEditorViewMode`), `set_diff_layout_options` and `set_diff_word_wrap` set a layout (`MultiDiffLayoutOptions`, from `diff_layout_options`) that goes over every file's options, its own ones included (Monaco `_diffLayoutOptions`). The new `MultiDiffEditor::context_keys` adds `multiDiffEditorRenderSideBySide` and `diffEditorAutomaticRenderSideBySide` (from the new `DiffEditor::renders_side_by_side_in_automatic_mode`) to `inMultiDiffEditor` and `multiDiffEditorAllCollapsed`. The port's diff editor has no temporary inline view during a resize, so switching to automatic has nothing to reset (Monaco `resetWidthBasedLayout`).
  - **Kept files with new models:** a file kept by its key whose models changed keeps its collapsed state and selections but no longer gets its old view state (revealed regions, scroll) back on the new text. In Monaco, a new document object gets a fresh view model.
  - **Tests:** Monaco's "applies document and responsive layout options before attaching the diff model" is ported, with tests for each item.
- **Focused widgets keep the text's keys out (#400):** a focused widget draws inside the editor's key context, and GPUI matches a binding at any depth, so Enter in a focused hover split the line, and Backspace, Delete, Cmd/Ctrl+V, Cmd/Ctrl+X and Tab edited the document from the hover, find widget buttons, context and code action menus, the peek list, the marker zone and the color picker (audit 4 §C.1).
  - **`editorTextFocus`:** as in Monaco, the default `CodeEditor` bindings for the text's own commands (typing, deleting, moving, clipboard, Tab, folding, …), the suggest widget's keys and the Tab rules now need `editorTextFocus`. The `editorFocus` commands (find and its toggles, F3, Cmd/Ctrl+D, Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+L, F8, the quick inputs), Escape's and Shift+Escape's chains, Toggle Tab Key Moves Focus and Cmd/Ctrl+K F2 still apply from widgets; Undo, Redo and Select All focus the editor first, as Monaco's `EditorOrNativeTextInputCommand` does. Escape from a widget no longer removes the secondary cursors or the selection. The find, rename and quick input boxes are editors with the text focus, so their keys are unchanged. **Breaking** for hosts that rebound a default key in `CodeEditor` expecting it to also act from a focused widget: give such bindings `editorTextFocus`, or not, as needed.
  - **Tab in widgets:** Tab and Shift+Tab from a widget are `FocusNextElement` / `FocusPreviousElement`, the browser's tab order: the find widget's buttons and toggles keep cycling in read-only editors and tab focus mode, where Tab used to leave the widget (§C.3).
  - **Hover buttons (§C.2):** the copy buttons and the status bar's actions ("View Problem", "Quick Fix...", AI fixes) are tab stops after their row, Enter or Space press them, and a click (not a mouse down) runs them, as Monaco's `SimpleButton` and `HoverAction`. Markdown links in hovers still aren't tab stops: they're runs of text in GPUI, not focusable elements.
  - **Labels:** keybinding labels for the text's commands (the hover's Quick Fix, the context menu, the code action menu's Organize Imports) still show while the widget has the focus, as Monaco looks them up without the focus keys.
- **Hover computing (#405):** the hover streams its parts and shows "Loading...", stays away while a context menu shows, keeps glyph hovers current, and draws its separators and default ranges as Monaco does (audit 4 §C.4–§C.8, §G.13).
  - **Streaming:** as in Monaco's `HoverOperation`, the hover providers and the color picker are asked at half `hover.delay`, the markers, decoration messages, long line warnings, unicode parts and inline suggestion toolbar show at the delay, and each provider's part is added as it answers instead of after all of them. A mouse hover still waiting at three times the delay shows "Loading..." last until the providers are done; the keyboard's Show Hover streams without it. An adopted hover (the mouse moved along its line) stays until the new hover is complete.
  - **Part order:** markdown parts follow Monaco's `MarkdownHover.ordinal`: the providers' parts in provider order, then the long line warnings and decoration messages, which used to come first.
  - **Context menu:** showing a context menu (the editor's, the sticky scroll's, the inlay hint location menu) hides the hover, and mouse moves, downs, leaves and scrolls don't touch it until the menu closes (Monaco `onDidShowContextMenu`, `_ignoreMouseEvents`). The inline suggestion toolbar's "⋯" menu keeps the hover it's in. Keys aren't ignored meanwhile, and the glyph hover is ignored too (Monaco's separate glyph controller doesn't listen for context menus).
  - **Glyph hovers:** a visible glyph margin or line number hover is computed again after the hover delay when decorations change, and closes if nothing is left (Monaco `_onModelDecorationsChanged`). Moving to another row or lane hides it at once, without the content hover's `hiding_delay` grace period.
  - **Separators:** hover row separators and markdown rules use `editorHoverWidget.border` at 50% alpha (Monaco `hoverContribution.ts`), not the full color and `textSeparator.foreground`. The verbosity column keeps the full border color, as Monaco's `hover.css` has it.
  - **Default range:** a provider hover without a `range` is about the word at the position (else the empty range there), as Monaco's standalone `registerHoverProvider` sets it, so the word gets `editor.hoverHighlightBackground`.
  - **Docs:** the book's providers and decorations pages and the parity doc.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for streamed parts and "Loading..." timing, keyboard streaming without it, no hovers under the context menu, the default range's highlight, glyph hovers following decoration changes and moving between glyph rows; `long_line_warnings` expects Monaco's part order, and `hovers_without_a_range_highlight_nothing` is now `hovers_without_a_range_highlight_the_word`.
- **Diff editor hidden unchanged regions (#404):** four fixes to how the diff editor hides unchanged regions (audit 4 §E.5–§E.8).
  - **Debug panic after edits:** an edit that collapsed two regions onto the same lines (say, replacing everything from a line to the end) could build an inverted line range on the next diff and panic in debug builds. The port now drops a tracked region that overlaps the one before it (Monaco's `filterWithPrevious`), and drops a region whose clipped range is empty instead of moving it to line 1 (`LineRangeMapping.clip`).
  - **New regions after a diff that hid nothing:** once a diff has shown everything, new regions now start shown, as in Monaco. Only the first diff's regions start collapsed. Before, reverting changes that had split the file into short gaps (or filling empty models after creating the editor) collapsed the lines you were reading. When nothing changed, the whole-file region now stays in the state, so what the cursor reveals in it carries over, but it gets no bar, glyph or tint.
  - **Inline view:** the original column's bars show only their background and edges (Monaco `_hide`), without a second unfold button and label in the line-number column. With `compact_mode` they're empty.
  - **`compact_mode` fold glyph:** fully shown regions get the "Fold Unchanged Region" glyph with `compact_mode` too. Monaco skips it only in its multi diff `compact` variant.
- **Accessible diff viewer for screen readers (#403):** the viewer now speaks, keeps its place and follows the models, as Monaco's does (audit 4 §E.1–§E.3).
  - **Screen readers read it:** focus now goes to the viewer's line list (`Role::List`), not to its root, which had no role. GPUI only builds an accessibility node, and so only reports focus, for an element with a role, so screen readers used to read nothing. The current row is the list's active descendant and is `aria_selected`, the way Monaco focuses the row itself, so F7 reads the change header and each Up and Down reads the new line (for example "+ foo modified line 5"). GPUI's test platform builds no accessibility tree, so the tests check the viewer's roles, labels and selection through a hidden `accessible_viewer_aria_for_tests` that uses the same labels as rendering.
  - **State across re-applies and model changes:** the groups are rebuilt only for a new diff, not for every re-apply (theme, options, layout switch, the end of a moved-block compare), and the current line's index is kept and clamped. Previously every re-apply moved the viewer to the group after the cursor and back to its header. New models empty the list until their diff comes, and `set_model(None)` closes the viewer, as Monaco's `setModel(null)` does.
  - **`diffEditorAutomaticRenderSideBySide` with a screen reader:** the key is now set at any width when a screen reader is in use (Monaco `renderSideBySideInAutomaticMode`). The side-by-side layout already ignored `use_inline_view_when_space_is_limited` with a screen reader, so the key now matches it.
- **Go to Definition and References (#401):** symbol navigation follows Monaco's `gotoSymbol` (audit 4 §D.1–§D.3, §G.1).
  - **At the cursor:** "already at the only result", which runs the alternative command, tests a `LocationLink`'s `target_selection_range` (else its range), as Monaco's `referenceAt` on `OneReference.range` does. F12 on a recursive call inside a function returned as a link now goes to the function's name instead of opening a references peek. F12 stepping through `Goto` results and the peek view's nearest result use the same range, as do the peek's Enter and close jumps.
  - **Compact references:** Go to References asks a reference provider that finds exactly two references again with `include_declaration: false`, and uses that answer if it has one result (Monaco's `compact` `getReferencesAtPosition`). Shift+F12 on a variable used once now jumps to the use. Peek References isn't compact.
  - **Alternative commands:** **Breaking:** `GotoLocationOptions::alternative_*_command` and `alternative()` take a new `NavigationCommand` (Monaco's command ids: `GoToReferences`, `PeekReferences`, `RevealDefinitionAside`, …; `id()` and `from_id()` convert) instead of a `LocationKind`; replace `Some(LocationKind::References)` with `Some(NavigationCommand::GoToReferences)`. The alternative runs with its own configuration, so Peek Definition on a definition with one reference no longer opens a one-item peek, and a muted Cmd/Ctrl+click's alternative shows "No references found". As in Monaco, a command whose alternative led there can't run as an alternative again, so chains of alternatives end.
  - **Reveal:** jumps within the editor's model (Go to Definition and the others, F12 stepping, and peek Enter and close) center the target at once, as standalone Monaco's `openCodeEditor` does; before, they revealed it near the top only when it was outside the viewport, smoothly with `smooth_scrolling`.
- **Ported test suites (#357):** Monaco's `cursorMoveCommand.test.ts`, `linkedEditing.test.ts` and `snippetVariables.test.ts` are ported in full (audit 3 §A.32). No behaviour changes were needed.
  - **`cursorMoveCommand.test.ts`:** the 46 cases #333 hadn't ported (character, half-line, line position, model-line, wrapped-line, blank-line and `foldedLine` moves) run the `cursorMove` command in a GPUI editor (`tests/cursor_move_command.rs`); the nine viewport cases were already unit tests.
  - **`linkedEditing.test.ts`:** all 38 cases run with Monaco's word-matching provider and word patterns (`tests/linked_editing.rs`). "Selection insert - across boundary" is ignored: Monaco's `nodeAcceptEdit` keeps a decoration edge inside a replaced span in place for the length of the shorter text, but the port moves it to the start of the new text, so the typed character joins the linked range.
  - **`snippetVariables.test.ts`:** the 16 cases #351 hadn't ported run as unit tests (`src/snippet/variables_tests.rs`), with "editor variables, selection" also in an editor (`tests/snippet.rs`). The label service and workspace mocks are replaced by the context's path and `SnippetWorkspace`.
  - **Integration tests:** typing without a language, word moves under wrap, reveals after adding cursors, PageDown scrolling and column select across folds were already covered by #326, #333, #323 and #334.
- **Input (#356):** trackpad pinch zoom and press-and-hold accents at every cursor, as in Monaco.
  - **Pinch zoom:** with `mouse_wheel_zoom`, a trackpad pinch zooms every editor's font (Monaco's `hasMouseWheelZoomModifiers` accepts the Ctrl+wheel browsers send for a pinch). Each GPUI `PinchEvent` counts as one wheel notch of the trackpad gesture, a zoom level per five, like Chromium's synthetic pinch wheel; Shift or Alt held stops it. Before, pinching did nothing.
  - **Press-and-hold at every cursor:** replacing text around the primary cursor without a composition (picking `é` from the macOS accent menu after holding `e`, or autocorrect) replaces it at every cursor, as Monaco's `compositionType` with `replacePrevCharCnt` does. Before, only the primary cursor's character changed.
  - **Touch docs:** the book's differences page says touch isn't handled (GPUI 0.3.8 doesn't dispatch `TouchEvent` yet).
- **Diff editor polish (#354):** the diff editor has Monaco's context keys and content size event, and its keys, focus, overview slider, high contrast borders and options follow Monaco.
  - **Context keys:** `DiffEditor::context_keys()` returns Monaco's diff editor keys, which GPUI sets on the diff editor's element and both editors inherit, as Monaco's editors inherit them from the diff editor's scoped context key service: `isInDiffEditor`, `inDiffEditor`, `isInDiffLeftEditor`/`isInDiffRightEditor`, `isEmbeddedDiffEditor`, `comparingMovedCode`, `diffEditorRenderSideBySideInlineBreakpointReached`, `diffEditorInlineMode`, `diffEditorAutomaticRenderSideBySide`, `diffEditorHasChanges`, `diffEditorOriginalWritable`, `diffEditorModifiedWritable` and the `diffEditorOriginalUri`/`diffEditorModifiedUri` strings, plus `inMultiDiffEditor` and `multiDiffEditorAllCollapsed` in a `MultiDiffEditor` (also on its element). So `add_command("…", h, Some("isInDiffEditor && diffEditorHasChanges"))` now fires. Before, only `DiffEditor`, `comparingMovedCode` and `isEmbeddedDiffEditor` were set, and only on the diff editor's element.
  - **Breaking:** Alt+F5 and Shift+Alt+F5 are no longer bound to `GoToNextDiff`/`GoToPreviousDiff`: they're VS Code's workbench keys, and standalone Monaco binds no key to `goToDiff`. Bind the actions to keep them.
  - **Breaking:** `go_to_diff(next, cx)` takes no window and leaves the focus where it is, as Monaco's `goToDiff` does, instead of focusing the modified editor.
  - **Content size event:** `DiffEditorEvent::ContentSizeChanged(ContentSizeChange)` fires when either editor's content size changes (Monaco `onDidContentSizeChange`), with `content_height()` (the taller side) and the new `content_width()` (both editors' content widths and the 30px overview ruler), so an auto-height diff editor can follow its content. **Breaking:** a new `DiffEditorEvent` variant; `ContentSizeChange` is now `Eq`.
  - **Overview slider:** the diff overview ruler's slider takes `scrollbarSlider.hoverBackground` under the mouse (Monaco's `.diffViewport:hover`).
  - **High contrast borders:** changed lines get `diffEditor.insertedTextBorder`/`removedTextBorder` around the whole line, as changed characters already did, and in high contrast themes both are dashed (Monaco's `.line-insert`, `.char-insert`, … in `style.css`). The decorations also follow theme changes. **Breaking:** `DecorationOptions` has a `border_style` field (`gpui::BorderStyle`, solid by default).
  - **`split_view_default_ratio`:** clamped to 0.1–0.9 (0.5 for NaN), as Monaco validates it, so a ratio of 0.98 no longer leaves the modified side 100px wide.
  - **`max_file_size`:** still skips the diff for larger models, as Monaco documents the option and its earlier diff widget did; Monaco's current widget only validates it, which the docs now say (set 0 to match).
  - **Docs:** the book's diff editor and differences pages, the `GoToNextDiff`/`GoToPreviousDiff` docs and the parity doc.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the context keys (focus, options, inline, read-only, no changes), `add_command`'s `when` over them, the multi diff editor's keys, `go_to_diff` keeping the focus and Alt+F5 being unbound, the content size event, the high contrast borders and the ratio clamping. Monaco has no widget tests for these.
- **Model API (#353):** markdown links go through the link openers, model uris are compared normalized, and the model's edit, range, word and language helpers follow Monaco's `ITextModel`.
  - **Markdown links:** a link clicked in a hover, suggest details, parameter hints or a message is offered to the `register_link_opener` openers first, as Monaco's opener service does, so the host sees `file:`, custom-scheme and untrusted `command:` links. One no opener takes still opens in the browser (`http`, `https`, `mailto`) or runs (a trusted string's `command:`); others are dropped, and `internal:` links aren't offered.
  - **Uris:** the new `normalize_uri` is Monaco's `URI.parse(s).toString()` (`file:///a b` is `file:///a%20b`, `file:///C:/x` is `file:///c%3A/x`). `model_for_uri`, `create_model`'s taken-uri check, `MarkerFilter::resource` and same-model checks in navigation compare uris in that form. `TextModel::resource()`, and so `ModelMarker::resource` and the `on_did_change_markers` uris, are now the normalized uri; `uri()` stays as given.
  - **Breaking:** `TextModel::push_edit_operations` takes `Vec<SingleEditOperation>` (Monaco `IIdentifiedSingleEditOperation`), so edits can `force_move_markers`; a `(range, text)` pair converts with `.into()`.
  - **`apply_edits`:** the undo edits come back in the order of the edits, not by position (Monaco issue #46342).
  - **`validate_range`:** a range whose end is inside a surrogate pair grows to take the character, and an empty range inside one moves before it, as Monaco's does, instead of shrinking. `value_in_range`, `apply_edits`, decorations at points and the scoped searches use it.
  - **`word_at_position`:** uses the word pattern of the language at the position (an embedded one's, from tree-sitter injections or Monarch `nextEmbedded`) within that language's part of the line, and at a language boundary also the word ending there, as Monaco's tokenization part does. `word_until_position` follows.
  - **New helpers:** `is_valid_range`, `line_min_column`, `line_max_column` and `language_id_at_position` (Monaco `isValidRange`, `getLineMinColumn`, `getLineMaxColumn`, `getLanguageIdAtPosition`).
  - **Docs:** the book's model page, `migrating.md` rows and the parity doc.
  - **Tests:** Monaco's `uri.test.ts` normalization cases, `textModel.test.ts` `validateRange` around surrogate pairs, `model.test.ts` "Get word at position", "getWordAtPosition at embedded language boundaries" and issue #46342 as unit and GPUI tests, plus GPUI tests for markdown links through openers, `pushEditOperations` with `forceMoveMarkers`, `language_id_at_position` and normalized model and marker lookups.
- **View lines (#327):** tab stops, the scroll width under word wrap and the indent guides on wrapped lines follow Monaco's view lines.
  - **Tabs after wide characters:** a full-width character (CJK) counts two visible columns and other characters one per UTF-16 unit (an astral emoji two), as in Monaco's `_renderLine`, so a tab after `中` or `😀` stops at the next tab stop instead of one column past it. Injected text counts the same way.
  - **Word wrap:** wrapping at the viewport (`On` or `Bounded`), the content is as wide as the widest line, without `scroll_beyond_last_column` or the view zones' and overlay widgets' min widths (Monaco `ViewLayout._computeContentWidth`), so End, the horizontal reveal and a sideways wheel no longer scroll wrapped text; `content_width()` reports that width. `WordWrapColumn` keeps `scroll_beyond_last_column`, and its horizontal scrollbar shows when the column doesn't fit.
  - **Indent guides:** a wrapped row's continuation lines have no indent guides when they start at column 0 (`wrapping_indent: None`), as Monaco's `getViewLinesIndentGuides` blocks them (`BlockSubsequent`, or `BlockAll` when the row's first line is scrolled off).
  - **Docs:** the book's options page and the parity doc.
  - **Tests:** `ViewLines::indent_guides` unit tests, including Monaco's "Invalid line numbers" guides; GPUI tests for tabs after wide characters (with Monaco's `issue-22832-1`) and for wrapped text not scrolling horizontally.
- **Editor API (#349):** cursor change sources, decoration events, ids, action descriptors and the missing editor events follow Monaco's `ICodeEditor`, `ITextModel` and `IActionDescriptor`.
  - **Cursor change sources:** `CursorChangeSource` is Monaco's free-form `source` string, with an `Other(SharedString)` variant for any name that isn't one of Monaco's, so a host can tag its own changes and ignore them (`executeEdits('my-ext', …)` then `e.source === 'my-ext'`). `set_position_from`, `set_selection_from`, `set_selections_from`, `execute_edits_from`, `execute_edit_operations_from` and `trigger_from` take a source (the versions without one report `"api"`, and `trigger` `"keyboard"`, as in Monaco). `execute_command(s)` reports the source given, and no source as the keyboard (Monaco `source || 'keyboard'`). Before, every source but `"keyboard"` and `"mouse"` was reported as the API's. The editor's own edits pass Monaco's sources: `"formatEditsCommand"`, `"diffEditor"`, `"inlayHint.default"`, `"colorpicker"`, `"editor.contrib.dragAndDrop"` and the indentation actions' ids.
  - **Decoration events:** edits, undo and redo fire `TextModelEvent::DecorationsChanged` (and `ModelDecorationsChanged`, `DecorationsCollection::on_did_change`) while the model has decorations, flagging the minimap, overview ruler and glyph margin, as Monaco's `_doApplyEdits` does. Before, a list built from a collection's ranges went stale while typing. An undecorated model fires nothing, so typing costs nothing more. A `delta_decorations` that removes and adds nothing (including stale ids, `remove_decorations` and an empty collection's `clear`) fires nothing, as Monaco's `deltaDecorations([], [])`; before, a listener re-applying decorations on the event looped.
  - **Line heights and fonts:** `TextModelEvent::LineHeightChanged` and `FontChanged`, and `CodeEditorEvent::LineHeightChanged` and `FontChanged` for the decorations an editor shows (Monaco `onDidChangeLineHeight` and `onDidChangeFont`), report decorations with a `line_height` or a font being added, changed and removed.
  - **Decoration options and getters:** `lines_decorations_tooltip` (Monaco `linesDecorationsTooltip`) shows a tooltip over the decoration's bar or text in the line decorations area. `BlockDecoration` has `is_after_end` and `does_not_collapse` (Monaco `blockIsAfterEnd`, `blockDoesNotCollapse`), and an empty range's block covers only the view zones above its line, as Monaco's does. `text_direction` (Monaco `textDirection`) is kept, though GPUI can't lay out right-to-left lines. The getters take a `DecorationFilter` with Monaco's `filterFontDecorations`, `onlyMinimapDecorations` and `onlyMarginDecorations` (a `bool` still means `filterOutValidation`), and `TextModel::custom_line_heights_decorations(_in_range)` are Monaco's `getCustomLineHeightsDecorations(InRange)`.
  - **Ids:** `CodeEditor::id` (`vs.editor.ICodeEditor:N`), `DiffEditor::id` (`vs.editor.IDiffEditor:v2:N`), `editor_type` with `EditorType`, and `TextModel::id` (`$modelN`, the number of its `inmemory://model/N` uri), as Monaco's `getId`, `getEditorType` and `ITextModel.id`.
  - **Actions:** `EditorActionDescriptor` has `keybinding_context` (Monaco `keybindingContext`) and `new_with_args`, whose body gets Monaco's run argument: `trigger_with_payload`'s payload, a keybinding rule's `command_args` (before, a rule with arguments skipped host actions) or `run_action_with_args`'s. `get_action(id)` returns an `EditorAction` with its names, `is_supported` and `run(args)`, and `actions()` lists every action (Monaco `getAction`, `getActions`). Keybindings that don't parse are skipped instead of panicking.
  - **Events:** `ModelLanguageChanged` and `TextModelEvent::LanguageChanged` have Monaco's `source` (`TextModel::set_language_from`, else `"api"`). New `CodeEditorEvent`s: `DropIntoEditor` (Monaco `onDropIntoEditor`), `WillTriggerEditorOperation` (`onWillTriggerEditorOperationEvent`), and `MouseLeave` carries the mouse position and modifiers (`IPartialEditorMouseEvent`).
  - **Breaking:**
    - `CursorChangeSource` has an `Other(SharedString)` variant and is no longer `Copy`; clone it, and add an arm for `Other` to exhaustive matches.
    - `execute_command(s)` with `None` reports `CursorChangeSource::Keyboard`, and with a name other than Monaco's `CursorChangeSource::Other`, where both were `Api`; pass `Some("api")` for the old report.
    - `ActionRun` takes the run argument (`Option<serde_json::Value>`) after the editor; `EditorActionDescriptor::new` still takes a body without it. The descriptor has a new public field, `keybinding_context`.
    - `BlockDecoration` has new public fields (`is_after_end`, `does_not_collapse`) and derives `Default`; add `..Default::default()` to struct literals. An empty range's block no longer covers its line; set `does_not_collapse` for that.
    - `DecorationOptions` has new public fields, `lines_decorations_tooltip` and `text_direction`.
    - `TextModelEvent::LanguageChanged` and `CodeEditorEvent::ModelLanguageChanged` have a `source` field; add `..` to patterns. `CodeEditorEvent::MouseLeave` carries a `PartialEditorMouseEvent`; match `MouseLeave(_)`.
    - `TextModelEvent` has new variants `LineHeightChanged` and `FontChanged`, and `CodeEditorEvent` `LineHeightChanged`, `FontChanged`, `DropIntoEditor` and `WillTriggerEditorOperation`.
    - The decoration getters' last parameter is `impl Into<DecorationFilter>`; `true`/`false` still work.
- **Options (#348):** the defaults are standalone Monaco's, the computed options are public, and option, theme and doc details follow Monaco.
  - **Breaking:** `EditorOptions::default()` has standalone Monaco's overrides (`editor.api.ts`) instead of VS Code's core defaults: `glyph_margin` is `false` (was `true`), `overview_ruler_lanes` 2 (was 3), `wrapping_indent` `WrappingIndent::None` (was `Same`) and `auto_indent` `AutoIndent::Advanced` (was `Full`, so Enter, paste with `auto_indent_on_paste` and moving lines no longer apply the language's indentation rules by default). Set them to keep the old look; the playground keeps its glyph margin for its breakpoints and demo glyphs.
  - **Breaking:** the default theme is `vs`, Monaco's (was `vs-dark`): for editors without a theme set with `set_theme`, `EditorColors::default()`, the find widget's inputs, the multi diff editor's fallback colors and the high contrast switch.
  - **Computed options:** `CodeEditor::computed_options()` returns what the editor runs with (Monaco `getOptions` and `getOption`): validated, zoomed, with `font_size` and `line_height` in pixels and `accessibility_support` resolved to `On` or `Off` (and, optimized for screen readers, `wrapping_strategy: Advanced` and `wrapping_indent: None`). `ConfigurationChange::old_options` and `new_options` are these (**Breaking:** `line_height` is now always `Some`, in pixels).
  - **`occurrencesHighlight: MultiFile`:** with another editor showing a different model, the word's textual occurrences are highlighted in every model and every editor showing one of them shows its model's (Monaco's `WordHighlighter` with standalone's textual multi-document provider); typing, moving off the word or changing the option stops them everywhere. Before, it acted like `SingleFile`. Changing `occurrences_highlight` restarts the highlights.
  - **`minimap.mark_section_header_regex`:** one that doesn't compile keeps the default, as Monaco's `EditorMinimap.validate` does, instead of turning `MARK:` headers off.
  - **Theme colors:** a theme color that isn't hex (`#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa`), in `Theme::define` or a VS Code theme, is red, as Monaco's `Color.fromHex` makes it, instead of being dropped.
  - **Docs:** the book's options page (defaults, computed options, `MultiFile`, regex validation), themes page (default theme), the `migrating.md` rows for `setModelLanguage`, `addKeybindingRule(s)`, `getOptions`, `getRawOptions`, `getOption`, `getAction`, `getId`/`getEditorType` and `getLineHeightForPosition`, and the `content_height` docs, which now say that a shown horizontal scrollbar counts without `scroll_beyond_last_line`.
  - **Tests:** unit tests for the defaults, regex validation and invalid theme colors; GPUI tests for `computed_options` (raw, validated, zoomed, screen reader, `ConfigurationChanged`), `MultiFile` across editors and the invalid regex; tests written for VS Code's defaults now set them.
- **Scroll commands (#355):** the scroll commands and smooth scrolling follow Monaco's `EditorScroll`, `RevealLine` and `Scrollable`.
  - **Breaking:** during a smooth scroll, `scroll_top()`, `scroll_left()` and `scroll_position()` return where the scroll is now (Monaco `getScrollTop`), and `ScrollChanged` fires at each step with that position, instead of reporting the destination once. A host mirroring the scroll, such as the diff editor's other side, now follows the animation. The new `future_scroll_position()` is the destination (Monaco `getFutureScrollPosition`); the reveals, the scroll-by-line command, view state and the multi-diff editor's cursor following use it.
  - **`scrollEditorBottom`:** puts line `lineCount - pageSize` at the top, as Monaco does, so the last line ends a page down instead of at the top of an almost empty viewport.
  - **Smooth commands:** `scrollLineUp`/`Down`, `scrollPageUp`/`Down`, `scrollEditorTop`/`Bottom`, `scrollLeft`/`Right` and every `editorScroll` unit animate with `smoothScrolling` on (`ScrollType.Smooth`), and the line, page and column steps count from where the scroll is now, as Monaco's `getCurrentScrollTop() + delta` does.
  - **`revealLine`:** the command scrolls only vertically (`revealHorizontal = false`) instead of also scrolling left to the line's start.
  - **Long smooth scrolls:** past 2.5 viewports they ease through the first 0.75 viewport in the first third of the animation and in from 0.75 viewport before the end, skipping the middle (`createComposed`), and every smooth scroll starts 10ms in, as Monaco's `SmoothScrollingOperation.start` does.
  - **Timing:** smooth scrolls run on the executor's clock and tick on its timers between frames, so tests can drive them with `advance_clock`.
  - **Tests:** the composed animation, its start and retargeting as unit tests (Monaco has no `scrollable.test.ts` for them), and GPUI tests for each command, the per-step events and the composed animation.
- **Accessibility (#347):** the screen reader content is windowed like Monaco's `SimplePagedScreenReaderStrategy`, and the missing announcements are made, polite for Monaco's `status` and assertive for its `alert`.
  - **Screen reader content:** the AccessKit text runs are the `accessibility_page_size` page holding the selection's start and the page holding its end, with a `…` run for the pages between, so Select All in a 100k-line file reads two pages instead of putting every line in the tree. Pages are view lines: folded lines are left out and each wrapped segment is a line. The content is kept until the view lines, the selection or the page size change, instead of being rebuilt every frame.
  - **Polite status messages:** `CodeEditor::announce_status` announces in a polite live region (role `Status`); `announce` stays assertive. The marker zone, added and removed cursors, suggest's "Loading..." and "No suggestions.", and code action context are now polite, as Monaco's `status`. Live regions also set their value, which AccessKit's macOS adapter announces.
  - **Navigation results:** Go to Definition and the other navigation commands (and `editor.action.goToLocations`) alert Monaco's `ariaMessage`: "Found 3 symbols in 2 files", "Found 1 symbol in /src/a.rs", "No results found".
  - **Other announcements:** "Line {n}, column {c}: {text}" after quick access goes to a line or symbol, "Anchor set at {line}:{column}", "Accepting '{label}' made {n} additional edits", "Copied to clipboard" from the hover copy button, and "Received {n} rename suggestions".
  - **Docs:** the book's differences and options pages, and the parity doc.
  - **Tests:** Monaco's `textAreaState.test.ts` paged strategy cases, plus GPUI tests for Select All, folded and wrapped view lines, and each announcement and its politeness.
- **Diff editor unchanged regions (#352):** hidden unchanged regions merge, drag and keep the scroll position like Monaco's (audit 3 §E.7–§E.10).
  - **Merging:** regions whose hidden lines touch become one region with one "N hidden lines" bar, as Monaco's "collapse touching unchanged ranges" does. Before, reverting a change and then collapsing all regions could stack two bars.
  - **Dragging:** a bar stays while its edge is dragged, even once every line shows, and goes when the mouse is released (Monaco `shouldHideControls` with `isDragged`).
  - **Scroll anchor:** showing or hiding lines from a bar's bottom edge keeps the code below in place by adding up the view lines shown, so wrapped lines count each part (Monaco `_runWithLowerScrollAnchor`), and a click that shows the rest also accounts for the bar going away.
  - **Bars between changes:** a bar whose next line is hidden shows again (Monaco's `PlaceholderViewZone` has `showInHiddenAreas`); only the first bar showed. `MultiDiffEditor::reveal_range` and the other line reveals wait for the hidden lines to lay out, so they center the right line now that those bars take room.
  - **Bar edges:** the top and bottom edges are Monaco's 4px bands straddling the bar's top and bottom, with a 10px hit area reaching past the bar, and the dragged edge stays highlighted for the whole drag.
- **Go to definition and navigation (#350):** link clicks, the definition link, F12 through results and the selection anchor follow Monaco (audit 3 §H.8, §D.33–§D.35).
  - **Clicks on mouse-up:** Cmd/Ctrl+click on a link, a symbol or an inlay hint part, and the middle click with `mouse_middle_click_action` `OpenLink` or `CtrlLeftClick`, act when the button comes up on the line it went down on, with the modifier held since the press (Monaco's `ClickLinkGesture`). Before, they acted on mouse-down and skipped the click. Now the press places the cursor (or column-selects for the middle button) as usual, so Cmd/Ctrl+drag selects, and a drag, a selection, a scroll, an edit or a new model in between cancels the gesture and removes the Cmd/Ctrl+hover links. A Cmd/Ctrl+click goes to the definition whenever a definition provider applies, even before the link shows.
  - **Link color:** the Cmd/Ctrl+hover definition link is underlined in `editorLink.activeForeground` (#4E94CE in the dark theme), not `inputOption.activeBorder`.
  - **F12 through results:** stepping through `Goto` results (`gotoNextSymbolFromResult`) no longer highlights each one, as Monaco's `SymbolNavigationService.revealNext` doesn't.
  - **Selection anchor:** Cmd+K Cmd+B marks the position with Monaco's 2px `#007ACC` bar on an empty range, with a "Selection Anchor" hover, instead of a box around the next character.
- **Cursor movement (#333):** word moves, edits made elsewhere, `stickyTabStops` and `cursorMove`'s viewport targets work as in Monaco.
  - **Word moves:** the word and word part moves (`cursorWordLeft` and the rest, Alt/Ctrl+Left/Right with and without Shift) and `cursorTop`/`cursorBottom` run on model lines, as Monaco's do. With word wrap they went by wrapped segments, so from the start of a continuation they only reached its wrap indent (the cursor didn't move, and selecting selected nothing), and they stopped at wrap points.
  - **Edits from elsewhere:** an editor's cursors follow edits it didn't make (`TextModel::apply_edits`, another editor on the same model, undo in another editor) as Monaco's tracked ranges do (`AlwaysGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges`, `readSelectionFromMarkers`): text inserted at a cursor goes before it, a selection grows with text typed at its edges, and a replaced range keeps the cursors in it where Monaco does. Before, an insert at a cursor left it in front of the text. Monaco's `sideEditing.test.ts` is ported.
  - **`forceMoveMarkers`:** `TextModel::apply_edit_operations` applies `SingleEditOperation`s with their `force_move_markers` (Monaco `applyEdits`), moving decorations and the cursors of every editor showing the model past a forced edit's text.
  - **Sticky tab stops:** with `sticky_tab_stops`, a click or drag in soft-tab indentation lands on the nearest tab stop, and the mouse target's position and range say so (Monaco `_snapToSoftTabBoundary`).
  - **Viewport targets:** `cursorMove` to `viewPortTop`, `viewPortBottom`, `viewPortCenter` and `viewPortIfOutside` count only the lines that show in full (`getCompletelyVisibleViewRange`), skip a first line whose start is wrapped off screen, and `viewPortIfOutside` moves by view lines, keeping the visible column.
  - **Breaking:** `TextModelEvent::Edited` has a `force_move_markers` field, the old ranges of the edits made with `forceMoveMarkers`.
- **Clipboard, format and snippet edits (#351):** block paste, copies, format edits, workspace edits and snippet insertion follow Monaco's `ColumnSelectionPasteCommand`, `getPlainTextToCopy`, `FormattingEdit`, `FormatOnPaste`, the standalone bulk edit service and `SnippetController2` (audit 3 §A.17–§A.24).
  - **Block paste:** copying a column selection marks the clipboard as a block (Monaco `isBlock`). Pasting it at a single cursor with `multi_cursor_paste: Spread` (the default) puts each row on the next line at the cursor's visible column: the first row replaces the selection, short lines are padded with spaces, rows are padded to the block's width and rows past the end are appended. The cursor ends after the last row. It pasted the rows as lines of text.
  - **Copy line endings:** copies use the model's line ending within and between the copied pieces and after whole lines (with CRLF within pieces on Windows, as Monaco); they always used `\n`.
  - **Forced marker moves:** format edits and workspace edits (rename included) move cursors and decorations at an edit's edges past its text (`EditOperation.replaceMove`), in this editor's model and in others, so a cursor before reformatted indentation stays before the code. A single format edit replacing the whole text keeps Monaco's replace semantics.
  - **Format on paste:** only runs with a single cursor, over the paste event's range (from the cursor before the paste to after it). With several cursors it formatted everything from the first to the last.
  - **Formatter line endings:** formatters return `FormattingEdits`, whose `eol` switches the model's line ending in the same undo step as the edits (Monaco `TextEdit.eol` and `pushEOL`).
  - **Snippet overwrite:** `overwrite_before` and `overwrite_after` count from the selection's head, so selecting `console.log` and inserting `far` over 3 characters before gives `console.far`. With a selection, the whole selection was replaced.
  - **Snippet choices:** while a choice is active, asking for suggestions lists only its options (Monaco's exclusive `snippetChoiceCompletions` provider), so they come back after Escape and Ctrl+Space. The other providers were asked.
  - **Snippet path variables:** `TM_FILENAME`, `TM_DIRECTORY`, `TM_DIRECTORY_BASE`, `TM_FILEPATH` and `RELATIVE_FILEPATH` use the path of a URI that isn't a file, as standalone Monaco does: `http://h/abc/def/ghi` has `TM_DIRECTORY` `/abc/def`. They gave the whole URI, or nothing for the directories.
  - **Tests:** Monaco's `columnSelectionPaste.test.ts` paste cases (40 of its 47; the others test editor options and events), the "editor variables, file/dir" case of `snippetVariables.test.ts`, and copy line ending unit tests; GPUI tests for copying a column selection and pasting it as a block (and not after reselecting or with `Full`), CRLF copies, forced moves for format and workspace edits (in another model too), whole-text format edits, formatter line endings, format on paste with two cursors, snippet overwrite with selections in both directions and choices offered again.
  - **Breaking:**
    - `DocumentFormattingProvider::provide_document_formatting_edits`, `DocumentRangeFormattingProvider::provide_document_range_formatting_edits` and `provide_document_ranges_formatting_edits`, and `OnTypeFormattingProvider::provide_on_type_formatting_edits` return `FormattingEdits`; a `Vec<TextEdit>` converts with `.into()`.
    - `cursor::copy_text` takes the model's line ending and whether to force CRLF, and `cursor::paste` takes `is_block`.
    - `ClipboardCopyEvent` and `ClipboardPasteEvent` have an `is_block` field.
    - `SnippetVariableContext::uri` is gone: `path` holds the path of any URI.
- **Folding (#339):** what recomputes the folding ranges, `folding_imports_by_default`, range kinds, the chevron clicks and the fold highlight follow Monaco's `folding.ts`, `foldingRanges.ts`, `foldingDecorations.ts` and `minimap.ts` (audit 3 §D.6–§D.15).
  - **Recompute triggers:** changing `folding_strategy`, the model's language or its language configuration, and registering or unregistering a folding range provider compute the ranges again at once; they waited for the next edit. Changing `folding` or `folding_maximum_regions` starts over: turning folding off discards the folds, which no longer come back when it's turned on again. With folding off, Go to Parent/Previous/Next Fold, Remove Manual Folding Ranges and folding control clicks do nothing.
  - **Imports by default:** with `folding_imports_by_default`, every computation folds the import ranges until some have been folded, so a provider registered after the editor opened folds them; it was only tried on the first computation, and toggled ranges already folded open. The first visible line stays put. `FoldingViewState` saves whether imports were folded (Monaco `foldedImports`), so a restored view state's folded imports stay folded, and unfolded ones aren't folded again.
  - **Range kinds:** ranges from providers that give no kinds have no types (Monaco `fromFoldRanges`), so Fold All Block Comments and Fold All Regions go by the language's comment and region markers; they folded nothing.
  - **Reveals:** Go to Parent/Previous/Next Fold reveal the cursor centered when it's off screen, as every folding action does; Toggle Import Fold computes the ranges again afterwards. Restoring a view state no longer moves cursors out of the folds it restores (Monaco skips `adjustSelections` while restoring).
  - **Chevron clicks:** a folding control toggles on mouse up, when pressed and released on the same line, not on mouse down; the first 4px of the control don't count (Monaco leaves them to the dirty diff). Middle clicks on a control fold recursively, as Shift+clicks do. Clicking at the end of a collapsed line's text unfolds it on mouse up too, and the click places the cursor there, as in Monaco.
  - **Minimap:** with `folding_highlight`, a collapsed region's header line is shaded in the minimap with `editor.foldBackground` at half strength (Monaco `foldedBackgroundMinimap`).
  - **Limit reporting:** `CodeEditor::folding_limit` returns how many ranges the last computation found and the `folding_maximum_regions` they were cut to, if any, and `CodeEditorEvent::FoldingLimitChanged` reports changes (Monaco `FoldingController.limitReporter`). `api_commands::execute_folding_range_provider` computes a model's ranges as an editor with given options would (Monaco `_executeFoldingRangeProvider`).
  - **Tests:** "Limit by indent" from Monaco's `indentFold.test.ts`, with the reported limit, and the limit report in "Limit by nesting level"; GPUI tests for each repro: a late provider folding imports, restoring folded and unfolded imports after `set_model`, untyped provider ranges, strategy, language and provider changes, turning folding off, restoring a cursor into a fold, the limit report and event, `execute_folding_range_provider`, chevron press, drag-off, dead zone and middle clicks, the end-of-line click, recovered folds' manual icon, the minimap shading and Go to Parent Fold centering.
  - **Breaking:**
    - `view::folding::sanitize_ranges` and `compute_indent_ranges_limited` return a `FoldingLimit` (the ranges found and the limit applied) with the ranges; `sanitize_ranges` returned a `bool`, and `compute_indent_ranges_limited` only the ranges.
    - `FoldingViewState` has a new public field, `folded_imports`; struct literals need it or `..Default::default()`.
    - `CodeEditorEvent` has a new variant, `FoldingLimitChanged`.
- **Inline toolbar, paste and drop (#341):** the inline suggestion toolbar and its menus, the paste and drop options button, their progress and the peek title follow Monaco's `inlineCompletionsHintsWidget.ts`, `gutterIndicatorMenu.ts`, `postEditWidget.ts`, `inlineProgress.ts` and `referencesWidget.ts` (audit 3 §C.47–§C.53).
  - **Inline toolbar:** with `show_toolbar: Always` it's a hover card (Monaco's `withBorder` adds `monaco-hover`): `editorHoverWidget.*` colors, 8px corners and `shadow-lg`, not `editorWidget.*` with none. Hovered buttons take `toolbar.hoverBackground` (or `toolbar.hoverOutline` in high contrast). The long-distance hint's lower bar is in `descriptionForeground`.
  - **Keybinding labels:** the toolbar's Accept and Accept Word keys, its Previous and Next tooltips and the inline edit gutter menu's keys are looked up from the keymap (Monaco `lookupKeybinding`), so rebinding shows and macOS writes "⇧Tab". They're keycaps as in Monaco: 8px outlined keys in the toolbar, plain 13px keys in `descriptionForeground` in the gutter menu.
  - **The "⋯" menu** is the editor's context menu (Monaco's toolbar dropdown), below the button: Accept Line with its keys, Always Show Toolbar checked while on, then the provider's commands. The hover and the inline suggestion stay while it shows (Monaco `dropDownVisible`).
  - **The inline edit gutter menu** looks like Monaco's: a hover card, a 13px semibold `descriptionForeground` header with 28px lines, rows with 16px codicons (`check`, `close`, `collapse-all` or `expand-all`, `symbol-event` for the provider's commands) in `editorActionList.*` colors, `editorHoverBorder` separators, and the link action in a footer action bar.
  - **The paste and drop options button** is Monaco's `$(insert)` icon button: `button.*` colors, 2px padding, 4px corners, `shadow-lg` and a `widget.border`, titled "Show paste options... (⌘.)" (or drop), and below the edit only. It went away only on text changes and Escape; it now also goes when the cursor moves (Monaco `onDidChangeCursorPosition`), and Cmd/Ctrl+. with it.
  - **Inline progress:** slow paste and drop providers show Monaco's spinner where the text goes after 500ms ("Running paste handlers. Click to cancel and do basic paste", "Running drop handlers. Click to cancel"), in a 1em gap; resolving a paste edit shows it at once ("Resolving paste edit for '…'. Click to cancel"). Hovered it's an `x`; clicking it cancels, and a cancelled paste does the basic paste. Escape clears it with the operation. As in Monaco, changing the text cancels a pending paste or drop, and moving the selection cancels a pending paste; the paste was applied anyway after a selection change, and both kept running until their providers finished.
  - **Peek title:** a peek over a model without a URI, or with an `inmemory:` URI, is titled "References" whatever the kind, as in Monaco; it was the kind ("Definitions - Definitions (2)"), or the URI split into "1" and "model".
- **Multi diff editor (#346):** updating the file list keeps each file's state, files load at their first change, an empty list has Monaco's placeholder, and files can have their own options, following Monaco's `multiDiffEditorViewModel.ts`, `multiDiffEditorWidgetImpl.ts`, `multiDiffEditorWidget.ts`, `diffEditorItemTemplate.ts` and `model.ts`.
  - **Updating the files:** `set_documents` matches files by their paths, as Monaco's `mapObservableArrayCached` items: a file still there keeps its collapsed state, selections, measured height and editor, the active file stays active, and the list keeps its scroll position. Before, every file started over expanded and unmeasured, and the list jumped to the top. **Breaking:** to start over, call `set_documents_loading` first (Monaco's `'loading'` documents), which also shows "Loading...".
  - **First change on load:** when files load and none is active, the list goes to the first change and focuses it (Monaco's "Initialize first change"); `set_preserve_focus_on_load(true)` leaves the focus. Before, it showed the top with nothing selected. **Breaking:** `MultiDiffEditor::new` now takes the focus.
  - **Placeholder:** an empty list says "No Changed Files", or "Loading..." while loading (`placeholder_message`). Before, it was blank.
  - **Per-file options:** `MultiDiffDocument` has `options` (in place of the list's), `hide_original_line_numbers` and `context_keys`, set on that file's editors (Monaco `IDocumentDiffItem.options` and `contextKeys`); `set_document_options` changes them (`onOptionsDidChange`). **Breaking:** `MultiDiffDocument` has new fields; it now implements `Default`, so literals can end with `..Default::default()`.
  - **Original line numbers:** an explicit `Some(false)`, from `set_hide_original_line_numbers` or the document, shows them in the compact variants (Monaco `override ?? options.hideOriginalLineNumbers ?? variant`). Before, the variant always hid them.
  - **Padding and active control:** `set_padding_bottom` leaves room below the last file (Monaco `setPaddingBottom`), and `active_control` with `MultiDiffEditorEvent::ActiveControlChanged` follow the active file's diff editor (`getActiveControl`, `onDidChangeActiveControl`). **Breaking:** `MultiDiffEditorEvent` has a new variant.
  - **Reveals wait for the file's height:** a pending reveal now waits until the file is measured, so going to a change in a file not laid out yet no longer scrolls against its guessed 500px height.
- **Decorations overlay (#345):** decoration backgrounds follow Monaco's `DecorationsOverlay` (`decorations.ts`), `findDecorations.ts`, `unicodeHighlighter.ts` and `foldingDecorations.ts`.
  - **Unicode highlights:** have `showIfCollapsed`, as in Monaco, so an invisible or narrow character (a zero-width or thin space) is boxed a character wide, centered. Before, a zero-width space was a 2px sliver.
  - **Find matches:** the match, current match and no-overview decorations have `showIfCollapsed`: an empty regex match (`^`, `^$`) is a character wide instead of a 2px bar, and a match ending at the start of a line (`;\n`) stops at the end of the line before instead of leaving a sliver on the next. The non-current matches cover each line break they span, half a character wide (Monaco's `includeNewLines` for `findMatch`), empty lines included. The no-overview matches have z-index 0, Monaco's default, instead of 10.
  - **Empty pieces:** an empty range, an empty line inside a multi-line range and a range's end at a line's start draw nothing, as Monaco's 0px wide elements, instead of a 2px bar. Snippet placeholders keep their 2px (`min-width` in `snippetSession.css`), and draw their border as Monaco's 1px outline, outside the range, so an empty final tab stop still shows.
  - **Layering:** whole-line decorations are painted before the others, each in z-index order, so a whole-line highlight with a high z-index no longer covers find matches or word highlights. The fold background (`editor.foldBackground`) is one of them, at z-index 0, over the selections and indent guides instead of under them.
  - **API:** `DecorationOptions::include_new_lines` (Monaco's `includeNewLines`) and `DecorationOptions::min_width` (CSS `min-width`). A `show_if_collapsed` whole-line decoration ending at a line's start keeps that line, as Monaco only shortens the others.
  - **Docs:** the book's decorations and options pages and the parity doc.
  - **Playground:** the demo's whole-line decoration has z-index 20, as a debugger line highlight, to show find matches over it.
