  - **Tests:** GPUI tests of the painted pieces and their order: empty pieces, the minimum width and outlines, whole-line decorations first, the fold background between whole-line decorations, find matches with `^$`, `;\n` and a match over an empty line, and a zero-width space's highlight; a unit test of the line break, widening, filling and minimum width. Monaco has no tests of the overlay to port.
  - **Not as in Monaco:** Monaco keys the line breaks on the `findMatch` class name; here it's the option. Touching pieces of one class aren't merged, as there are no class names. A range a float's error narrower than a character isn't widened.
  - **Breaking:** `DecorationOptions` has new public fields, `include_new_lines` and `min_width`; struct literals need `..Default::default()`.
- **Suggest widget and parameter hints (#344):** the suggest widget follows Monaco's `SuggestWidget`, `SuggestDetailsWidget`, `SuggestController` and `suggest.css`, and parameter hints its `ParameterHintsWidget`.
  - **Anchoring:** the list is placed at the cursor when it shows and on every refilter (`showSuggestions`' `setPosition(editor.getPosition())`), as is "Loading...". Before, it stayed under the start of the word, so Ctrl+Space at the end of `foobar` opened it under the `f`, and it didn't follow typing.
  - **Details while resolving:** while the focused item resolves, the details panel keeps what it showed, shows "Loading..." (two rows tall) after 250ms, then the resolved item (`_onListFocus`, `renderLoading`). Before, the panel disappeared until resolving ended when the unresolved item had no docs, so it flickered and a list with details west of it shifted. The focused item is resolved before the widget updates, and refiltering doesn't ask the provider again while it's still resolving.
  - **Show More:** the status bar's "Show More" / "Show Less" follows Monaco's `suggestionCanResolve` (the provider resolves items, the item has docs, or its detail isn't its label), so word-based suggestions have it too. Before, it needed docs or a detail.
  - **Toggle Details:** Ctrl+Space (`toggleSuggestionDetails`) opens the details only when the focused item has docs or a detail (or explain mode is on), as `toggleDetails` does. Before, it flipped the remembered setting anyway, so the next item with docs opened them.
  - **Tab alternatives:** only Tab and Enter (and the status bar's Insert, Tab completion and `focusAndAcceptSuggestion`) keep the other suggestions for Tab and Shift+Tab (`KeepAlternativeSuggestions`). Before, a click, Shift+Enter or a commit character did too.
  - **Parameter hints:** follow the cursor on every selection change, as Monaco lays the widget out at the editor's position. Before, they stayed where they were requested until the next answer, then jumped.
  - **Rows:** unfocused rows under the mouse get `list.hoverBackground`. The kind icon takes 16px and a 4px margin (`.suggest-icon`), so a large `suggest.line_height` no longer pushes the labels right; a color swatch is Monaco's `.colorspan`, 0.7em after 0.3em.
  - **Sashes:** double-clicking one of the list's resets its size (forgetting the stored one); one of the details panel's fits it to its content again.
  - **Details:** a detail equal to the label shows in the details panel when there are docs, as `renderItem` does; only whether the panel can open compares them. A detail with a `label_detail` or `label_description` (Monaco's label object) never equals the label, and an empty one is no detail.
  - **Docs:** the book's providers page and the parity doc.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the list at the cursor, details while resolving with "Loading...", Show More for word-based suggestions, Toggle Details without docs, Tab alternatives after Enter, Shift+Enter, a commit character and a click, sash double-clicks, a detail equal to the label, and parameter hints following the caret.
- **Quick input and menus (#343):** the quick input, the context menu, the rename suggestions and the standalone color picker follow Monaco and VS Code more closely.
  - **Go to Line:** relative line numbers show as absolute ones while Go to Line (`:` or `::`) is the provider, and are relative again when another provider takes over or the input closes (Monaco `gotoLineQuickAccess.ts`). Lines, columns and offsets are read as `parseInt` reads them, so `:12.` goes to line 12 instead of showing the prompt. A column inside a tab goes to the nearest side of it, as Monaco's `columnFromVisibleColumn`, and full-width characters count two columns. A negative offset counts from the end in one-based mode too (`::-1` is the end, not the start).
  - **Go to Symbol:** the whole query is scored against the name first, so `@getting started` finds `# Getting Started`; otherwise the first word matches the name and every other word must match the container. Symbols are scored as VS Code's `scoreFuzzy2` does, with a weak first match allowed, and the query normalized (`*`, `…`, quotes and a trailing `#` dropped).
  - **Previews:** the Go to Line and Go to Symbol preview's overview ruler mark is in the Full lane, not the Right one.
  - **Quick input chrome:** it's centered on the whole editor, gutter and minimap included, with 6px corners and VS Code's `0 0 8px 2px widget.shadow` (none in high contrast) instead of GPUI's shadow. The input box has `input.border`, and `focusBorder` while focused, instead of `inputOption.activeBorder`. The input's text shows: its box had no height (the editor element fills its parent, whose height was auto), so what was typed was never painted.
  - **Context menu:** the wheel over the text hides it (Monaco `ContextMenuController`); over the menu it doesn't. Items run on mouse up, as VS Code's menu, so a press can be dragged off an item to cancel it. The menu's shadow is `0 2px 8px widget.shadow`, so high contrast themes have none.
  - **Rename:** the suggestions use the editor's font family and weight, as Monaco's `RenameCandidateView`, matching the width they're measured with.
  - **Standalone color picker:** shows below the cursor first when `hover.above` is off, and its header's original color box is sized from the body as Monaco does (52px rather than the hover's 74px).
  - **Tests:** ports of Monaco's `gotoLineQuickAccess.test.ts` ("parsePosition works as expected" and "with tabs", run through the quick input, zero-based offsets included); unit tests for the query normalization and multi-word scoring; GPUI tests for the line numbers switch, names with spaces, container words, the Full lane, centering, the input's height, the menu's wheel and mouse-up behavior, and the color picker's placement and header.
  - **Breaking:** `OverlayPosition::TopCenter` widgets are centered on the whole editor rather than on its text area.
- **Markers (#342):** ported Monaco's marker service and marker decorations (`markerService.ts`, `markerDecorationsService.ts`).
  - **Hint dots:** untagged hints draw Monaco's three dots under their first two characters (`editorHint.foreground`), and in high contrast a 2px dotted `editorHint.border`.
  - **High contrast:** errors, warnings and infos also get the `4px double` underline in `editorError.border`, `editorWarning.border` and `editorInfo.border`.
  - **Models without a uri:** get Monaco's `inmemory://model/N` as their new `TextModel::resource()`, so `on_did_change_markers` reports their markers, `MarkerFilter { resource }` selects them and `model_for_uri` finds the model. `uri()` is unchanged.
  - **Lifetime:** markers are kept by uri in a global marker service, not on the model. They outlive the model, except `inmemory:`, `internal:` and `vscode:` ones, and decorate the next model with that uri. `model_markers` returns them whether or not a model is alive, by owner and then uri in the order first set, as Monaco's `DoubleResourceMap` does. `remove_all_markers` also clears uris without a model.
  - **Cap:** only the first 500 markers of a uri are decorated (`MAX_DECORATED_MARKERS`). Markers that didn't change keep their decorations when another owner's change.
  - **Ranges:** an empty marker covers the word at its position, and stays empty (drawn one character wide) on an empty line or after the last non-whitespace character, instead of covering the next character or the line break. A whole-line marker, from column 0 to `u32::MAX`, starts at the first non-whitespace character. F8 still goes to an empty marker's own position.
  - **Tests:** ports of `markerService.test.ts` and `markerDecorationsService.test.ts` behaviors (uri-less models, in-memory disposal, markers outliving a `file:` model, word widening, end-of-line and empty-line markers, whole-line trimming, the 500 cap, decorations kept across owners), and unit tests for the hint dots and high contrast borders.
  - **Breaking:** `ModelMarker::resource` is a `SharedString` (the model's `resource()`), not an `Option`. `model_markers` without a `resource` lists markers by owner first, not by model. Markers no longer go away with a `file:` (or other non-in-memory) model.
- **Find widget (#340):** the find widget's option feedback, scope, reveal, margin, escaping and colors follow Monaco's `findOptionsWidget.ts`, `findWidget.ts`, `findController.ts` and `findDecorations.ts`.
  - **Options widget:** with find closed, Cmd+Alt+C, W, R and P (Alt+C, W, R and P elsewhere) show Monaco's `FindOptionsWidget`, the case, whole word and regex toggles at the editor's top right, for 2s, longer while the pointer is over it; its toggles can be clicked. Once find has opened, any change to those three toggles while it's closed shows it too (Cmd/Ctrl+D's whole-word override, say), as in Monaco. These commands no longer move the cursor to a match (Monaco's `change(…, false)`); the widget's own toggles still do.
  - **Find in selection:** focusing the find or replace input with find in selection on takes the selections as the scope again (Monaco `_updateSearchScope`). Monaco's `FindReplaceState.change` only replaces a scope that's set when one of the new ranges equals all of its ranges (for a one-range scope, when the selections include it), so selecting other lines keeps the old scope, as in Monaco.
  - **Reveal:** opening find seeded from a selection the widget would cover (on the first lines, spanning lines or reaching under the widget) adds the view zone without scrolling, so the text moves down from under the widget. Monaco does this only the first time the widget opens, since closing drops its view zone object; the port does it every time. Growing the zone (replace, multi-line inputs) scrolls by the difference instead of removing and adding it.
  - **Margin:** the widget is 4px below the editor's top (`margin-top: 4px`); the view zone keeps the widget's height.
  - **Escaping:** a search string seeded from the selection with regex on is escaped with Monaco's `escapeRegExpCharacters` (`\ { } * + ? | ^ $ . [ ] ( )`), not `regex::escape`, so `a-b` stays `a-b`.
  - **Colors:** as Monaco's theming has it, `editor.findMatchForeground` colors the other matches' text and `editor.findMatchHighlightForeground` the current match's. Over 1000 matches, the other matches have no text color and z-index 0 (`_FIND_MATCH_NO_OVERVIEW_DECORATION`).
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the options widget (showing, hiding, the toggle commands, clicking and hovering), the toggle commands not moving the cursor, the margin, the reveal with and without seeding, the scope on input focus, and the match colors, with a unit test for the escaping. Monaco's find tests have no cases for these (its `issue #6149` and `#38232` escaping tests are ported already).
- **Gutter and decoration hovers (#329):** decoration hovers follow Monaco's `ContentHoverComputer._getLineDecorations`, `MarkdownHoverParticipant`, `GlyphHoverComputer` and `GlyphHoverWidget`.
  - **Whole-line hovers:** a whole-line decoration's `hover_message` shows anywhere on its line (the blame pattern, an empty range with `is_whole_line`), a `show_if_collapsed` one a character either side of its range, and a multi-line decoration's part is about its range on the hovered line (the `hoverHighlight`).
  - **Placement:** the glyph margin and line number hovers are an overlay just right of the glyph margin (or of the line numbers), centered on the hovered line and kept inside the editor, 500px wide at most and not resizable, instead of a content hover above or below the line.
  - **Lanes and line numbers:** the glyph hover shows the decorations covering the row, not just those starting on it; in the glyph margin only those in the hovered lane (`glyph_margin.position`), and right of the line's lanes nothing; over the line numbers the new `line_number_hover_message`. `MouseTargetDetail::Margin::glyph_margin_lane` is the hovered lane (1 left, 2 center, 3 right) instead of always 1.
  - **Breaking:** `DecorationOptions::hover_message` and `glyph_margin_hover_message` are `Vec<MarkdownString>` (Monaco's `IMarkdownString | IMarkdownString[]`) instead of `Option<SharedString>`, so they can be trusted, use theme icons or HTML, and give several messages (a hover row each in the glyph hover); `vec!["text".into()]` replaces `Some("text".into())`. Document links' hovers are trusted, as Monaco's are. Decorations hidden by `render_validation_decorations` don't hover.
- **Find widget slide-in:** the widget now slides down from above the editor over 200ms when it opens, as Monaco's `.find-widget` transition does, and settles 4px below the editor's top (`margin-top: 4px`). The slide used to be set on the overlay's root, which the editor places by its origin, so the widget appeared at once at the editor's top. The slide follows the executor's clock, so tests can step through it, and with `App::reduce_motion` set the widget shows in place at once, like Monaco's `.monaco-reduce-motion`. The view zone stays the widget's own height, without the margin.
  - **Tests:** the widget's top at the start, halfway through and at the end of the slide, and with reduced motion.
- **Tab handling (#332):** Tab and Shift+Tab follow Monaco's rules: `tab` and `outdent` need `editorTextFocus`, `tabDoesNotMoveFocus` and `writable`, while the suggest, snippet and tab completion rules don't test tab focus mode.
  - **Quick input and rename box:** Tab and Shift+Tab stay in the widget. Before, they reached the editor and indented it at its hidden cursor, adding an undo step.
  - **Tab focus mode:** Tab accepts the focused suggestion, moves to a snippet's next tab stop and inserts tab completions, and Shift+Tab moves back and swaps in the previous completion, instead of moving the focus. Ghost text and inline edits still give way to the focus, as Monaco's `inlineSuggest.commit` and `inlineSuggest.jump` require `!tabMovesFocus`.
  - **Read-only editors:** Tab and Shift+Tab move the focus, as in Monaco, where the key falls through to the browser, instead of showing the read-only message and keeping it. That includes the diff editor's original side by default. The `tab` and `outdent` commands do nothing there. The find widget's inputs keep their Tab order.
  - **Snippet choices:** Shift+Tab goes back to the previous tab stop (Monaco's second `jumpToPrevSnippetPlaceholder` rule, `EditorContrib + 91`) instead of accepting the option in the other insert mode.
  - **Docs:** the book's keymap page and the parity doc's precedence row.
  - **Tests:** GPUI keystroke tests for each case, the find widget's inputs in a read-only editor with tab focus mode on included.
- **Sticky scroll and section headers (#337):** state follows the model, and sticky clicks, pointer and keyboard focus match Monaco's `StickyScrollController`, `StickyScrollWidget` and `SectionHeaderDetector`.
  - **Switching models:** `set_model` resets the sticky scroll model and the minimap section headers, and their caches key on the model as well as its text version, so an unedited model no longer shows the previous model's sticky lines or `MARK:` labels.
  - **Stale sticky lines:** while the sticky model is recomputed after an edit, the widget keeps the lines it last drew (Monaco renders only when the model's version is the text's), instead of drawing old rows over new text.
  - **Clicks:** a click on a sticky line's number does nothing, a click right of its text goes to column 0, and a click needs the mouse down and up on the same part (text, number or chevron) of the same line, as a DOM `click` does.
  - **Pointer:** a hand over sticky text and shown chevrons, the arrow over the rest of the sticky gutter; the gutter's folding controls under the sticky lines no longer show their hand or tooltip.
  - **Keyboard focus:** sticky focus ends when the editor loses focus or a sticky line is pressed, and while it's focused typed text and unmodified keys other than Up, Down, Enter and Escape don't reach the text.
  - **Folding model:** sticky scopes come from the folding ranges without manual ("Create Folding Range from Selection") or recovered regions, as Monaco's sticky model computes its own.
  - **Minimap:** section headers follow edits until they're found again (Monaco tracks them as decorations), and `minimap.autohide: "scroll"` shows the minimap when the scroll top or scroll height changes, not on horizontal scrolling.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests in `tests/sticky_scroll.rs` and `tests/minimap.rs` for each item.
- **Diff editor (#338):** diff results, revert, moved-block compare and alignment follow Monaco's diff editor (audit 3 §E.1–§E.6).
  - **Normalized diffs:** each computed diff goes through Monaco's `normalizeDocumentDiff`: a character change from column 1 to the line end on both sides, not on the last line, ends at the start of the next line. Its highlight fills to the right edge (`shouldFillLineOnLineBreak`), the true inline view shows such lines as deleted lines instead of striking them through, `compact_mode` doesn't count them as simple, and reverting them restores the line break.
  - **Revert at the cursor:** the `Revert` action (`diffEditor.revert`) reverts the changes whose modified lines overlap or touch the selection's `[start line, end line)`, as Monaco's `revertFocusedRangeMappings` does. So a cursor right below a deleted block, or on the line after a change, reverts it; before, deletions could never be reverted this way.
  - **Selection toolbar:** "Revert Selection" (and the margin's selection arrow) only takes changed spans the selection overlaps, not ones it only touches (Monaco `Range.areIntersecting`). Changes on the selected lines get no button of their own while it shows, as in Monaco.
  - **Moved-block compare:** moving the cursor off the compared block stops comparing it, and each new diff finds the compared block again by its modified lines instead of by index, then stops comparing if the cursor isn't in it (Monaco `setActiveMovedTextFromCursor`). The highlighted moved block also follows the cursor through new diffs.
  - **Line heights:** both sub-editors turn off `allow_variable_line_heights` (Monaco `_adjustOptionsForSubEditor`), so decorations with a `line_height` no longer break the alignment.
  - **Scroll stability:** realigning keeps the modified editor's first visible line where it is on screen (Monaco's `StableEditorScrollState` around its view zones), so padding added or removed above the viewport, e.g. after toggling `ignore_trim_whitespace` or editing the original, no longer makes the text jump. The original side follows through the scroll sync.
  - **Tests:** unit tests for the normalization and for finding the compared move; GPUI tests for whole-line highlights and the true inline view, reverting a deletion and a change that ends at the cursor, the selection arrow on touching and overlapping selections, compare across cursor moves and new diffs, the sub-editors' line height option, and keeping the text still while padding is added above it.
  - **Breaking:** `DiffEditor::diff()` and `line_changes()` return the normalized diff, as Monaco's `getDiffComputationResult` does, so whole-line character changes end at column 1 of the next line. `diff::compute_diff` is unchanged. The sub-editors' `allow_variable_line_heights` is always off.
- **Multi-cursor (#334):** Cmd/Ctrl+D matching, column selection, undo stops and the multi-cursor command arguments follow Monaco's `multicursor.ts`, `cursorColumnSelection.ts` and `coreCommands.ts` (audit 3 §A.9–§A.11, §A.14 and §A.16).
  - **Selected-text matching:** with the default `selected_text_match_mode: FindOptions`, Cmd/Ctrl+D, its previous-match and move variants, Select All Occurrences and the selection highlights match with the find state's match case and whole word whether or not the find widget is open (Monaco `getSelectionSearchOptions`). Both are off until set, so selected text now matches case-insensitively by default; it matched case-sensitively unless the widget was open. A session that uses the find toggles ends when they change, and changing `selected_text_match_mode` ends the session. The selection highlights refresh when the find state or the mode changes, without the cursor moving.
  - **Several empty cursors:** Cmd/Ctrl+D with several selections and an empty one only expands them to their words, as Monaco does; it started a whole-word, case-sensitive session.
  - **Column selection on view lines:** the keyboard (`cursorColumnSelect*`, Shift+arrows with `column_selection`) and the mouse (Shift+Alt+drag, middle drag) select on view lines, as `columnSelect` with a `viewPosition` already did: folded lines are skipped and each segment of a wrapped line gets a cursor. They selected model lines, so a cursor could land inside a fold. `columnSelect` without a `viewPosition` starts from where `position` shows.
  - **Undo stops:** Add Cursor Above/Below, Focus Next/Previous Cursor and keyboard column selection end the undo group (Monaco `pushStackElement`), so undo after typing with the new cursors removes only that typing.
  - **Read-only:** Add Cursor Above/Below and Focus Next/Previous Cursor do nothing in a read-only editor.
  - **Arguments:** `editor.action.insertCursorAbove`/`Below` take `logicalLine` (`false` adds the cursor on the next view line, such as a wrapped line's next segment), and `deleteInsideWord` takes `onlyWord`, through `trigger_with_payload`.
  - **Tests:** "default selected-text matching uses Find match case and whole word options", the "Selected text occurrence matching" matrix for all five actions, and the "Selected text occurrence matching highlights" suite's match-case, duplicate-find-highlight and configuration-refresh tests from Monaco's `multicursor.test.ts`; GPUI tests for the several-empty-cursors repro, ending a session on a find toggle change, the undo stops, the read-only no-ops, `logicalLine`, `onlyWord`, and keyboard and mouse column selection across a fold and a wrapped line. `add_previous_selected_text_case_sensitive`, `move_previous` and `move_from_the_find_input_searches_the_find_string` set `selected_text_match_mode: CaseSensitive`, as their Monaco originals do.
  - **Breaking:**
    - Selected text matches case-insensitively by default (see above); set `selected_text_match_mode: CaseSensitive` for the old behavior with find closed.
    - `AddCursorAbove` and `AddCursorBelow` are structs with a `logical_line: Option<bool>` field, and `DeleteInsideWord` one with `only_word: bool`; `AddCursorAbove::default()` and the others are the old actions.
- **Folding follows the cursor and the text (#324):** folds behave as Monaco's folding model and hidden range model do (`folding.ts`, `foldingModel.ts`, `foldingRanges.ts`, `hiddenRangeModel.ts`, `viewModelLines.ts`).
  - **Unfold on reveal:** 200ms after a cursor change, every collapsed region hiding a selection's start unfolds, and the primary cursor shows, centered if it's off screen (`revealCursor`). Find, Go to Line, Go to Definition, Jump to Bracket, Cmd+D, `set_selections` and the rest no longer leave the target hidden under its fold.
  - **Regions move with edits:** regions are tracked from the end of their header line to the end of their last line, as Monaco's folding decorations are, so chevrons stay on their lines while typing and fold commands in the 200ms before the ranges are recomputed fold the right lines. Ranges computed for an older version of the text move through the edits made since.
  - **Edits don't unfold:** lines inserted into a folded region stay hidden, so Replace All and Format Document keep the folds. The hidden lines follow edits as Monaco's view model does: a line typed after a folded header shows until the ranges are recomputed. Replacing the whole text (`set_value`) drops the folds, as Monaco's `setValue` does.
  - **Recomputed ranges:** collapsed and manual regions merge into the new ranges (`sanitizeAndMerge`). A collapsed region takes the new range with the same start line, and expands only if its lines changed and a cursor starts in it. A collapsed region with no new range stays folded, with the manual icons (`FoldSource.recovered`). Ranges crossing their enclosing one are dropped. After an update hides a cursor, it moves out of the hidden lines.
  - **Breaking:** `view::folding::FoldState` tracks regions itself. It has `sync`, `clear`, `toggle`, `reveal_rows`, `memento` and `apply_memento`; `set_ranges` takes the text the ranges were computed for and the selections' start rows. Its queries no longer take a buffer, and `collapse`, `expand`, `expand_intersecting`, `prune` and `collapse_all` are gone. `FoldRange` has a new public field, `recovered`; add `recovered: false` to struct literals.
- **Rename and code actions off screen, lightbulb and menu gaps (#325):** rename and the code action menu reveal an off-screen symbol or cursor before showing, and the lightbulb and the menu follow Monaco's `RenameWidget`, `CodeActionController`, `LightBulbWidget`, `codeAction.ts` and `codeActionMenu.ts` more closely.
  - **F2 off screen:** the symbol's line is revealed (`revealLineInCenterIfOutsideViewport`) before the rename box shows. Before, the box was cancelled at once as out of view, so F2 on a symbol scrolled out of view did nothing.
  - **Cmd/Ctrl+. off screen:** the code action menu reveals its position (`toCoords`'s `revealPosition(…, Immediate)`) before anchoring there. Before, it opened invisible but focused, and Enter applied a fix you couldn't see.
  - **Lightbulb placement:** on the last line after code, the bulb goes in the gutter, as Monaco's `endLine` has no next line to block. Before, it was drawn over the previous line's code. The check that keeps an inline bulb off the cursor uses Monaco's 1-based column, so it no longer hides the bulb one column early.
  - **`lightbulb: Off`:** the bulb never shows, even after Cmd/Ctrl+. or another command asked for actions. Before, it showed until the cursor moved.
  - **Focused action:** the highlight covers the action's `ranges` (its diagnostics when it fixes several, as for "fix all"), or else its diagnostics, as Monaco's `onFocus`; the first action is highlighted as the menu opens. Focusing an action with diagnostics announces "Context: {word} at line {line} and column {column}." Before, `ranges` was ignored.
  - **Order:** AI actions (`is_ai`) come after the others, so `apply: "first"` no longer picks one a provider listed first. The module doc no longer says AI actions aren't ported.
  - **`showHeaders`:** `EditorOptions::code_action_widget` (`CodeActionWidgetOptions`, Monaco `editor.codeActionWidget.showHeaders`). With `show_headers` off, the menu lists the actions in order without headers, group icons or keybindings, and a disabled action shows why instead of its title. `code_action_menu_rows` returns the rows as shown.
  - **Keys:** in the menu, Cmd/Ctrl+. accepts and Cmd/Ctrl+Up/Down move, as Monaco's secondary keys, so Cmd/Ctrl+. twice applies the first action.
  - **Menu from the lightbulb:** opens at the click's x, a third of a line below the bulb, and a line further when the bulb is on the line above the cursor's (`LightBulbWidget`'s mouse down), so it covers neither. Before, it went below the cursor's line at column 0 and could cover the bulb.
  - **Docs:** the book's code action, options and keymap pages and the parity doc.
  - **Playground:** `PLAYGROUND_KEYS` takes `click:x,y`, a left click at window pixels `x, y`.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for F2 and Cmd+. off screen, the last-line bulb, `lightbulb: Off` after Cmd+., the focused action's ranges, diagnostics and announcement, AI actions last with `apply: "first"`, `show_headers` off, the secondary keys and the menu from the lightbulb (both pads); unit tests for the placement rules and the sort.
  - **Not as in Monaco:** with no room below, the menu from the lightbulb goes above the bulb rather than ending at the point under it.
  - **Breaking:** `EditorOptions` has a new public field, `code_action_widget`; struct literals need `..Default::default()`.
- **Injected text in view columns (#298):** inlay hints, `before`/`after` decoration text and ghost text are part of the view lines' columns, as Monaco's `ModelLineProjectionData` makes them, instead of only the shaped line.
  - **Selections:** drawn and hit-tested in view columns (Monaco's view selections). A selection across injected text covers it; one ending at its column (a model position) stops before it; one whose head moved past it (Shift+Right, a Shift+click on its right side) covers it.
  - **Wrapping:** `Simple` and `Advanced` wrapping count injected text (Monaco `createLineBreaks` with injected text), and a long one wraps mid-text. The view lines follow decorations with injected text as they're added, removed or moved by edits.
  - **Up/Down:** keep the visible column with injected text included (`CursorColumns` on view lines), so the cursor moves straight up and down past inlay hints. Moves normalize around injected text and wrap points as Monaco's `MoveOperations` do, and `_validateViewState` keeps the columns it skips as leftover.
  - **Inlay hint updates:** as Monaco's `_updateHintsDecorators`, new hints replace only the decorations in the rows they were asked for, so hints above and below stay after scrolling. Hints that come back the same keep their decorations (a port-side optimization: the view lines don't gather them again), which keeps Page Down as fast as before.
  - **Same as before:** cursor stops, `attachedData` and mouse targets, clicks on injected text, ghost text, and where moves and clicks put the cursor. The cursor's side of injected text is now its real view column instead of an offset tracked beside its model position. Word moves still step over the text only. Tabs after injected text line up with its columns, as in Monaco.
  - **API:** `ViewLines::set_injected_text`, `injected_text`, `projection`, `to_view_with_affinity`, `normalize_position`, `line_min_column`, `injected_text_at`, and `segment_content` (the old view line content, without injected text) with its column conversions; `view::line_breaks::create_line_breaks`; `view::injected_text::LineInjectedText`; `CursorModel::normalize_position` (defaults to the position).
  - **Tests:** ports of Monaco's `modelLineProjection.test.ts` `getViewLinesData` with wrapping, with and without injected text, and `viewModelImpl.test.ts` "normalizePosition with multiple touching injected text" on the view lines; unit tests for wrapping with injected text, edits and `applyInjectedText`; GPUI tests for selection drawing and hit-testing across injected text, wrapping with it (both strategies), Up/Down columns, and Page Down keeping the inlay hint decorations it asks for again. Monaco's `monospaceLineBreaksComputer.test.ts` injected-text cases are all about `widthInEm`, which isn't ported.
  - **Breaking:**
    - `ViewLines` view columns count injected text: `to_view`, `to_model`, `line_content`, `line_max_column` and `normalize_view_position` work in them. `SegmentInfo` has a new `offsets` field.
    - `view::injected_text`: `ModelLineProjectionData::new` takes the break offsets' visible columns.
- **Keybinding precedence (#336):** the default bindings follow Monaco's `KeybindingWeight`s and `when` clauses. GPUI runs, of the bindings matching at the same depth, the one added last, and the platform bindings came after the widget and state ones, so plain editor keys took their keys away.
  - **Order:** `default_key_bindings()` lists the plain `CodeEditor` bindings first, the platform ones included, then every binding with a more specific context, each group in its old order. `goToNextReference`'s F12 and Shift+F12 with a peek view open (`EditorContrib - 10`) come before both, so Go to Definition and Go to References still win while they have a provider.
  - **Ghost text:** Cmd+Right (Ctrl+Right on Windows and Linux) accepts the next word (`inlineSuggest.acceptNextWord`, `EditorContrib + 1`) instead of moving the cursor, and, as in Monaco, not with a screen reader (`!accessibilityModeEnabled`), where it moves the cursor (on Windows, by accessibility words).
  - **Quick input:** VS Code's list keys (`quickInputActions.ts`, `quickAccessActions.ts`) reach the list: Ctrl+Home/End (WinCtrl on macOS) and with Alt, on macOS also with Cmd, go to the first and last item, Ctrl+Up/Down move by one, and on macOS Ctrl+N/P too. Before, Cmd/Ctrl+Home/End and Ctrl+N/P moved the input's cursor.
  - **Escape:** runs Monaco's rules in weight order: the reference peek first (`closeReferenceSearch`, `WorkbenchContrib + 50`, above linked editing and the suggest widget; also when parameter hints show), the problem zone before a snippet (+50 against +30), and the find widget before F12 navigation (+5 against `EditorContrib`).
  - **Menu key:** the Menu (Apps) key, which GPUI calls `menu` on Windows and Linux, opens the context menu (Monaco's `KeyCode.ContextMenu`).
  - **Docs:** the book's keymap page has a precedence section and the new keys; the parity doc has a precedence row.
  - **Tests:** a test that no binding with a more general context and the same keys comes after one with a more specific context, and one for the F12 exception; GPUI keystroke tests for accepting a word of ghost text (and not with a screen reader), the quick input's keys, the three Escape orders and the Menu key, with each platform's keys.
  - **Breaking:** on macOS, Cmd+Home/End in the quick input stay in the input, as in VS Code (Ctrl+Home/End move in the list), and Ctrl+N/P no longer move in the list on Windows and Linux. The order of `default_key_bindings()` changed; a host binding in plain `CodeEditor` after `init` still beats every default, the widget and state ones included.
- **Content hover dismissal and stability (#335):** the content hover comes and goes as Monaco's `ContentHoverController` and `ContentHoverWidgetWrapper` have it.
  - **Keys:** any key but a modifier, the first key of a chord, or a hover command while the hover shows (Show or Focus Hover, Increase or Decrease Hover Verbosity) hides the hover, or cancels a pending one, before its own command runs. Arrow keys after Cmd/Ctrl+K Cmd/Ctrl+I close the hover as the cursor leaves, and one Escape closes it and the other cursors.
  - **Mouse:** a mouse down off the card (any button) hides it, and so does the start of a drag-select, during which no hover shows. Leaving the editor hides it and cancels a pending one, unless the mouse went onto the card or a drag (resizing it, its color picker, its scrollbar) holds the pointer. GPUI's unhovering on keyboard input isn't taken for a leave.
  - **Scrolling:** any scroll that moves the text hides it (wheels, scrollbar drags and clicks, Page Up/Down, reveals and `set_scroll_*`). A wheel that scrolls nothing, at the top of a file or with `scrollbar.handle_mouse_wheel` off, leaves it.
  - **`hover.enabled`:** turning it off hides the hover.
  - **Stability:** moving off what the hover is about, on the same line, keeps the card with the parts that still apply there (Monaco `canAdoptVisibleHover`, `isValidForHoverAnchor`) while the hover there is asked for, so moving along a long squiggle no longer makes it blink for the hover delay. The card is anchored where the hover was asked for and pulled left to the start of the parts on that line, such as the marker's (Monaco `computeHoverPositions`' secondary position), not at the word under the mouse.
  - **Scrollbars:** the hover card (10px) and the marker zone's body (6px) show scrollbars while their content overflows and the mouse is over them; the slider drags and a click on the track centers it there. They show at once, without Monaco's fade.
  - **Unicode hover:** its parts come after the markdown and inline suggestion parts (Monaco's `hoverOrdinal` 5), as one markdown paragraph with "Adjust settings" as a link after the reason, instead of a fixed 480px box over a separate row. The "too many characters" banner is a tab stop, with its message as its accessible label.
  - **Marker zone:** related information links are `textLink.activeForeground`, and a click opens the location and closes the zone.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for keys, chords and hover commands, Escape with several cursors, clicks on and off the card, leaving the editor with a hover shown or pending, drag-selects, wheels that do and don't scroll, API scrolls, turning hovers off, adopting a hover along a squiggle and its anchor, another line or no valid part, the unicode part's order, the hover's and the marker zone's scrollbars, related information in the marker zone, and the banner's focus. Typing and scrolling a 3.5MB Rust file perform the same as before (6 interleaved release runs each).
- **Typing leftovers (#331):** the rest of Monaco's typing behavior from the third parity audit (§A.1–A.7): `ShiftCommand`'s doc comment alignment, `compositionEndWithInterceptors`, snippet closing characters, `EnterOperation.lineInsertBefore`/`lineInsertAfter` and auto-closed tracking.
  - **Doc comments keep their alignment:** Tab and Shift+Tab on a selection and Indent/Outdent Lines treat the leading spaces of the line above's onEnter `appendText` (less its `removeText`) as alignment, as Monaco's `ShiftCommand` does, so ` * ` lines of a `/** */` comment stay under the `*` of `/**`. Before, indenting ate their space and outdenting an unindented comment moved them.
  - **IME and dead-key commits:** when a composition ends, a single character it typed gets the interceptors after the fact (Monaco `compositionEndWithInterceptors`): a dead-key `'` then Space auto-closes, types over an auto-inserted quote, or surrounds the selection it replaced (`'test'`). Overtype mode's composition handling moved there too. This runs for the input handler's commits (`replace_text_in_range`, `unmark_text`, an emptied composition) and between the `compositionStart` and `compositionEnd` commands, where typing now skips the interceptors except overtyping, as Monaco's `isDoingComposition` does.
  - **Snippet closing characters are auto-closed:** a snippet whose text ends with a closing character (`id="$1"`, `log($1)`) has it tracked as auto-inserted (Monaco `executeEdits('snippet')`), so typing `"` or `)` there types over it and Backspace deletes the pair.
  - **Insert Line Above/Below:** indent the new line by Enter's onEnter and indentation rules at the end of the line (Monaco `EnterOperation._enter`), so Cmd+Enter on `if (x) {` indents one level and in a doc comment continues it.
  - **Auto-closed characters survive other edits:** undo, redo, `apply_edits` and other editors on the model move them as Monaco's decorations move, instead of forgetting them.
  - **`type` runs the interceptors per character:** `type_text` and `trigger("type", …)` with several characters type them one at a time through the interceptors, as Monaco's `Cursor.type` does, so `f(` gives `f()`.
  - **Cursor core API:** `indent_lines_with_language`, `outdent_lines_with_language`, `outdent_with_language`, `insert_line_above_with_language` and `insert_line_below_with_language` take the language; `CursorCollection::start_composition`, `is_composing` and `end_composition`, `CompositionState`, `CompositionOutcome` and `composition_end_with_language` port Monaco's composition handling; `snippet_auto_closed_pairs` finds a snippet's closing characters; `AutoClosedRanges::map` moves the ranges through an edit known by how it moves positions. The stale "not ported" notes about overtype mode are gone.
  - **Tests:** Monaco's `shiftCommand.test.ts` #348, #1609 and #1620 a–d, the IME tests of `cursor.test.ts` (#15825, #90016, #2773, #26820, #144690, #144693, #82701), #78975, and the overtype composition test in the cursor core; GPUI tests for dead-key commits through `replace_and_mark_text_in_range`/`replace_text_in_range`/`unmark_text` (auto-close, overtype, surround, several cursors, accents, canceling), the composition commands, Insert Line, doc comment indenting, multi-character `type`, auto-closed characters across another editor's edit, `apply_edits` and undo, and snippet closing characters.
  - **Breaking:** `CodeEditor::type_text` with more than one character now types it through the language's interceptors one character at a time (auto-closing, auto-indenting), with Monaco's undo grouping per character. With a composition started, `type_with_language` skips the interceptors other than overtyping.
- **Multi diff editor timing in tests (#359):** the list's smooth scroll, scroll-direction retention (Monaco's 100ms `scrollDirectionRetentionDurationMs`) and scrollbar fades read the executor's clock instead of `Instant::now`, so tests control time. Under load, wall-clock time between test frames passed the 100ms retention and changed which edge the list anchored to, which made `go_to_file_and_reveal_range` and `smooth_wheel_scrolling` fail now and then. Apps see no difference: the executor's clock is real time outside tests.
- **Line and bracket commands (#328):** in-place replace, Go to and Select to Bracket, bracket highlights, Trim Trailing Whitespace, the tab size pickers and Sort Lines, checked against Monaco's `inPlaceReplace`, `bracketMatching.ts`, `trimTrailingWhitespaceCommand.ts`, `indentation.ts` and `sortLinesCommand.ts` (audit 3 §A.25–§A.31 and §F.9).
  - **In-place replace:** Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+, ("Replace with Previous Value", `inPlaceReplace.up`) steps down and Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+. ("Replace with Next Value") steps up. They were crossed, and a test asserted the crossed result. The new value's flash is Monaco's `valueSetReplacement`: a 2px `editorBracketMatch.border` outline in the `vs` theme only, instead of an `inputOption.activeBorder` border in every theme. The new `DecorationOptions::outline` draws it, outside the range as a CSS `outline` is.
  - **Go to Bracket:** with no bracket next to the cursor and no pair around it, goes to the next bracket (Monaco `findNextBracket`). Closing brackets with no opening one are passed over, as Monaco's bracket pair tree leaves them out.
  - **Select to Bracket:** falls back to the next bracket's pair the same way, selects backwards from a closing bracket, drops cursors with no pair (and changes nothing when none has one), and takes Monaco's `selectBrackets` argument: `trigger_with_payload("editor.action.selectToBracket", json!({ "selectBrackets": false }), …)` selects only what's between the brackets. Both commands reveal the first selection, as Monaco's `revealRange` does.
  - **Bracket highlights:** up to 100 cursors, Monaco's limit, instead of 32. Matched brackets take `editorBracketMatch.foreground` over their pair colors when the theme sets it (Monaco's `bracket-match-inline`); the color was resolved but never drawn. With `match_brackets: Near`, the enclosing pair is no longer looked for. The cursor's row is scanned once for the adjacent and enclosing pairs, and the bracket scans look up each row's length once rather than up to four times, which keeps Page Down at parity with the added work.
  - **Trim Trailing Whitespace:** leaves a line whose trailing whitespace starts in a string or regular expression token, as standalone Monaco does (it never sets `files.trimTrailingWhitespaceInRegexAndStrings`), so a template literal's or multi-line string's spaces stay. Monaco checks the token at its `fromColumn` used as an offset, the character after the first trailing space, and so does the port. `cursor::trim_trailing_whitespace_with_language` takes the token types; `trim_trailing_whitespace` still trims every line.
  - **Tab size pickers:** "Indent Using Spaces", "Indent Using Tabs" and "Change Tab Display Size" start on the current size (Monaco's `activeItem`, 8 for larger sizes), so Enter keeps it; they started on 1. `CodeEditor::show_quick_pick_with_active_item` opens a quick pick on a given item, until the input is typed in.
  - **Sort Lines:** compares lines with the Unicode root collation, as the default `Intl.Collator` does for English and most locales: punctuation, then symbols, digits and letters, with case and accents only breaking ties (`b`, `_a`, `1`, `}`, `é`, `f`, `e` sort to `_a`, `}`, `1`, `b`, `e`, `é`, `f`). It compared lowercased code points. The locale isn't applied; the differences page says so.
  - **Dependency:** `icu_collator` 2.3 (bringing `icu_collator_data` and `utf16_iter`), from the ICU4X stack the word segmenter already uses. It's pinned to `~2.3` because `CollatorBorrowed::new_root` needs its `unstable` feature, which links only the root collation data rather than every locale's tailoring.
  - **Cursor API:** `cursor::match_bracket`, `enclosing_brackets` and `next_bracket` (Monaco `matchBracket`, `findEnclosingBrackets` and `findNextBracket`); `matching_bracket` is the first two, as before.
  - **Tests:** the 7 tests of Monaco's `bracketMatching.test.ts` and "skips strings and regex if configured" from `trimTrailingWhitespaceCommand.test.ts`; unit tests for the collation order and `next_bracket`; GPUI tests for the corrected in-place replace keys, the flash in each built-in theme, `selectBrackets` through `trigger_with_payload`, bracket highlights at 50, 100 and 101 cursors, `editorBracketMatch.foreground`, trimming around a JavaScript template literal, and the three pickers' active item.
  - **Breaking:**
    - Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+, and `InPlaceReplaceUp` replace with the previous value, and Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+. and `InPlaceReplaceDown` with the next. `CodeEditor::in_place_replace(up, cx)` is unchanged: `true` is the next value, as Monaco's `run(source, up)`.
    - `SelectToBracket` is a struct with a `select_brackets: Option<bool>` field (`SelectToBracket::default()` is the old action), and `CodeEditor::select_to_bracket` takes `select_brackets: bool`.
    - `DecorationOptions` has a new public field, `outline`; use `..Default::default()` in struct literals.
- **Language configuration and plain text (#326):** a language's configuration no longer depends on a grammar, plain text gets Monaco's `plaintext` defaults, and models follow languages registered after them (`languageConfigurationRegistry.ts`, `languageService.ts`, `languagesRegistry.ts`).
  - **Configuration without a grammar:** a language registered with only a configuration (no grammar or tokenizer, or while a tokens provider factory's task is pending) gets auto-closing, surround, Enter between brackets, bracket matching, Toggle Line/Block Comment, its word pattern, folding markers and off-side folding. `TextModel::language()` is now the resolved language whether or not it highlights.
  - **Plain text:** models without a language, or with an unregistered one, are Monaco's `plaintext`: the brackets `()`, `[]` and `{}` auto-close, are matched and indent on Enter, and selections are surrounded by them, `<>` and the three quotes; bracket pairs aren't colorized and folding is off-side. `Language::plain_text()` and `LanguageConfiguration::plain_text()` give it; `gpui_monaco_languages::plain_text()` returns it (with `text/plain`, and the alias `text` but no longer `txt`), and without one in the registry models use a built-in copy. `set_language_configuration(cx, "plaintext", …)` reaches models without a language when the registry has `plaintext`. The editor's own inputs (find, replace, rename, quick input) stay without a language, as Monaco's are HTML inputs. The cursor unit tests' plaintext configuration is now the shipped one.
  - **Late registration:** `register_language(cx, language)` (Monaco `languages.register`) registers a language and updates models as Monaco's `LanguageSelection` does: one created with its id or MIME type before it was registered, or whose uri now guesses it, takes it (emitting `LanguageChanged` when its id changes, else `LanguageConfigurationChanged`) and starts highlighting. `set_language_registry` updates existing models too. An unregistered id is still reported by `language_id()`, where Monaco reports `plaintext`.
  - **MIME types:** a registration without MIME types gets `text/x-<id>`, and when two languages claim a MIME type the one registered last wins (Monaco rebuilds its map in registration order). `colorize` and `colorize_element` take a MIME type too.
  - **Standard token types:** a token is a comment, string or regex by Monaco's `\b(comment|string|regex|regexp)\b`, first match wins: `string.regexp` is a string, and `invalid.string` and `meta.comment` count for `autoClosingPairs.notIn`, quick suggestions and bracket matching. Brackets in regexes are skipped too.
  - **Tokens provider factories:** `register_tokens_provider_factory` isn't called when the language already has a tokenizer (Monaco `TokenizationRegistry.getOrCreate`). A tree-sitter grammar doesn't count, so a factory can still replace it.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for typing `(`, surround, Enter between brackets and bracket matching with no language, with `plaintext`, with an unregistered id and with a configuration-only language; Toggle Line Comment and the word pattern without a grammar; a pending factory; `plaintext`'s runtime configuration; late registration by id, MIME type and uri, with its events; `text/x-<id>` and the last registration winning; standard token types; a factory with a tokenizer already set; the find input not auto-closing; and `colorize` by MIME type.
  - **Breaking:** editors without a language now auto-close, surround and match brackets, and `TextModel::language()` returns plain text for them (and a language without a grammar for those) instead of `None` once resolved. `LanguageRegistry::register` adds `text/x-<id>` to a language registered without MIME types. `string_and_comment_ranges` includes regexes.
- **Reveal and autoscroll (#323):** the editor scrolls only where Monaco reveals, and its cursor commands reveal every cursor rather than only the primary one (`cursor.ts`'s `setStates` and `revealAll`, `viewModelImpl.ts`, `coreCommands.ts`, `viewLines.ts`'s `_computeScrollTopToRevealRange`).
  - **API setters:** `set_position`, `set_selection` and `set_selections` no longer scroll the cursor into view, as Monaco's `setPosition`/`setSelection(s)` only emit events. Restoring a scroll position and then setting an off-screen cursor keeps the scroll, syncing selections into a background editor doesn't scroll it, and `set_position` around a `reveal_*` call no longer reveals twice. Features that set the cursor reveal it themselves where Monaco does: Go to Bracket and Select to Bracket (`revealRange`), Escape clearing a selection (`revealAllCursors`), F8 (`revealPositionInCenterIfOutsideViewport`), Focus Next/Previous Cursor (`revealPrimaryCursor`), `go_to_location`, and the multi-diff editor's last change (`revealLineInCenter`).
  - **All cursors:** keyboard moves, edits and the other cursor commands reveal the box from the first cursor's line to the last's (`revealAllCursors`), and don't scroll at all when it's taller than the viewport, horizontally either. Before, only the primary cursor was revealed, so a Down press could leave the other cursors off screen.
  - **The cursor Monaco picks:** Add Cursor Above/Below reveal the top- or bottom-most cursor (`revealTopMostCursor`/`revealBottomMostCursor`), so holding Cmd+Alt+Down follows the new cursors; keyboard, mouse and `columnSelect` column selection reveal the top-most cursor when the selection goes up from where it started and the bottom-most one otherwise; Cmd+D and the previous-match and move-selection variants center the new match if it's off screen (`revealRangeInCenterIfOutsideViewport`).
  - **Folding:** a chevron click reveals the clicked line, centered if off screen, instead of scrolling back to the cursor (`FoldingController.onEditorMouseUp`). Folding commands reveal the selection's start the same way (`FoldingAction`). Cursors moved out of a fold don't scroll the view.
  - **`editorScroll` with `revealCursor`:** scrolls as asked and puts the primary cursor, as the only cursor, in the lines that fully show at the new scroll top, inset by `cursorSurroundingLines` (or the sticky lines) and off the last line, without revealing it (`_runVerticalEditorScroll`, `findPositionInViewportIfOutside`). Before, the cursor stayed and the next frame scrolled back to it.
  - **PageUp/PageDown:** move by Monaco's `pageSize`, the lines the editor's height holds less two (at least one), and then reveal the cursors minimally instead of scrolling a page. On a 20-line editor, PageDown from line 1 puts the caret on line 19 without scrolling. Column selection's PageUp/PageDown, `scrollPageUp`/`scrollPageDown` and `editorScroll` by page or half page use the same size, so two lines of the last page stay in view.
  - **Scroll after a reveal:** `set_scroll_top`, `set_scroll_left` and `set_scroll_position` drop a reveal still waiting for the next frame, since Monaco's reveal would already have happened.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the API setters, revealing every cursor and not revealing cursors that don't fit, Add Cursor Above/Below, Cmd+D, keyboard column selection both ways, the fold chevron and folding commands, `editorScroll` with `revealCursor` (with and without `cursorSurroundingLines`), PageUp/PageDown and the page scrolls, and ports of `cursorMoveCommand.test.ts`'s view-top cases with a real viewport.
  - **Breaking:** `set_position`, `set_selection` and `set_selections` don't scroll; call `reveal_position`, `reveal_position_in_center` or `reveal_all_cursors` after them to show the cursor. PageUp/PageDown move two lines less and no longer scroll a page, and the page scrolls move two lines less.
- **F8 marker navigation (#330):** which markers F8 / Alt+F8 visit, in what order, and the marker zone following marker changes, revealing itself and reading out, as Monaco's `MarkerList`, `MarkerController` and `MarkerNavigationWidget` (`markerNavigationService.ts`, `gotoError.ts`, `gotoErrorWidget.ts`).
  - **No hints:** only errors, warnings and infos are visited and counted ("N of M problems"). Before, hints were too, so Alt+F8 on the playground's first line opened the "unused import" hint as "1 of 4".
  - **Order:** most severe first, then by range, as Monaco's default `problems.sortOrder: 'severity'` (standalone Monaco has no setting). Before, by position only.
  - **First move:** takes the first marker, in that order, containing the cursor (an empty one as the word at its start) or after it; backwards, before it. The zone shows at the cursor when it's in the marker, else at the marker's start. Later moves step through the list, so markers starting at the same place are each visited; moving the cursor off the shown marker, or the markers changing, makes the next move start from the cursor again. Before, the first marker starting after the cursor, which skipped the one under it and the second of two markers with the same start.
  - **Marker changes:** when the markers change while the zone is open, it shows the marker now at its position (the message only; the title, count and colors stay, as in Monaco), or turns stale: faded to 0.6 and italic (`.stale`). With no markers left, F8 leaves the zone as it is rather than closing it. Before, the zone kept the old marker.
  - **Reveal:** showing the zone reveals the marker's line and the zone under it (`ZoneWidget.show`; the last line near the top), then the position, horizontally too (`revealPositionInCenterIfOutsideViewport`), through the editor's reveal helpers. Before, a marker near the bottom opened its zone below the viewport.
  - **Accessibility:** the message is a `role="alert"` labelled with Monaco's "`<line>, Error: <message> at 3:5.`", also announced when shown or updated (Monaco's `aria.status`); the body is focusable (a click focuses it) with the tooltip role. Showing the zone gives the editor the focus, and so does closing it from its focused body.
  - **Docs:** the book's diagnostics page and the parity doc describe the order, the first move and marker changes.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for hints, the severity order and wrapping, the first move at the cursor (forwards and backwards), same-start markers, empty markers, the cursor leaving the marker, the zone following and going stale, both reveals and the labels and focus; the F8 and "View Problem" tests now expect the severity order and the zone at the cursor.
  - **Not as in Monaco:** "View Problem" still shows the marker it was clicked on; Monaco's `showAtMarker` runs the first-move search from the marker's start, which in severity order can land on a more severe marker further down. A hint (not in the list) does get that search.
  - **Breaking:** `go_to_marker` visits markers in severity order and skips hints, and no longer closes the zone when there's nothing to go to.
- **Differences page brought up to date (#304):** the book's differences page checked bullet by bullet against the code and Monaco's source.
  - **Removed:** the suggest widget's details placement, keyboard focus, explain mode and glyph icons (all ported in #246 and #248), and the inline completions' secondary cursor pairing (Monaco pairs each cursor with its own edit too).
  - **Corrected:** find replacement supports Monaco's case-changing escapes; snippet transforms run on `fancy-regex`, and case shorthands ignore the locale; folding uses tree-sitter fold queries; the diff editor has `maxFileSize`, `hideOriginalLineNumbers`, `compactMode`, `diffWordWrap` and the legacy algorithm, and the bullet now lists what isn't ported.
  - **Parity doc:** no longer says the peek view's sash hover shows at once or that the diff editor's codicons wait on #246; the syntax folding row says where `folds.scm` comes in.
  - **README and introduction:** the diff editor is no longer a non-goal or "not there yet"; the introduction lists the diff editors, Monarch grammars and the high contrast themes.
- **Zone and widget API leftovers (#296):** the rest of Monaco's `IViewZone`, `IContentWidget` and `IViewZoneChangeAccessor` (`viewZones.ts`, `contentWidgets.ts`, `codeEditorWidget.ts`'s `changeViewZones`), and the sash hover delay.
  - **Zone callbacks:** `ViewZone::on_dom_node_top` and `on_computed_height` (Monaco `onDomNodeTop`, `onComputedHeight`) are told the zone's top, from the editor's top with scrolling (Monaco's -1000000 less the scroll top when out of view or hidden), and its height (0 while hidden). They run after the frame that lays the zone out, and only when the value changed; Monaco calls them on every render. Editors where no zone has them do no extra work per frame.
  - **Content widget render hooks:** `ContentWidget::before_render` (Monaco `beforeRender`) returns an optional size, used instead of the view's measured size to place the widget and lay its view out. `after_render` (Monaco `afterRender`) is told the preference the widget was placed by, or `None` when it isn't shown, after the frame and when that changes.
  - **`useDisplayNone`:** `ContentWidget::use_display_none` places the widget (and tells `after_render`) without ever laying out or painting its view. Monaco has it on `IContentWidget`, not `IViewZone`.
  - **Batched zone changes:** `change_view_zones(|zones| …, cx)` (Monaco `changeViewZones`) hands a `ViewZoneChangeAccessor` with `add_zone`, `remove_zone`, `layout_zone` and `set_zone_height`; together they emit one `ViewZonesChanged`, or none when nothing changed. It returns the closure's result. `add_view_zone`, `layout_view_zone` and `set_view_zone_height` stay, one change each.
  - **Sash hover delay:** the peek view's height and split sashes and the diff editor's sash show `sash.hoverBorder` once the mouse has rested on them for Monaco's 300ms `hoverDelay`, instead of at once, and hide as soon as it leaves. Nothing repaints while waiting: one timer ends the delay. The diff editor's sash still shows it at once while dragged, now in `sash.hoverBorder` rather than `focusBorder` (the same color in the built-in themes).
  - **Zones after a fold:** a zone hides (and shows again) in the frame a fold or `set_hidden_areas` changes, instead of keeping its old place until the text or the zones changed: the zone rows cache is keyed by the view lines' generation rather than their address, which stays the same when they change in place.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the zone callbacks (scrolling, resizing, hiding, out of view), `beforeRender`/`afterRender`, `useDisplayNone`, batched changes and their single event, and the peek and diff sashes' hover delay.
  - **Breaking:** `ViewZone` has new public fields, `on_dom_node_top` and `on_computed_height`, and `ContentWidget` has `use_display_none`, `before_render` and `after_render`; struct literals need `..ViewZone::new(…)` or `..ContentWidget::new(…)`.
- **Small parity gaps (#302):** the last small behaviors from the book's differences page.
  - **Word distance:** `suggest.locality_bonus` already ranked every suggestion, word-based ones included, by Monaco's `WordDistance` (as `suggestModel.ts` passes it to the completion model); the differences page said otherwise. Its code moved to `suggest::word_distance`, with Monaco's `wordDistance.test.ts` ported.
  - **`$SELECTION` from overtyped text:** typing over selections remembers their text (Monaco's `OvertypingCapturer`, locked while a suggest session is open, up to 51 200 characters), and a suggestion inserted next gets it for `$SELECTION` and `$TM_SELECTED_TEXT` when nothing is selected, multi-line text re-indented as for a selection.
  - **Horizontal smooth scrolling:** with `smooth_scrolling`, horizontal scrolls (reveals, cursor moves, mouse wheels, `set_scroll_left` with `ScrollType::Smooth`) animate over 125ms as vertical ones do, both axes from the same request (Monaco's `SmoothScrollingOperation` animates `scrollLeft` and `scrollTop`).
  - **"Show more" chip:** the chip at `stop_rendering_line_after` gets Monaco's `.mtkoverflow` box: 4px padding inside its 1px border, which the line is shaped around, and a background the text's height plus that padding, centered on the line (it overflows the line as in Monaco). Its hover and click area is that box.
  - **Rounded inline code:** inline code in the suggest details and parameter hints is drawn on a background rounded by 3px (`.suggest-details code`, `.parameter-hints-widget .docs code`), following the text when it wraps. Monaco's hover CSS sets only the background, so hovers and messages stay square.
  - **Minimap per-line redraw:** after an edit, the minimap's tiles keep the pixels of the lines that didn't change, moved up or down with the lines around them, and draw only the edited lines and those the highlighter recolored, as Monaco's `onLinesChanged`, `onLinesInserted`, `onLinesDeleted` and `onTokensChanged` do for its canvas. Before, every visible tile was drawn again after each edit and again after each parse. Tiles where most lines changed, or minimaps whose lines can't be drawn apart, are still drawn whole. Typing and scrolling a 100k-line Rust file perform the same as before (6 interleaved release runs each).
  - **Tests:** the ported `wordDistance` test and unit tests for the word distance, both scroll axes animating from one request and wrapped code backgrounds; GPUI tests for locality-ranked word-based suggestions, `$SELECTION` after typing over single- and multi-line selections, horizontal smooth scrolling, the chip's padding and box, rounded inline code rendering, and minimap tiles after typing, inserted and deleted lines and recoloring, compared with tiles drawn from scratch.
  - **Breaking:** `MarkdownStyle` has a new public field, `code_corner_radius`; add `code_corner_radius: px(0.)` (square, as before) to struct literals.
- **Sticky scroll and gutter leftovers (#295):** the last of #254's sticky scroll and gutter behavior, from Monaco's `StickyScrollController`, `StickyScrollWidget`, `foldingDecorations.ts`, `GlyphMarginWidgets` and `MouseHandler`.
  - **Sticky chevrons fade:** with `show_folding_controls: Mouseover`, the sticky lines' expanded folding chevrons fade in over 0.5s while the mouse is over the sticky line numbers and fade out after, as the gutter's do. Before, they appeared and vanished at once.
  - **Sticky line clicks reveal:** a click on a sticky line (and Enter on a focused one) puts the cursor there and reveals it with Monaco's plain `revealPosition`, so the line scrolls into view with the usual context lines above it. Before, the line was scrolled to where it showed in the sticky widget.
  - **Manual folding icons:** manual folding ranges (Create Folding Range from Selection) draw Monaco's `folding-manual-collapsed` and `folding-manual-expanded` icons. They default to the same chevrons as the other ranges; the new `view::folding::FoldingIcon` and `set_folding_icon` change any of the four folding icons, as a product icon theme does in VS Code.
  - **Ctrl+click on macOS:** opens the context menu, as Monaco's does, instead of moving the cursor and starting a selection. It never adds a cursor or follows a definition link, whatever `multi_cursor_modifier` is; Ctrl+click on a folding control still folds.
  - **Glyph margin widget ranges:** `GlyphMarginWidget` takes a `range` (Monaco `IGlyphMarginWidgetPosition.range`) with its lane and z-index. The widget shows once, beside the range's first line or the first line in view while the range starts above the viewport, and takes its lane on every line of the range. `GlyphMarginWidget::with_range(range, view)` makes one, and `CodeEditor::layout_glyph_margin_widget(id, GlyphMarginWidgetPosition { range, lane, z_index }, cx)` moves one (Monaco `layoutGlyphMarginWidget`). `GlyphMarginWidget::new(row, view)` still makes one beside a line.
  - **Playground:** the demo widgets (`PLAYGROUND_DEMO=1`) include a glyph margin widget for lines 21 to 30.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the sticky chevrons' fade, a sticky click's reveal, the manual folding icons and `set_folding_icon`, glyph margin widget ranges (first line, first visible line, lanes across the range, `layout_glyph_margin_widget`) and Ctrl+click with both `multi_cursor_modifier`s.
  - **Breaking:** `GlyphMarginWidget`'s `row` field is now `range: Range<PointUtf16>` (`GlyphMarginWidget::new(row, view)` is unchanged). On macOS, Ctrl+click no longer moves the cursor or selects.
- **Hover: Quick Fix menu position and part focus (#294):** the problem hover's "Quick Fix..." opens the code action menu below the clicked link, as Monaco's `showCodeActions` with the link's box does, instead of at the marker's start. The hover's rows (each part, then the status bar) take the focus as Monaco's `hover-row`s do.
  - **Code action menu anchor:** the menu takes a window box as well as a text position (Monaco's `IAnchor | IPosition`), and goes below it, or above it without room. "Show Disabled" keeps the anchor.
  - **Part focus:** Tab and Shift+Tab walk the focused hover from the card into its rows and back, then out to the editor; a click on a row focuses it. A focused row is scrolled into view, has the focus border and shows its copy button. Show or Focus Hover focuses a lone row itself (Monaco `ContentHoverWidgetWrapper.focus`). The verbosity actions use the focused row (Monaco `focusedHoverPartIndex`). As in Monaco, the arrow keys still scroll the hover; the vendored source has no "Focus Next/Previous Hover Part" actions. Tab in a focused hover no longer indents the text.
  - **Screen readers:** each row has its accessible content as its label and is read when it takes the focus: the part's content, or the status bar's description (Monaco `getAccessibleHoverContentAtIndex`). The whole hover's accessible content now includes the color picker, inline suggestion toolbar and inlay hint command rows.
  - **API:** `CodeEditor::focus_hover_part`, `focused_hover_part_index`, `hover_part_count` and `hover_part_accessible_content` (Monaco `focusHoverPartWithIndex`, `focusedHoverPartIndex`, `hoverPartsCount`, `getAccessibleWidgetContentAtIndex`). `update_hover_verbosity_level`'s index is a row of the hover, as Monaco's is.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for the menu below the clicked link (and at the cursor from the keyboard), Tab and Shift+Tab over the rows with their announcements, a lone row taking the focus, and a click focusing a row.
- **Commands leftovers (#300):** the rest of Monaco's command behavior from #261.
  - **`DiffEditor::add_command`:** Monaco's `IStandaloneDiffEditor.addCommand(keybinding, handler, context)`, with per-editor keybindings as `CodeEditor::add_command` has: `add_command(keystrokes, handler, when, cx)` binds in that diff editor, with either side focused, while `when` holds in the focused side's context keys, and returns the command's id (which `DiffEditor::trigger` runs) and a `Disposable`. It used to register a global command with no keybinding.
  - **Widget preconditions:** the hover, rename input, peek list, standalone color picker, find widget and its inputs put their editor's context keys on their own key contexts (without `CodeEditor`), and the default bindings there get their action's precondition folded in, as Monaco does: Up in a focused hover needs `editorHoverFocused`, Enter in the rename input `renameInputVisible`, Escape in the color picker `standaloneColorPickerVisible`, so a failing one lets the key fall through. Keybinding rules can test the editor's keys there (`HoverWidget && editorLangId == rust`).
  - **`viewPosition`:** `_moveTo`, `_moveToSelect`, `createCursor`, `_lastCursorMoveToSelect`, `_lineSelect(Drag)` and `lastCursorLineSelect(Drag)` go through view positions, as Monaco's `CursorMoveCommands.moveTo` and `line` do: a `viewPosition` that shows `position` (Monaco `validateViewPosition`) keeps the cursor on its segment of a wrapped line, extending a line selection goes by view lines, and a position inside a fold lands where the fold shows. `columnSelect` with a `viewPosition` selects on view lines. `_wordSelect` takes model positions, as in Monaco.
  - **`pasteAs` preferences:** every entry of `preferences` counts, not just the first: the first edit whose kind any of them contains is applied (Monaco filters the edits by the preferences and takes the first, in the providers' order), and the "No paste edits" message names them all (or "empty"). With `kind` or `preferences` the matching edit is now applied without the quick pick, also when there are several. `CodeEditor::paste_as_preferring` is the API for it.
  - **`inlineSuggest.trigger`:** `providerId` asks only the provider it names, through the new `InlineCompletionsProvider::provider_id` (Monaco `providerId`, matched as the id or `extension:*`), or every provider when none matches. `showNoResultNotification` logs "No inline suggestion is available." (at info level) when nothing shows, as standalone Monaco's notification service logs it to the console. `CodeEditor::trigger_inline_suggest_with_args` takes them from code.
  - **Tests:** GPUI tests for `DiffEditor::add_command` (both sides, `when` on the focused side, `trigger`, disposal), the folded widget preconditions and a rule seeing the editor's keys in a focused hover, the mouse commands at a wrap point, line selections by view lines and into folds, `columnSelect` on view lines, `pasteAs` with several preferences and `kind`, and `inlineSuggest.trigger` with `providerId` and `showNoResultNotification`.
  - **Breaking:**
    - `DiffEditor::add_command` takes `(keystrokes, handler, when, cx)`, with a handler getting the `DiffEditor`, and returns `anyhow::Result<(SharedString, Disposable)>`; for a global command by id, use `gpui_monaco::add_command`.
    - `editor.action.pasteAs` with a `kind` applies the first matching edit even when several match, instead of asking.
    - `CreateCursor` has a new `view_position` field.
- **Web runner (#303):** `examples/web` runs the editor in a browser through gpui's web platform, with the playground's sample.
  - **Runner:** opens `examples/playground/samples/sample.rs` instead of a four-line snippet. `build.sh` takes `--font <path>` (copied to `www/fonts/editor.ttf`, since gpui's web platform ships no fonts), checks the `wasm-bindgen` CLI matches `Cargo.lock`, and prints how to serve `www/`.
  - **CI:** a `build-web` job (best-effort, as it needs nightly) installs nightly with the wasm target and `llvm-tools` and the matching `wasm-bindgen` CLI, runs `build.sh --release` and uploads `www/` as the `web` artifact.
  - **Docs:** the guide's new "Running in a browser" chapter, the README, `examples/web/README.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md` say how to try it; the parity doc's wasm row is ✅ with its limits (nightly, a font from the page, no language servers).
- **Inline completions leftovers (#297):** the rest of Monaco's `InlineCompletionsModel`, `SuggestWidgetAdaptor`, `GhostTextView` and inline edit view transitions.
  - **Suggest preselection:** when the suggest widget opens or refilters while an inline completion is selected, it selects the suggestion the completion extends, the one inserting the most of several, before the selection memory gets a say (Monaco's `registerSelector` with priority 100 in `SuggestWidgetAdaptor`).
  - **`enableForwardStability`:** `InlineCompletionList::enable_forward_stability` keeps the shown item first through the requests typing makes, as long as typing agrees with it (Monaco `itemToPreserve` with `forwardStable`). An explicit request (Alt+\, cycling) keeps it first too, as Monaco's `preserveCurrentCompletion` does.
  - **`disposeInlineCompletions`:** `InlineCompletionsProvider::dispose_inline_completions(list, reason)` runs once per list when none of its items is used any more (Monaco's ref-counted `InlineSuggestionList`), with `InlineCompletionsDisposeReason` `Other` (its items were used), `Empty`, `NotTaken` or `TokenCancellation` (the request was superseded after the list came back). `LostRace` is there for parity; the editor waits for every provider, so it doesn't send it.
  - **Secondary cursors:** the completion shows and applies at every other cursor where the text around it matches the primary's (Monaco `getSecondaryEdits`), for the suggest widget's preview too. Accepting puts a cursor after each edit, and word or line accepts apply at each cursor.
  - **Multi-line ghost text mid-line:** the rest of the line moves to the end of the ghost text's last line, hidden in the line itself (Monaco `computeGhostTextViewData`, `ghost-text-hidden`); parts after one that adds lines go to the added lines.
  - **`warning`:** the squiggle covers every line of multi-line ghost text, not the line's text moved below it.
  - **Inline edit transitions:** the gutter indicator eases its colors (as what Tab does changes), its width (as it docks beside the edit or pins to an edge) and its icon's opacity over 200ms `ease-in-out`, and the long-distance hint fades out (and back in) as Monaco's `transition: opacity 200ms` does, instead of going at once. They're GPUI animations that repaint only while they run, and reduced motion skips them.
  - **Tests:** ports of `getSecondaryEdits.test.ts`, the "Forward Stability" ("Typing agrees", "Support forward instability", "Push item to preserve to front") and "Multi Cursor Support" suites of `inlineCompletions.test.ts`, unit tests for `computeGhostTextViewData`, and GPUI tests for list disposal, preselection, ghost text at several cursors, the moved rest of the line, the warning on every line and the transitions (including that nothing asks for frames once they're done).
  - **Breaking:**
    - `InlineCompletionList` has a new public field, `enable_forward_stability`; use `..Default::default()` in struct literals.
    - Accepting ghost text with several cursors edits at each cursor it fits and drops the cursors it doesn't, as in Monaco.
- **Multi diff editor leftovers (#299):** the rest of Monaco's `multiDiffEditor` folder (`multiDiffEditorOptions.ts`, `compressedVirtualizedScrollView.ts`, `diffEditorItemTemplate.ts`, `multiDiffEditorWidgetImpl.ts`, `style.css`).
  - **Variants:** `MultiDiffEditorVariant` (Monaco `MultiDiffEditorVariant`) with `set_variant`/`variant`: `NoCardsNonCompact` (the default, standalone Monaco's), `NoCards` (`multiDiffEditor-compact`: 32px headers on the list's background, a `panel.border` line between files, full-width diffs with 8px below) and `Cards` (`multiDiffEditor-card`: bordered, rounded cards in `sideBarSectionHeader.background`, `focusBorder` while focused, a square shadowed header when stuck). Both compact variants collapse a file on a header click or Enter/Space on the focused header (`headerClickToCollapse`), show the toolbar only on hover or focus, hide original line numbers, and color the status letters with the git decoration colors. The theme registry gains `panel.border`, `sideBarSectionHeader.background` and `gitDecoration.{added,deleted,modified}ResourceForeground` defaults (workbench colors standalone Monaco doesn't register), and `EditorColors` the `multi_diff_entry_border`, `multi_diff_card_header_background`, `multi_diff_card_border` and `multi_diff_{added,deleted,renamed}_foreground` fields.
  - **Binary files:** `MultiDiffDocument::binary` (Monaco `isBinary`) shows the "Binary file changed" placeholder (100px, `binaryFilePlaceholderContentHeight`) instead of a diff editor. `set_open_binary_diff_handler` adds Monaco's "Open Diff" button (`IWorkbenchUIElementFactory.openDiffEditor`) for files with both sides. Navigation focuses the placeholder and goes past it.
  - **Smooth wheel scrolling:** line deltas (a mouse wheel) scroll the list smoothly over Monaco's 100ms from where it's going, pixel deltas (a trackpad) at once, as `SmoothScrollableElement` does. `has_pending_scroll_animation`.
  - **Compressed layout:** Monaco `CompressedVirtualizedScrollView`'s anchored geometry edits: when files change height, the point at the viewport's top (its bottom while scrolling up) stays put, with leading or trailing scroll slack when needed, used up by scrolling towards it. `scroll_height` includes it.
  - **View state:** `save_view_state`/`restore_view_state` (Monaco `getViewState`/`setViewState`): `MultiDiffEditorViewState` with the scroll position, each file's `MultiDiffDocState` (collapsed, selections) by `MultiDiffDocumentKey`, and the active file. A restored scroll position waits until the list is tall enough (`_applyPendingScrollState`).
  - **Keyboard cursor moves:** moving the cursor with the keyboard past what shows of a file scrolls the list along, keeping it in view.
  - **Reveal highlight:** `reveal_range` highlights the range with `editor.symbolHighlightBackground` for 350ms (Monaco `highlightRange`).
  - **Keybindings:** standalone Monaco binds no keys to next/previous change or file in the multi diff editor (VS Code's workbench does), so none are added; documented in the book.
  - **Not ported:** the `cards` variant's compact hidden-lines controls (the diff editor's `compact` `DiffEditorVariant`) and the per-item scroll anchors while revealing hidden lines (`runWithOriginalEditorScrollAnchor`/`runWithModifiedEditorScrollAnchor`).
